Where the words of a page are invisible text (text rendering mode 3, which `rulingLines.ts` follows through the page's content and marks on each word) standing over a picture, the page is a scan with its recognised text: the words are typed like any others and that picture is left out (`PageLayout.scan`), so nothing is printed twice. Only invisible text counts as a scan layer; a layer hidden another way still prints double. A picture that no invisible word stands over is kept, and a page of visible text is read as before. Each result says so (below) and the record counts such pages. What recognition did not read is not on the page: the lead's ruling, open to the owner's change (11b).
- **In the Word file** (`wordBuild.ts`): a section for each page with its size and margins; tables to the twip with merged cells, a border on each side, shading and turned text; pictures; rules and filled boxes as shapes anchored to the page; text that stands at its own spot (labels beside placed boxes, the foot of a page, turned labels) as a box of text anchored to the page.
- **In the Excel file** (`excelBuild.ts`): merged ranges (a heading over several columns, a label over several rows), column widths, row heights, fonts, borders, alignment, shading and turned text; pictures placed beside the rows they stand beside, made smaller in proportion where the sheet's rows are shorter than the page's; a picture that would lie over text there stands in empty rows of its own just above that text, in its size (T-068: the sheet writes a text at its column's left edge, so a logo the page shows left of a name would otherwise cover the start of the name); a sheet row holds at most three lines of the page and never two amounts in one cell; the foot of a page stands after an empty row on a sheet that holds one page. A superscript is plain text in a cell.
- **What it does not do** is in 11b. The scores that measure it are in section 9: a measure, not a promise.

#### PDF to Excel

The dialog: "Put the tables of this PDF into an Excel workbook. Amounts become numbers you can add up."; the preview; the choice "Sheets"; the result name ("Will be saved as `<name>.xlsx`"); the main button "Make Excel workbook". Blocked, besides the scanned case: "This file has more than 1,000 pages, too many for one workbook. Split it into parts, then convert each part."

**Preview** (contract decision 10). A plain grid of one page as its sheet will hold it, from the same plan as the run: at most 30 rows by 12 columns, numbers right-aligned and shown as Excel will show them, with Previous and Next page and "Page N of M". At most 300 px or 28% of the window high, so that the Sheets choice under it stays in view at 1280 × 720. Under the grid, one of: "Table found from the lines drawn on the page." / "Table found from how the text lines up. It may need tidying in Excel." / "No table found on this page. Its text goes in column A, one line per row." / "This page has no text. It is left empty."; and, only for what is cut, "Showing the first 30 of 45 rows and 12 of 15 columns." While it reads: "Reading the file for the preview…", then "Looking for text: page N of M…". If it fails: "The preview could not be read from this file. You can still make the workbook."

**Sheets** (contract decision 11; **lead ruling** on the default, 17 November 2026): "All pages on one sheet" (the default; "Best for statements and ledgers that run over several pages."), a sheet named `All pages`; or "One sheet per page" ("Each page on its own sheet: Page_1, Page_2 and so on."). Sheet names are made valid for Excel (no `[]:*?/\`, at most 31 characters, no apostrophe at either end, not `History`, unique ignoring case).

**What goes in.** Blocks in reading order: a table's rows as rows of cells; a line of text in column A, one line per row; a line of text is never a number. Nothing is dropped: repeated heading rows, page headers and footers are kept. Empty cells at the end of a row are trimmed. So the fallbacks of section 13 hold page by page: a ruled table, else an aligned grid, else the page's text in column A from A1. A table drawn with lines keeps its merged cells (a heading over several columns, a label over several rows), and a table read from how its text lines up gets merged headings over its columns; a narration's second line in a table without lines is a row of its own (11b). Columns are sized from the table cells (characters × 1.1 + 2, from 6 to 60), not from the long text lines above a table; a sheet of text only sizes column A up to 100. The workbook names "ICAI VERA" as its creator and has no title.

**A table that runs over several pages** (**lead rulings**, 17 November 2026, two rounds). The contract's first rule (carry the columns on when the count matches and the starts are within 6 pt) did not hold: a right-aligned column's left edge is where its longest amount starts, so it moves from page to page. As built (`excelBuild.ts`):

- Every table that starts a new table becomes a reference: its column edges, which columns hold amounts, and its heading row. References are kept newest first, at most 8, and shared by all sheets, so "One sheet per page" gives a statement the same columns on every sheet. A table that carries on never moves a reference.
- A table found from how its text lines up is always tried against each reference, whatever its own column count: first its lines are placed again in the reference's columns (a right-aligned column matched by its right edge); if that is not consistent, its words are placed run by run (an amount, with a currency word just before it or Dr or Cr just after, goes to the column its right edge ends in; any other run of words, cut at a gap wider than 0.6 of the font size, to the column its left edge starts in). The first consistent result carries the table on, with no blank row.
- **Consistent** means all of: no word is lost and the result has exactly the reference's columns; an amount cell stands only in a column that holds amounts in the reference, and its right edge ends inside that column (no more than 6 pt past the next column's left edge); every other word lies inside its column (from 6 pt before its left edge to 6 pt past the next column's); a repeated heading has its texts in the same columns as the reference's.
- A ruled table carries on when its column count equals a reference's (its columns are drawn).
- Otherwise the table is a new one, from its own columns: on one sheet after one blank row, and it becomes the newest reference.

This fixed a fault found by the Excel lane's own test: on a page whose long balances crowd the Credit column, the page alone shows five columns instead of six and the balances landed in the Credit column; placed against the first page's columns they stay in Balance. A genuinely different table on a later page (for example Item, Quantity, Value under a statement) is refused as a carry-on, because its quantities would land in a column that holds no amounts.

**Numbers** (contract decision 12 and its number rules; **lead ruling**, 17 November 2026: grouped or decimal amounts of any size are numbers). A cell becomes a number only when the whole cell parses (`excelNumbers.ts`). A number may have:

- one currency mark before it (`₹`, `Rs`, `Rs.`, `INR`, in any case), before or after a leading minus or an opening bracket;
- one negative mark: a leading `-` (or the minus sign U+2212), a trailing `-`, or brackets around it;
- digits ungrouped, or grouped validly as Indian (`1,23,456.78`) or western (`123,456.78`), up to 15 digits;
- a trailing `Dr` or `Cr` (any case), only when it has no negative mark.

A space is dropped only next to a comma, a point, a bracket, a minus or `₹`, and between a currency word, the number and Dr or Cr (`1,23, 456.78` and `( 500.00 )` are numbers). These stay text, written as text cells with Excel's Text format so that Excel never turns them into something else even when edited: dates in any form; leading zeros; more than 15 digits; `%`; a lone `-`; `1.234,56`; digits apart by a plain space (`12 345`); an en dash; `+500`; a currency mark after the number; two marks; `-500 Dr`; `.50` and `500.`; and a bare whole number of more than 8 digits with no grouping, point, sign, mark or suffix, which reads as an account, mobile or customer number (`12345678` is a number, `123456789` is text) (**lane's call**).

The cell's format keeps what was shown: the decimals; Indian grouping, written as literal commas placed for that cell's own number of digits (`##","##","##0.00`; below a lakh the two groupings read the same and the western format `#,##0.00` is used); a negative as `-X`, `(X)` or `X-` as shown, and a zero shown with a mark keeps it; Dr or Cr as literal text after a positive value, always as a space and `Dr` or `Cr`. **The value is never negated for Dr or Cr**, because whether Dr means plus or minus depends on the account; the currency mark is not kept.

**Running.** Progress: "Opening the file…", "Reading page N of M…", "Placing the tables in rows and columns…", "Writing the workbook…". The plan is made in turns, on the window's thread; the workbook itself is written in a background worker (`convert/excelWrite.worker.ts`), because the spreadsheet library holds its thread for seconds on a long statement (0.8–3.4 s for 500 pages, measured by the lane). The worker says it is alive every second; if it is silent for 5 minutes the write is given up; Stop ends it at once. Where no worker can start, or it fails without replying, the workbook is written on the window's thread instead, which is correct but holds the window. More than 1,048,576 rows on one sheet is refused before the workbook is built. Limits: 1,000 pages a file; 80 cells a row (what lies beyond is joined, as text, into the 80th); 32,767 characters a cell (the rest is cut).

**Result.** The message "Made `<name>.xlsx`" with the detail "3 pages, 1 sheet, 142 rows; 2 pages had no table and were written as text." and, when they apply, the little-text sentence above and the scan sentence "N pages are scans: their text was typed from recognition and the scan pictures were left out. Check numbers against the page." (for one page: "1 page is a scan: its text was typed from recognition and the scan picture was left out. Check numbers against the page."), which makes the message a warning. Failures, each with nothing added: "The text of this file could not be read, and nothing was added. Open the file in a PDF reader, save a fresh copy and add that." / "The workbook could not be written, and nothing was added. Try again; if it fails again, restart the application." / "Writing the workbook stopped answering, and nothing was added. Try again; if it fails again, restart the application." / "This file gives more rows than one Excel sheet can hold (1,048,576). Choose "One sheet per page" and try again. Nothing was added." Record: section 6.

#### PDF to Word

The dialog (as `PdfToWordDialog.tsx` and `convert/wordForm.ts` have it): "A Word document that keeps the look of the pages: text, tables and pictures in their places, ready to edit. Fonts Word does not have are replaced by similar ones." (the first build said "An editable text version: paragraphs, headings and simple tables in reading order. It is not a copy of the page layout; pictures are left out."; the owner rejected that design on 19 November 2026); the result name ("Will be saved as `<name>.docx`"); the main button "Make Word document". No options (contract decision 13). Blocked, besides the scanned case: "This file has more than 1,000 pages, too many to turn into a Word document. Split it into parts first."

**Paragraphs** (within one text block of the page; each block starts a new paragraph; `wordBuild.ts`). These rules, from the first build, still decide where a paragraph starts; how each paragraph then looks is set from the page (next paragraph). A new paragraph starts:

1. at a change of size (more than 10%) or of heading level;
2. at a clear gap between lines: a spacing over 1.35 times the block's usual spacing plus 0.5 pt (in a block with no usual spacing, a gap over 0.75 of the font size);
3. at an indent: a line that starts 0.8 em or more away from where most lines of its size start, after a line that starts there (a first-line indent, or the next item of a hanging indent; continuation lines stay joined);
4. after a short line, when the next line's first word would have fitted on it, plus 0.5 em (so each line of an address is its own paragraph); not in a column with another block beside it.

A hyphen at a line end joins the word: it is dropped before a small letter ("finan-" + "cial" gives "financial") and kept otherwise ("2024-" + "25" gives "2024-25"); a lone "-" does not join. A hyphenated line overrides rules 3 and 4, not 1 and 2. Words are joined by single spaces in layout order; control characters are removed; Hindi and other scripts pass through.

**How a paragraph looks** (`wordLook.ts`, measured from the page): a line whose words stand apart by a wide gap, or that has a blank drawn as an underline, stays a paragraph of its own with its pieces at tab stops (an underlined tab draws the blank); other lines of one paragraph flow with the page's own line pitch; a paragraph is centred or set right when it sits so in its box (the cell, or the page's text area), and justified when its lines all end at one edge, but not set right when it is numbered or its lines share their start; a numbered or marked first line, with the lines under its text, is one paragraph with a hanging indent (mark, tab, text); a run is drawn as wide as on the page, in the font size to the half point and a character scale for the rest. Text that reads as an unruled table but is running text becomes paragraphs.

**Headings.** The body size is the size most characters on the page use, table words included (on a tie, the smaller). A line at 1.5 times the body size or more is Heading 1; at 1.2 times or more, Heading 2.

**Tables.** A ruled table is always a table. An aligned table becomes a table only when it has 3 column starts or more, each used by 3 rows or more (**lead ruling**, 17 November 2026: as built); otherwise each of its rows is a paragraph with its cells apart by a tab, so a two-column list of labels and amounts stays text. Rows are padded to the column count; an empty table is dropped. A table is written with the true cells, merges, borders and shading described above. A run that the page draws plain, in Word's heading style, says "not bold" so that the style does not make it bold and wider than on the page.

**Pages.** Each page is a section of its own, with its size and margins (the first build wrote one page break between pages). A page with no word at all is an empty page; a page with little text keeps its words as one paragraph (above).

**The document.** It follows the pages: the fonts, sizes, bold, italic, underline and colours of the page (a font Word does not have replaced as above; Calibri only for a run that names none), tables with their merged cells, borders and shading, pictures, rules and boxes, and text at its own spot; no title or author. Headings are Word's heading styles. The document is written by the `docx` library around a marker, and its body by PDF Tools itself, part by part and in turns (section 8).

**Running and result.** Progress: "Reading page n of N…", "Putting the paragraphs and tables in order…", "Writing the Word document…". The message "Made `<name>.docx`" with the detail "12 pages: 48 paragraphs, 6 headings, 3 tables." (zero counts left out) and, when they apply, the little-text sentence above and the scan sentence "N pages are scans: their text was typed from recognition and the scan pictures were left out. Check numbers against the page." (for one page: "1 page is a scan: its text was typed from recognition and the scan picture was left out. Check numbers against the page."), which makes the message a warning. A refusal at Run ends with " Nothing was added." Record: section 6.

### 5.12 Make searchable, OCR (phase 3)

Built in phase 3 (`BUILT = true` in `ocr/ocrRun.ts`). It takes exactly one PDF ("Select exactly one PDF to make searchable."; for an image, "Select a PDF, not an image, to make searchable."). The owner decided on 17 November 2026 that Make searchable comes last in phase 3 and reads English only, and on 18 November 2026, after the engine trial, said "go ahead" on these terms: the result says honestly what to check, each page has a time limit, no single confidence percentage is shown, the engine's packages are added as development dependencies, and the packaging setting is not changed (**owner decisions**).

Turns a scanned file into one whose text can be searched, selected and used by Find, Redact and the Convert tools. The pages look the same; recognised text is added invisibly over them. Recognition runs on this computer, with the English data shipped in the application. Default name `<name>_searchable.pdf` (contract decision 16). Producer is set to "ICAI VERA" and the source's Creator is kept.

**Which pages are recognised** (`ocrPlan.ts`; **lead ruling**, 18 November 2026): a page with fewer than 20 characters of text is recognised whenever anything but text is drawn on it: a picture or a form of any size, an inline picture, a shading, a stroked or filled path (so letters drawn as shapes are read too); a page whose content cannot be decoded counts as drawn. A page with 20 characters or more already has text and is copied as it is. A page with little text and nothing else drawn (only "Page 7") is left as it is and counted as blank. A page whose text cannot be read is left as it is too (recognising it could put its text in twice). So every page that PDF to Excel and PDF to Word count as having little or no text, and that holds anything to read, is one this tool takes; Document check counts its "pages that look scanned" with the same functions, so the two tools never disagree (5.13). A scan that carries a few characters of real text (a printed "Page 7") is recognised and keeps that text as well. "Recognise every page" is not offered on screen. Each page to recognise is drawn whole by the application's renderer, as shown (rotation and crop box applied), on white, at 300 dpi (lower only for a page too large for the canvas limits).

**The dialog.** "Adds the text of scanned pages, read on this computer, so the file can be searched, selected and redacted. The pages look the same. English text only." While the pages are looked at ("Looking for pages without text: page N of M…"), and then one line says what will happen: "8 of 12 pages have little or no text and will be recognised, about 5 to 20 seconds a page (about 1 to 3 minutes in all)." ("All 30 pages have …", "The page has little or no text and will be recognised, in about 5 to 20 seconds.", "1 of 6 pages has …"; the range is a **lead ruling** of 18 November 2026). With nothing to recognise, Run is blocked with one of "Every page already has text; nothing to recognise.", "Every page already has text or is blank; nothing to recognise." and "Every page is blank; nothing to recognise." The signed-file notice of 5.1; the result name ("Will be saved as `<name>_searchable.pdf`"); one main button, "Make searchable". Progress: "Getting the text reader ready…", "Reading the text of page P (n of N)…", from the second page on with what is left at the pace measured so far ("Reading the text of page 7 (3 of 8), about 40 seconds left…"), and "Adding the text to the file…".

**The invisible text** (`ocrLayer.ts`). Each recognised word is written in Helvetica in the invisible rendering mode, sized from the text size measured from the line's row (its box only when none was measured), stretched across its box, on the line's baseline, with a space after each word that is followed by a word of its line. When the engine straightened a skewed page, the word boxes come back in the straightened picture, so they are turned back by the engine's angle about the picture's centre (without this a page skewed 2° put the words a median 9.1 pt from the ink). The page's own content is kept byte for byte, wrapped in a save and restore of the graphics state, and the layer adds only text operators in its own pair; one Helvetica is embedded for the whole file. Words made only of ruling marks (`|`, `¦`, `_`, box-drawing characters, two or more em dashes) are left out, because each ruled column line of a ledger comes back as a `|`; a single em dash is kept, because statements use it for "nil". Characters the standard font cannot encode (₹, ligatures, Devanagari) are left out and counted. In the tests every word comes back with pdf.js in order, within 2 pt of its ink (under 0.1 pt on upright, turned, cropped and skewed test pages). In the application, pages drawn at 100 dpi before and after differed in no value, and the file grew by about 19 bytes a word.

**The result, in honest words** (`ocr/ocrForm.ts`, `ocrQuality.ts`). The message "Made searchable: `<name>`", with a detail built from these sentences, in order, each only when it applies:

- "8 of 12 pages recognised; 4 already had text; 1 had nothing to recognise." (or "All 12 pages recognised." / "The page was recognised.");
- "Page 7 took too long and was left as it was." / "Pages 3 and 4 took too long and were left as they were.";
- "Page 5 could not be read and was left as it was.";
- "No words were found on pages 2 and 4; they may be blank or too faint.";
- "Little could be read on page 6: it may be faint, handwritten or not in English." (for several pages: "Little could be read on pages 1 and 3: they may be faint, handwritten or not in English."; for a page that is partly read, as below, and of which more was left out than kept or of which the words kept come to under one a square inch of the page, `LITTLE_READ_WORDS_PER_SQUARE_INCH`, under 97 words on A4; such a page is not also named in the next sentence, and when every page that was read is of this kind the "pages recognised" sentence is left out, so that no page is called recognised);
- "Parts of page 6 could not be read and were left out: it may be faint, handwritten or not in English." (for several pages: "Parts of pages 1 and 3 could not be read and were left out: they may be faint, handwritten or not in English."; only for a page of which a tenth or more of what the engine saw was left out; no count and no percentage);
- always: "Recognised text can be wrong: check numbers against the page.";
- "37 numbers to check on pages 2, 3, 5, 8 and 9; pages 3 and 9 look faint." (pages listed up to five, then counted: "on 14 pages");
- "3 symbols could not be added to the searchable text and were left out."

A **number to check** is a recognised word that contains a digit and has an engine confidence below 80. A page **looks faint** when it has at least one word and either 10% or more of its words have a confidence below 80, or it has fewer than 0.1 words a square inch (under 10 words on A4). The message is a warning when any page timed out, failed, gave no words or looks faint, or when any number needs checking or any symbol was left out; otherwise a success. **No single confidence percentage is shown** (**owner decision**, 18 November 2026): the trial found the engine's own figure unreliable. When no word could be read on any recognised page, nothing is added: "No words could be read on the pages without text, and nothing was added. They may be blank, too faint or not in English." When the engine cannot start (it has 30 s): "The text reader could not start, and nothing was added. Restart the application and try again."

**What is left out, and how the text is sized** (rework of 20 November 2026, after the owner's trial; `ocrReadable.ts`, `ocrLayer.ts`, `ocrQuality.ts`, `ocrTypes.ts`, `ocr/ocrEngine.ts`, `ocr/ocrForm.ts`, `ocr/ocrRun.ts`, `activity.ts`). The owner tried a real two-language scan and found the result not up to the mark. The causes, by kind: about half of the text layer was nonsense, made by the English-only engine from print in another script, from logos, stamps and signatures; the text was sized from the engine's line box, which a neighbouring line's marks swell, so a tenth of the words were far too tall (selection bars across two lines); words were joined without a space, and where two overlapping lines were read together a text extraction tool wove them letter by letter; and the message called pages "faint" that were not, and counted digits inside nonsense as "numbers to check". What changed, all general, with no word list and no rule for a kind of document, and no new option in the dialog: (1) the words the engine itself doubts are left out (`readableWords`): a word with a confidence under 50 (`UNSURE_CONFIDENCE`); in a line where half the words or more are under 50, the words under 80 (`LOW_CONFIDENCE`) as well; a number (a word with a digit) is trusted further, kept whenever the line is readable and, in an unreadable line, from 50; a line is judged on its words with a letter or a digit, so specks do not decide it; (2) the text size is measured: the smaller of the engine's row height divided by 0.96 and the confident words' median height above the baseline divided by 0.75 (`lineTextSize`), and a word the line's baseline misses stands on its own bottom; (3) a space follows each word of a line; (4) a page of which a tenth or more of what the engine saw was left out is "partly read" and is then not also called faint or without words, and the message says so in the sentence above; (5) the record holds eleven counts: `pages`, `pagesRecognised`, `pagesSkipped`, `pagesTimedOut`, `words`, `lowConfidenceWords`, `numbersToCheck`, `pagesFaint`, `charactersDropped`, `wordsLeftOut` and `pagesPartlyRead`: counts only, never a recognised word. The engine and its settings are unchanged. The thresholds and the sentence's wording are developer choices (the lead and the lane), open to the owner.

**A page of which little could be read, and the engine's notes** (20 November 2026, after the independent final pass; `ocrQuality.ts`, `ocr/ocrForm.ts`, `ocr/ocrEngine.ts`). On an invented faint photocopy the layer held 13 of its 353 real words, and the message said "The page was recognised. Parts of page 1 could not be read …", which understated it: nearly all of the page was left out. A page is now "little read" when it is partly read (a tenth or more left out) and either more was left out than kept or the words kept come to under one a square inch of the page; the sentence is quoted above. The measure was taken from 46 measured pages: pages read well kept 1.9 words a square inch or more (181 to 513 words on A4), and the faint photocopies that were mostly lost kept 46 to 93 words. It also names a sparse page with a stamp and a page half in another language (11b). Separately, the engine wrote its own notes on noisy scans to the console as errors; it is now asked not to write them, and nothing reads them. The record is unchanged (eleven counts).

**A page that fails or runs out of time** (the lead's ruling of 18 November 2026, and three choices the final lane made within it, which the lead confirmed the same day). A page gets 60 s (`OCR_PAGE_TIME_LIMIT_MS`); a page that takes longer is left as it was and named ("Page 7 took too long …"), and the engine is started afresh for the next page. A page the engine fails on, or that cannot be drawn, is also left as it was and named ("Page 5 could not be read …"), and the run goes on with a fresh engine. After the engine has failed on three pages in a row it is not asked again in that run: the remaining pages are left as they were and named with the others, because a dead engine would otherwise wait its 30 s start-up limit on every page. Only when every page fails does the run add nothing: "No page could be read, and nothing was added. Try again; if it fails again, restart the application." In the record a failed page is counted under `pagesSkipped`, with the pages that already had text or were blank; the first nine keys are unchanged and two were added on 20 November 2026 (section 6).

**Stop** ends the engine at once and adds nothing ("Stopped. Nothing was added.", 19–26 ms after the Stop was confirmed in the application); the engine's thread ends about 2 s later, and the page's pending answer is never waited for.

**Record:** "Made searchable: 8 of 12 pages recognised"; keys in section 6. Never a recognised word.

**What the engine trial measured** (17–18 November 2026, in the real application from a scratch copy of `main` at `8dc2165`, this two-core machine; invented scans drawn and degraded in the window: a ledger of 34 ruled rows with 70 amounts in Indian grouping, and a letter with 5 amounts; the "best, integer" English data with the settings now required). "Amounts exact" means digits, commas, decimal point and brackets all right.

| Page | Characters right | Amounts exact | Seconds |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Typed letter, 300 dpi | 100% | 5 of 5 | 4.5 |
| Typed letter, 200 dpi | 100% | 5 of 5 | 3.3 |
| Ledger, 300 dpi | 97.1% | 70 of 70 | 7.1 |
| Ledger, 200 dpi | 97.1% | 68 of 70 | 5.2 |
| Ledger skewed 2°, 300 dpi | 97.1% | 70 of 70 | 9.2 |
| Letter skewed 2° | 100% | 5 of 5 | 5.9 |
| Lightly faded photocopy, ledger | 96.1% | 61 of 70 | 7.0 |
| Lightly faded photocopy, letter | 99.9% | 5 of 5 | 4.4 |
| Faint, noisy photocopy, ledger | 14.9% | 22 of 70 | 7.2 |
| Faint, noisy photocopy, letter | 99.9% | 5 of 5 | 4.5 |
| Phone photo at 150 dpi, ledger | 74.9% | 65 of 70 | 5.5 |
| Phone photo at 150 dpi, letter | 99.7% | 2 of 5 | 3.1 |
| **All 12 pages** | | **383 of 450** | |

Typical wrong amounts lost or added a comma, or read a comma as a point. Of 55 wrong amount-shaped words, 9 had a confidence of 80 or more (3 of 90 or more); marking those below 80 caught 46 of the 55 and also marked 20 of the 383 right ones, which is why the message always asks for numbers to be checked. The settings the trial found necessary, all in PDF Tools code: local paths for the worker, engine and data (with none, the library tries a public address); its cache switched off; automatic page layout (the default single block read 0 of 70 amounts on the ruled ledger); the Sauvola threshold (the default read a faint letter at 6% in over a minute); straightening; an error handler and a time limit on start-up, because a failed start otherwise never ends and leaves a worker behind.

**In the application, as built** (the engine lane's scratch copy, plain production build and packaged form, name resolution switched off; then the final pass on the repository's tree). On a six-page invented scan (a ruled ledger, the same ledger 2° askew, a page of real text, a typed letter, a blank scanned page, a lightly faded letter) the ledger read 69 of 70 amounts and the invented code `QZXCV0000W`, the skewed ledger 70 of 70; the text page stayed as it was and the blank page has no text; the viewer's Find found the recognised words; the message named "14 numbers to check on pages 1, 2, 4 and 6" and gave no percentage. In the final pass's own three-page scan the engine read all 6 amounts and the total exactly in every run, and the total became a number cell when the result went through PDF to Excel. Times are in section 9; the faint photocopy pages were not run again in the application.

**What is shipped, and what stays on the machine.** `public/pdf-tools/ocr/`, 8 files and 14,777,799 bytes, committed like the pdf.js files: three engine files (the worker picks one by the processor's features), the engine's worker script and its licence note, the English "best, integer" data (`eng/eng.traineddata.gz`, 2.95 MB), `LICENSE` and `VERSION`, copied there by `scripts/sync-ocr-assets.mjs`, which is run by hand. As the packaging setting stands they are in the installer twice (section 3, candidates). The packages are build-time only (section 8). With its cache switched off the engine writes nothing to disk (no browser database before or after, in every run; the system's temporary folder was not compared), and with local paths it makes no network request: during recognition only the three files are read, from `file://…/pdf-tools/ocr/`, in the plain build and from inside `app.asar` of the packaged form.

Design inputs from the owner's earlier tool (when a page counts as scanned, language and resolution settings, how recognised words are grouped) are recorded in section 13. Redact's finding (5.9) takes words with their page positions, so recognised text can feed it without a change to its rules.

### 5.13 Review: Document check and Compare (phase 3)

The meeting draft has the heading only. The owner decided on 17 November 2026 that Review holds both proposals, Document check and Compare two versions, and that Compare looks at text differences only. Both are built (`BUILT = true` in `review/documentCheckRun.ts` and `review/compareRun.ts`), each a button of its own in the Review group (section 4). Each makes a PDF report, which previews in the viewer and goes to the three destinations like any result (contract decisions 2 and 3). The reports use A4 portrait, 36 pt margins, Helvetica 10 and a bold 14 pt title, with the footer "Made by ICAI VERA PDF Tools on this computer · page n of N"; they set no title, author, subject or keywords, and Producer and Creator are "ICAI VERA". They use the standard fonts only: a word with a character those fonts cannot draw prints as `[word in another script]` and is counted (contract decision 6).

#### Document check

Exactly one PDF ("Select exactly one PDF to check."). No page limit. The dialog: "See what this PDF holds, read on this computer. Add the facts to the list as a PDF report." It reads the file as soon as it opens ("Reading page N of M", with Stop; meanwhile Run says "Wait for the check to finish, or stop it.") and shows the facts as a list. The only field is "Report name" ("Will be saved as `<name>_check.pdf`"); the main button is "Add report to list". After a Stop: "The check was stopped." with "Check again, or add the report: it reads the whole file first." and the button "Check again". If the file cannot be read: "This file could not be read to check it. Open the file in a PDF reader, save a fresh copy and add that." For a result made from a signed file: "This copy was made in PDF Tools from a signed file. A signature still in it does not apply to this copy; check the signed original."

The facts, in this order, on screen and in the report alike (`documentCheck.ts` `factRows`):

| Row | Value | Note |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Pages | the count | |
| Page size | "A4 portrait", or "A4 portrait on 10 pages; A4 landscape on 2 pages" (four sizes, then "2 other sizes on 4 pages"); sizes as shown after the page's rotation: A4, A3, A5, Letter, Legal, otherwise in mm | |
| File size | for example "1.2 MB" | |
| Protection | "No password or restrictions" / "Opened with its password" / "Restrictions, for example on copying or printing" | for the last two: "The copy in this list has no password; the original file still has one." / "The copy in this list has no restrictions; the original file still has them." |
| Digital signatures | "None" / "n signatures" | "Shown as stated in the file. Open the file in Adobe Acrobat Reader to verify the signature." |
| Signature n | "Signed by X on D. Reason: R. Location: L.", or "Signed by X; no readable date in the file.", or "Name not stated in the file; signed on D." | only when the signed range does not reach the end of the file and the file was not opened with its password: "The file was added to after this signature, for example by another signature or a change." |
| Searchable text | "On all n pages" / "On k of n pages" / "On no page" / "On its only page" | |
| Pages that look scanned | k pages | "Make it searchable before converting or redacting." |
| Pages with no text | k pages | "They may be blank, or hold only pictures or drawings." |
| Pages not read | k pages | "Their text could not be read. Open the file in a PDF reader, save a fresh copy and add that." |
| Title, Author | the value, or "Not filled in" | |
| Subject, Keywords; Created with, PDF produced by | only when filled in | |
| Created, Last changed | a plain date ("17 November 2026, 14:05 (UTC+05:30)"), or "Not stated in the file" | |
| PDF version | for example "1.7" | |
| Bookmarks, Form fields, Attachments | "Yes" / "No" | attachments "Yes": "Files are attached inside this PDF. Open it in a PDF reader to see them." |

How it decides: a page with fewer than 20 characters of text looks scanned when anything but text is drawn on it (a picture or a form of any size, a shading, a line or a filled shape), and has no text when nothing is drawn. This is Make searchable's own rule, applied by the same functions (`pageKind` and `pageHasDrawing` of `ocrPlan.ts`), so the pages counted here are exactly the pages that tool recognises (**lead ruling**, 18 November 2026; the earlier rule of a picture over 90% of the page is gone, and a test holds the two tools' counts together). Signatures are taken from the signature fields in their order, then from any other signature dictionary, each once; an empty signature field is not counted. Signer, date, reason and place are shown as the file states them; **Document check never says that a signature is valid** and reads no certificate (contract decision 7; the word "valid" appears nowhere in its screen text or report, tested). Whether more was written after a signature is judged only for files not opened with a password, because an unlocked copy is not the signed bytes. Properties come from the document information first, then from the XMP metadata (read up to 4 MB). Dates are turned into plain words; the stored value is never shown. Shown values have control characters removed and are cut at 300 characters.

The run: "Reading the file…" (only when the dialog's facts do not match the file), "Writing the report…"; then "Report added: `<name>`", with the detail "Open the file in Adobe Acrobat Reader to verify the signature." when the file has a signature and "4 words in another script are shown as [word in another script]." when it applies. A run failure: "The file could not be checked, and nothing was added. Open the file in a PDF reader, save a fresh copy and add that."

#### Compare

Exactly two PDFs ("Select exactly two PDFs to compare."). The dialog: "Finds the words that differ between two versions of a PDF and lists them in a report. The files are not changed." It labels the two files "Earlier version" and "Later version"; the list's order decides which is which, and one "Swap" button changes it (contract decision 4: no guessing from dates). The report's name follows the later file until a name is typed ("Will be saved as `<later name>_changes.pdf`"); the main button is "Compare".

How it compares (`compareText.ts`):

- **Words.** The text of each page is read in the order the file stores it. It is normalised (compatibility forms; soft hyphens, zero-width marks and control characters dropped) and split at any white space. A word broken by a hyphen at a line end before a small letter is one word with the hyphen dropped ("inter-" / "national"); a year range keeps its hyphen ("2024-" / "25"); each also remembers its other form, and takes it when only that form is in the other version, so a paragraph re-wrapped with "co-operative" unbroken in one version and broken in the other is no difference (**lead ruling**, 17 November 2026). Not joined: before a capital, across a blank line, a lone dash, an amount with a trailing minus, a hyphen inside a line.
- **Pages paired** (contract decision 5): pages with identical text first; then, in each gap between them, pages that share at least half their words (of the larger page's words), choosing the pairs that share the most; the rest are "only in the earlier version" or "only in the later version". So an inserted page does not make every later page differ.
- **Word differences** by PDF Tools' own word diff (no package). The common start and end of a page pair are set aside first; if more than 5,000 words a side are left, or the exact diff uses up its step budget, the page is compared in larger pieces (cut where the words allow, so an insertion moves no cut), and then word by word in each differing passage of up to 2,000 words; a larger passage is shown as removed and added whole. Removed and added words next to each other pair up as changed.

The message: "7 of 42 pages differ." ("1 of 13 pages differs."; counts are written with grouped digits, "1,000 of 1,000 pages differ.", in the message and the report alike), where the first number counts the pages in both versions whose text differs and the second is the later version's page count (**lead ruling**, 17 November 2026); pages in one version only have their own sentence: "1 page is only in the earlier version; 2 pages are only in the later version." When no page in both versions differs but some pages are in one only, a report is still made: "No page that is in both versions differs. 1 page is only in the later version." The detail adds, each when it applies: "The report `<name>` lists each change with the words around it."; "It details the first 200 pages that differ and counts the other 33."; "1 very long page was compared in larger pieces, so some changes there are shown as whole passages."; "3 pages have no text to compare; they may be scanned."; "4 words in another script could not be printed and are shown as [word in another script]." Identical text adds nothing: the notice "No text differences found." with "The two versions have the same words in the same order. Nothing was added."

The report (`compareReport.ts`, titled "Comparison of two versions"): a summary first (the row "Pages that differ: 2 of 13", with the small print "Pages that differ are pages in both versions whose text differs, out of the pages of the later version."), then each differing page with its removed, added and changed (old → new) words in five words of context that stop at the next change. It details 200 pages and counts the rest, lists at most 200 changes a page, and shortens a passage of more than 40 words to its first 25 and last 10 with its length. ₹ is printed as "Rs."; the arrow comes from the Symbol font.

Refusals, each ending "Nothing was added.": "`<name>` has more than 1,000 pages. Compare takes files of up to 1,000 pages: split it first."; "`<name>` has no text to read; it looks scanned. Make it searchable first."; "`<name>` could not be read. Open it in a PDF reader, save a fresh copy and add that." Progress: "Reading the earlier version: page n of N", "Reading the later version: page n of N", "Comparing the text…", "Writing the report…". Measured by the lane outside the application (Node, this machine): 1,000 pages against 1,000 different pages took 1.3 s, with no stretch between turns over 38 ms and about 102 MB more memory; a 5,000-word page against itself reversed, 0.24 s.

### 5.14 Limits

- Combined size of files in the list: a warning above 150 MB, and adding is refused above 300 MB, and a run whose results would take the list over 300 MB adds nothing **(design call; the application enforces no file-size limit elsewhere, and these files are held in memory)**. A 300 MB merge to confirm these figures has not yet been run.
- TIFF (5.7): 1,000 pages a file; each page at most 20,000 pixels a side and 50 megapixels (the image limits of the rest of PDF Tools); all pages together at most 1 GB once opened (JPEG pages, which are not opened, do not count); the PDF made at most 300 MB. Decided from the file's tags before anything is decoded; the conversion runs in slices of 25 ms and stops silently, adding nothing, if the engagement or session changes meanwhile.
- Split: at most 500 files in one run (5.3).
- Protect with password: files up to 150 MB (5.18).
- Redact: one file, 20 typed terms, 5,000 ticked places (5.9).
- Bookmarks: 10,000 bookmarks and 32 levels (5.10).
- Phase 3 (contract decision 17): PDF to Excel, PDF to Word and Compare take files of up to 1,000 pages; PDF to Excel writes at most 80 cells a row, 32,767 characters a cell and 1,048,576 rows on one sheet (5.11); the Compare report details 200 pages and counts the rest (5.13); Document check has no page limit; Make searchable gives each page 60 s (5.12).

### 5.15 Organise pages (phase 2)

Added by the owner on 14 November 2026 ("rotate and reorder pages"). One tool, on exactly one PDF, that reorders, rotates and removes pages in one dialog **(design call: one dialog instead of separate Rotate and Reorder tools, because users do these together; the owner chose this grouping on 14 November 2026)**. Merge does not gain page-level ordering: merge first, then organise the merged result **(design call)**. The word on screen is **Rotate** (owner's word; lead ruling); the record key `turned` and the test ids stay.

**The dialog** (`OrganisePagesDialog.tsx`, `organise/`). A grid of the pages (the page panel's tiles, 200 at a time, with the expand icon), each showing its page number in the result and "· was 7" when it moved; a rotated tile is drawn rotated with a badge "Right", "Left" or "180°"; a removed tile stays in place, greyed, struck through and marked "Removed", so that it can be restored. The grid's pages are drawn before the page panel's behind it, and a page the panel has drawn shows at once. Pages are chosen by click, Shift+click (a range) and Ctrl+click.

- Buttons: To start (Alt+Home), Earlier (Alt+Left), Later (Alt+Right), To end (Alt+End), Move to position, Rotate left (Shift+R), Rotate right (R), Remove (Delete), Restore (Delete on a removed page), Undo (Ctrl+Z), Redo (Ctrl+Y) and Reset to the file's own order; up to 100 steps. An unavailable button keeps its focus and says why on hover, focus and click.
- The grid is one Tab stop. Arrows, Home, End, PgUp and PgDn move the focus; Space chooses; Shift with those keys extends a range; Ctrl+A chooses all; Backspace removes as Delete does; Ctrl+Shift+Z also redoes; Enter opens the viewer. When the focused page is not chosen, an edit key acts on the focused page alone and leaves the user's choice as it was; when it is chosen, the key acts on all chosen pages. The VERA Tools tab keys are held back.
- Move to position counts pages of the result, not removed tiles ("Page 1–300", with the rule shown next to the box): the way to move across a long file. Dragging tiles with the mouse also reorders (the browser's own drag and drop, no new library), within the 200 tiles shown; it is an addition to the buttons and keys, never the only way.
- The summary line reads, for example, "2 moved, 2 rotated, 1 removed · 4 pages in the result"; "moved" counts the fewest pages that had to move. Every edit is announced ("Moved. Page 3 (was page 7), position 3 of 40", "Rotated right…"); screen readers hear "rotated right", "rotated left" or "rotated 180°".
- The Run button names the result ("Save 4 pages in the new order", "Save 5 pages with 1 rotated", "Save 9 pages without the 3 removed pages"). With nothing to do it stays focusable and says "Nothing to do yet. Move, rotate or remove a page first."; with every page removed, "You cannot remove every page. Keep at least one." The result name is under "More options".
- The notice says what is kept. Rotating only: "The pages are rotated in the file itself, so its bookmarks, links, form fields, page labels and attachments are all kept." A move or removal: what is not carried over, and, when the file has attachments, "Attachments in the document's list are kept when pages are removed."

**The result** (`pageOrder.ts`; lead rulings):

- Rotating pages only is done in the file itself: a rotation adds to the page's own `/Rotate` (kept a multiple of 90) and the page is not redrawn; everything in the file is kept, except content that earlier saved revisions left and the file no longer uses.
- When pages are moved or removed, the result is made through the page copy of Split and Delete pages (5.3); a run that does both copies, then rotates. It keeps the form fields, links and comments of the kept pages; the bookmarks (5.10); the document language; the attachments (they belong to the document and stay even when every page that mentions them is removed); the named destinations of kept pages; and the page labels, each kept page keeping the label it had. Labels that cannot be read exactly are dropped, and the result says so. The result does not keep XMP metadata, accessibility tags, document scripts and actions, the opening view, viewer settings, article threads or other document data; the result lists which of these the source had.
- Every page of the result is checked against the page it came from: the same content, and every image and font it uses still present; the check gives the window a turn every 25 pages. If a page would change, nothing is made ("This file uses a name form that cannot be kept; page N would lose an image or font. Nothing was created." or "Page N would not look the same in the result. Nothing was created."). A page listed twice is refused. Neither path keeps content of earlier saved revisions.

Default name `<name>_organised.pdf`. Record: summary "Organised pages: 3 moved, 2 rotated, 1 removed" (only what changed), followed by the library's own sentences about what was not kept; metadata `order` (the new order as a page list), `moved`, `turned`, `removed` (each cut within 200 characters with ", … and N more"), `method` (`in-place` or `page-copy`) and, when they apply, `notCarried`, `namedDestinationsDropped` and `pageLabelsDropped`. The flags of 5.3 (`sharedResourcesKept`, `bookmarksDropped`, `signatureInvalid`) apply. A plan that changes nothing makes nothing ("Nothing to change … Nothing was added."). Progress goes page by page ("Rotating / Copying the pages (n of N)…"), then "Checking every page against the original…", then "Checking the result…".

### 5.16 Watermark, stamp and logo (phase 2)

Added by the owner on 14 November 2026. The toolbar's "Watermark" button, a "soon" button in phase 1, is this tool. It works on exactly one PDF and has three parts, each with its own switch; at least one must be on **(design call: watermark, stamp and logo are all marks placed anywhere on the page with a size, angle and transparency; header and footer text belongs with the page numbers, 5.17, because both use the page margins)**. Each part's text, ready-made texts and switch are in view; font, size, angle, colour, opacity, layer and position are under that part's "More options", whose closed line shows the values ("Helvetica bold, auto size, diagonal, grey, 25% opacity, in front, centre"). The fold opens by itself when a field inside it is wrong. The Run button names what it does ("Add watermark to 5 pages", "Add stamp and logo to page 1").

**Watermark** (on by default):

- Text: DRAFT (default), CONFIDENTIAL, COPY, FOR AUDIT USE ONLY, NOT FOR CIRCULATION, the engagement's client name, or custom text of 1–60 characters. The "Client name" choice puts the token `{client}` into the text, never the name itself; the name appears only in the "Prints as …" line, the preview and the PDF, filled in when the file is written, so the record keeps `{client}`. The tokens `{date}` and `{client}` (5.17) may be used in the watermark text as well as the stamp; names are not case-sensitive; any other name in braces is refused by name. `{date}` is written in the format chosen in the dialog, DD-MM-YYYY by default.
- Letters, digits and common punctuation only: the built-in fonts cover Latin text. Anything else is refused with the words as typed and what works today: "“मौसम” and “₹” cannot be printed in the watermark text; use English letters, digits and common signs (write Rs. for ₹)." (at most three words, then "and N more words"; only characters that print as nothing are named by code, "Invisible characters (U+200B)").
- Font Helvetica (default), Times or Courier; bold (on by default) and italic.
- Size: Auto (recommended; the largest size whose text fits 80% of the line it runs along across the page as displayed and whose turned box stays within 90% of the page both ways), Small, Medium, Large (36, 72 and 120 pt, shown as their letter height of about 9, 18 and 30 mm) or another letter height of 3–50 mm, held within 12–200 pt. "Letter height" is Helvetica's capital height; Times and Courier capitals come out somewhat smaller. A fixed size that runs off a page is drawn and reported ("The watermark is larger than page N and is cut off. Choose Auto size or a smaller size.").
- Angle: Diagonal (default; corner to corner of the page as displayed, about 55° on A4 portrait), Horizontal, Vertical, or a custom angle from −90° to 90°.
- Position: centre (default), one of the other eight anchors (top, middle, bottom × left, centre, right; 20 pt, about 7 mm, from the edges), or a point clicked on the preview or typed as a percentage across and down.
- Colour: swatches Black, Grey (default, #808080), Dark red and Dark blue, and "Other…" for any colour; opacity 5–100% (default 25%).
- Layer: **In front of the page** (Recommended, the default) or **Behind the page**. A watermark behind a page is covered by anything opaque on it: behind a scan it cannot be seen at all. When chosen pages are scans, the dialog says "On 2 of the chosen pages (scans) a watermark behind the page cannot be seen. Put it in front to show it there." and Behind stays available. When marks in front meet pages with form fields, those pages are named, because the fields are drawn over marks in front.

**Stamp** (off by default): text VERIFIED, RECEIVED, CHECKED, PAID, ORIGINAL SEEN or custom (1–60 characters, the same characters and tokens as above); size Small, Medium (20 pt, recommended), Large (14, 20 and 32 pt) or another letter height of 2–18 mm, held within 8–72 pt; bold on; colour dark red #A00000; opacity 78% (the owner's numbers); angle 0° (−90° to 90°); position top right (default), another anchor or a point; 20 pt from the edges.

**Logo** (off by default): an image already in the working list (PNG or JPG, added from the computer or the Evidence Vault like any file, so the file limits and the engagement rules apply; WebP, BMP, GIF and one-page TIFF are already PNG after intake), chosen from a list of the list's images and never remembered after the dialog closes **(design call: no path or image is stored)**. Width 18% of the page's displayed width (5–50%, at least 28 pt), height capped at 35% of the displayed height (the owner's numbers); position top left (default), another anchor or a point; 20 pt from the edges; opacity 100% (10–100%, so that a large faint logo can serve as an image watermark). A logo over 1,600 px on its long side is reduced to at most 1,200 px before the run (480 px for the preview); a phone photo's stored rotation is applied; a CMYK JPEG is redrawn in RGB; over 25 megapixels is refused.

**Pages**: all (default), a range from–to, or the first page only, which applies to everything that is on, the watermark included ("Everything that is on, the watermark included, goes on page 1 only").

Marks are placed in the frame of the page as the reader sees it, so they come out right on turned and cropped pages. They are drawn in this order: watermark behind, the page, watermark in front, logo, stamp, each after the page's own drawing is closed off (5.6). Transparency uses a graphics state (`/ca`); the page's own content is not changed; pages that share resources share one copy with the mark's entries added, and the original resources are not changed (about 0.4–0.7 KB a page). Watermark and stamp text is real text: it can be searched and copied, and Redact will see it.

The preview is the library's own output for a one-page copy of the chosen page, so it cannot disagree with the result (within 0.72 pt in the application); it takes about 0.4–0.7 s after a change on a loaded machine. Its expand button opens the viewer with every chosen page drawn marked.

After the run: a watermark behind the page may not be visible (a scan, a picture or a solid background covers it), or may be hidden but this could not be checked (a pattern or a masked picture lies over it); "may be hidden" is an estimate. A page whose own drawing is damaged or cannot be read may have its marks misplaced: the result names the page, and the record gets the flag `markMayBeMisplaced` ("; a mark may be misplaced"). A stamp, logo or text that fits the previewed page but not a later chosen page is refused at Run, naming the page: "The stamp does not fit on page 4. Use shorter text, a smaller size or a smaller distance from the edge. Nothing was added."

Default name `<name>_watermarked.pdf`, or `<name>_stamped.pdf` when only the stamp or logo is on. The result message is "Watermark added to all 5 pages." with the notes above. Record (counts and choices only): summary "Watermark DRAFT, stamp (custom text), logo, all pages" (or "pages 1-12", "page 1"); metadata `pages`, and per part the ready-made text or a template of tokens and punctuation as it is (`DRAFT`, `{client}`), else `custom` with the text's length and the tokens it uses (`watermarkLength`, `watermarkUses`), never the text; font, size, angle, colour, opacity, layer and position; for the logo `logo: true`, its size in pixels, width and opacity, never its file name. `activity.ts` keeps only these keys.

### 5.17 Page numbers, header and footer (phase 2)

The owner asked for header and footer text, a stamp and a logo, and more page-number styles. Header and footer are added to the existing Page numbers tool rather than a second tool **(design call: page numbers, headers and footers share the same six places in the page margins; in one dialog they can never be placed on top of each other)**. The toolbar button stays "Page numbers"; its description is "Add page numbers, headers and footers." Stamp and logo are in 5.16.

**Layout.** Phase 1's position, format and pages stay in view, with the test ids the application test uses. Start number, number style, font, bold, italic, size, colour and margin are under "More options", whose closed line shows the values ("Starts at 1, numbers 1, 2, 3, Helvetica, normal size (2.5 mm), black, normal margin (10 mm)"). Header and footer text is under its own fold, "Header and footer" (closed line "None", or the places and texts): a switch "Add page numbers too" (numbers can be turned off when there is text), a token bar that inserts at the caret of the field last used, six fields in a header row and a footer row (the number's place reads "Page number"), "On page 3 this prints …" for the focused line, and "Write {date} as" once a line uses `{date}`. The Run button names what it does ("Add page numbers to 5 pages", "Add page numbers, header and footer to 5 pages", "Add header and footer to 5 pages"). A one-page file says "The only page".

**Places.** Six: top left, top centre, top right, bottom left, bottom centre, bottom right. The page number takes the place chosen under Position (5.6). Each of the other places can hold a line of text. Putting text in the page number's place is refused: "The page number is already at the bottom centre. Choose another place for this text, or write {page} in the text."

**Text and tokens.** Each line is 1–120 characters with these tokens, filled in when the tool runs:

- `{page}`: the page number in the chosen style; `{pages}`: the number of numbered pages plus the start number less one (as "1 of N" in phase 1);
- `{file}`: the source's file name as shown in the list (already cleaned, never a path); `{name}`: the same without `.pdf`;
- `{date}`: the date the tool runs, in the format chosen in the dialog: DD-MM-YYYY (default, the usual form on Indian audit papers), DD/MM/YYYY, YYYY-MM-DD, or "14 November 2026";
- `{client}`: the client name of the engagement in which the tool runs; `{fy}`: that engagement's financial year. Both are read when Run is pressed, with the engagement scope (section 7).

An unknown token such as `{pge}` is refused with its name. Characters outside the built-in fonts are refused with the sentence of 5.16 (in the prefix, suffix, header and footer, and in the dialog's live checks).

**Number styles** (applied to the number and to `{page}`): 1, 2, 3 (default); i, ii, iii; I, II, III; a, b, c (after z the numbers continue from 27, as the owner's tool); and numbers in circles. Formats gain "1." and "Pg-1" beside 1, Page 1, 1 of N, Page 1 of N and custom. Font Helvetica, Times or Courier, bold and italic. Numbers in circles: a circle of radius 0.75 × the larger of the number's width and height plus 3 pt, line 1 pt in the number's colour **(design call: lighter than the owner's tool)**; checked in pdf.js (digits centred, ring within 0.42 pt of its intended box) and offered only with the plain number format ("A number in a circle goes with the plain number format. Choose "1, 2, 3" or another style.").

**Sizes and margins.** Size: Small, Normal (recommended), Large (8, 10 and 14 pt; about 2, 2.5 and 3.5 mm letter height) or another letter height of 1.5–9 mm. Margin: Narrow, Normal (recommended), Wide (14, 28 and 57 pt; 5, 10 and 20 mm) or 3–50 mm. The values stay within 6–36 pt and 8–144 pt, and the recommended presets give exactly phase 1's 10 pt and 28 pt, so an untouched dialog gives phase 1's options, name and record **(design call: phase 1's default margin kept)**. Colour: swatches and "Other…". Header and footer lines are drawn upright in the page as displayed, by the phase 1 placement code. Every line is checked to fit on every page before anything is drawn; a header row and a footer row are refused when twice the margin, both rows and 8 pt do not fit the page height, naming the page; neighbours on one row need 8 pt between them. A refusal at Run ends with "Nothing was added." ("The header or footer text does not fit on page 2. Use shorter text, a smaller font size or a smaller margin. Nothing was added.").

**Placement.** Every mark is drawn after the page's own drawing is closed off (the phase 1 fix in 5.6). On pages with `/UserUnit`, margins and sizes are real points (a deliberate change: phase 1 numbers on such pages move; ordinary pages are unchanged). A page whose drawing cannot be read with certainty is drawn and named: "A page number, header or footer may be misplaced on page N: the page's own drawing instructions are damaged or could not be read. Check that page." The record gets the flag `markMayBeMisplaced` ("; a mark may be misplaced").

Default name `<name>_numbered.pdf`, or `<name>_header_footer.pdf` when header or footer text is used. Record: the phase 1 summary "Added page numbers", or "Added header and footer" / "Added page numbers, header and footer"; a header or footer line recorded under `text_top_left` … `text_bottom_right` as it is only when it is made of tokens and punctuation (`{client} - {fy}`), else as `custom` with its length and tokens (`text_top_left_length`, `text_top_left_uses`); the text before and after a custom number the same way (`prefix`, `prefixLength`, `suffix`, `suffixLength`), recorded only with the Custom format; plus the style, font and date-format choices; the values the tokens are filled with (`{client}`, `{fy}`, file name, date) are never recorded.

### 5.18 Protect with password (phase 2)

Added by the owner on 14 November 2026. Makes a copy of one PDF that asks for a password when opened ("…; without it, only the number and size of its pages can be seen.").

**Dialog** (`ProtectDialog.tsx`, `protect/`).

- "Password" and "Type it again", side by side, both hidden, with one "Show" / "Hide" button for both. The match is checked as soon as the second field stops being the beginning of the first ("The two passwords match." when it does).
- **Password limits.** 8 characters to 127 bytes: 127 letters, digits and symbols of an English keyboard, fewer of other characters (most Hindi letters count three bytes), because the standard cuts there and some PDF readers refuse longer passwords. The password is used in Unicode normal form NFC. Control characters are refused. A space at the start or end is kept, with the warning "The password starts or ends with a space. It must be typed with that space every time." Characters not on an English keyboard are allowed, with the warning "Some PDF readers cannot open files protected with non-English characters. Letters, digits and symbols on an English keyboard are safest."
- A plain strength hint, never a block: "Weak: use at least 12 characters and mix letters, digits and symbols.", "Fair: mixing in a third kind of character (letters, digits, symbols) makes it strong.", "Strong.".
- Under "Keep the password safe": "If the password is forgotten, nobody can open the protected file, including you. VERA does not keep the password. The unprotected file stays in the list."
- No permission settings: the protected file allows printing, copying and changes once opened (choices are a candidate, section 3).
- The Run button is "Make protected copy"; while it cannot be used it stays focusable and says why ("Type a password of at least 8 characters, then type it again to confirm it.", "The two passwords must be the same."). The result name is under "More options". Above 20 MB the dialog says "This can take a minute for large scans."

**How it is built.** `@cantoo/pdf-lib` 2.11.1, already a dependency and already used to open protected files, encrypts the result with **AES-256** (`/V 5 /R 6`, crypt filter AESV3, strings and streams encrypted, metadata encrypted). The open password and the permissions password are the same (as in the owner's tool). Every permission is granted explicitly: the library's default forbids printing, copying, editing, form filling and accessibility (`/P -3904`); with all permissions given it writes `/P -4`. AES-128 and RC4 are not offered: AES-128 refused non-Latin passwords in the trial, and RC4 is broken.

- **Sealing.** Even saved with object streams, the library left strings readable in the catalog, the page tree, pages, stream and signature dictionaries and objects of generation 1. `protect.ts` seals every such string itself before saving, with a test per kind of place; a file that claims PDF/A-1 is written without object streams with every string sealed. The file is saved once (a second save encrypted it twice).
- **What stays visible without the password.** The password hides every string and stream in the file: all text, form values, comments, bookmark titles, attachments, pictures and the XMP metadata. The file's structure stays visible: the number of pages, their sizes and rotation, font names, image sizes, and the kinds of annotations and attachments.
- **Signed files.** The value of a digital signature is encrypted with everything else, so that the signer's certificate cannot be read without the password. This departs from ISO 32000-2, which leaves it unencrypted **(lead ruling; the owner can override)**. The signature is no longer valid after protecting in any case; some readers then show it as unreadable rather than as changed. The pre-run warning says "This file carries a digital signature. The protected copy will not carry a valid signature: keep the signed original as the evidence."
- **PDF/A files** are protected, not refused, and the result says "This file is marked PDF/A (archival). A password-protected copy cannot be PDF/A; the copy keeps the mark but no longer meets the standard." The XMP is not changed; the record gains `noLongerArchival: true` **(lead ruling)**. The note is shown before the run when the file's uncompressed XMP shows the claim, otherwise after it.

**The check.** The result is opened with the password by VERA's own unlock code and compared with the source object by object, as well as by page count; any difference adds nothing ("The protected file could not be verified. Nothing was added. Try again; if it fails again, restart the application."). Both sides are read with the same library's parser (11a). In a review, pdf.js and six other open-source PDF readers and libraries refused the results without the password and opened them with it; Adobe Acrobat Reader, Microsoft Edge and Google Chrome were not tried (section 9).

**Size and time.** Files over 150 MB are refused before anything is read ("This file is larger than 150 MB, too large to protect in PDF Tools. Compress it or split it into parts, then protect the smaller files."): protecting and checking hold four to five times the file in memory. The run shows three steps with a moving bar: "Step 1 of 3: reading the file…", "Step 2 of 3: protecting it with the password…" and "Step 3 of 3: checking that the copy opens with the password…"; steps 2 and 3 show how far they have got as a percentage. The work reads and writes about 1 MB of objects at a time (up to 1,500 small objects; it was 50), giving the window a turn between slices, so the screen keeps painting and Stop works within each step. Measured in the application at 83–100% CPU: a 320-page text ledger took 21–26 s (146–173 s before the slices were enlarged); earlier, a 52 MB, 40-page scan took 35.7 s in all and a 26 MB scan 18–25 s, with Stop landing within about 0.2–2 s.

**The password** is held only while the tool runs. It is never stored, logged, written to the activity record, shown in a message or error, or kept with the result. Closing the dialog, a change of engagement or user, or the end of the run clears it. The password fields' values are wiped as the fields leave the screen, and after a Stop or a failed run fresh fields replace them, because the screen library otherwise kept the last value in memory after the dialog. A heap search in the application found the password in no DOM node, storage, database, console line or profile file after the dialog closed, and after a Stop and a failed run with the dialog still open.

**The result row.** Marked "Protected" (hint: "Opens only with the password you set. VERA does not keep it."). It is built from the checked copy: thumbnails, the page panel, the bookmark list and the viewer use the unprotected bytes kept with the row in memory (counted in the 300 MB list limit), never the password; those bytes go when the row is removed, the engagement or user changes, or the application closes. Save to computer, the Evidence Vault and the working paper always use the protected bytes; the vault and working-paper dialogs add "This file is password-protected. Reviewers will need the password to open it. VERA does not keep passwords." A protected result cannot be used as input to another tool; the toolbar says "This file is protected by PDF Tools. Use the file it was made from." **(design call: otherwise the password would have to be kept or asked again)**. When a protected file comes back into PDF Tools later (for example from the vault), it is a protected file like any other and asks for its password. The result message is "Protected:" with the result's name, then "It opens only with the password you set. VERA does not keep it. The unprotected original stays in the list."

Default name `<name>_protected.pdf`. Record: "Protected with a password (AES-256)", metadata `{ protection: "AES-256" }` and, for a PDF/A source, `noLongerArchival`; no password, hint or length. A result made from a signed file is marked "Signature no longer valid".

### 5.19 Resize or crop pages (phase 2)

Added by the owner on 15 November 2026 ("make every page of a mixed scan bundle A4, trim margins, add margins"). One tool, on exactly one PDF, in the Organise group after Organise pages (`pageBox.ts`, `pageBoxRun.ts`, `PageBoxDialog.tsx`, `pagebox/`). The dialog says "Make every page of a copy one size, or crop its pages. The original is not changed."

**The dialog.** Two plain choices:

- **Make every page one size** ("A4, or another size you choose."; the default). It opens ready to run: "Resize 7 pages to A4". The size is one drop-down: A4 (210 × 297 mm, default), A3, A5, Letter, Legal, or "Own size (set under More options)". Each page keeps its direction (a landscape page becomes A4 landscape); the content is scaled to fit and centred, never stretched. Small pages keep their size, in the middle of the page ("A receipt stays receipt-sized, in the middle of the page.") **(design call: a 1:1 copy of small evidence is the safer default)**. Pages already that size, within 1 mm, are left exactly as they are; the dialog says how many ("1 page is already A4 and stays as it is."). Under "More options" (closed line "Each page keeps its direction, small pages keep their size, no margin"): the own size (width × height, 10 to 5,080 mm), Direction (keep each page's direction, Recommended; every page portrait; every page landscape), "Do not enlarge small pages" (on, Recommended), a margin around the content (None, Recommended; Narrow 5 mm; Normal 10 mm; another value up to 200 mm; with a margin, pages already that size are fitted inside it too) and the result name.
- **Crop the pages** ("Trim the edges, shown by a frame on the preview."): four boxes in mm, laid out as the page is (10 mm each to start), and a frame on the preview: the mouse drags an edge or the whole frame; each edge is a slider that the arrow keys move (1 mm; Shift: 10 mm; Home: not trimmed; End: as far as it goes), announced ("Left edge: 15 mm trimmed"). Boxes and frame stay in step. The notice "Trimming hides, it does not remove" says "Trimmed parts are hidden, not removed. To remove content, use Redact." **(lead ruling)**. Under "More options" (closed line "The same on every page"): "Swap left and right on even pages" ("For pages printed on both sides."), "Add white space instead of trimming" ("Each page grows by the widths above; nothing is trimmed."; up to 500 mm on each edge) and the result name.

Pages: all, or a range, with the range field and messages of Watermark. The preview shows a chosen page as the run makes it (the library's own call on a one-page copy, drawn by pdf.js), or the page with the crop frame over it, with its size before and after ("This page: 216 × 279 mm (Letter) → 210 × 297 mm (A4)"; "Page 7 is already A4: it stays exactly as it is."); it steps through the chosen pages. The checks before Run use every page's real size, read by pdf.js in its worker; the run checks everything again.

Refusals, at the field and at Run, naming the page: "Trimming that much leaves less than 10 mm of page 8. Use smaller values."; "The margin leaves less than 10 mm for the page content. Use a smaller margin."; "Page 2 would be larger than 5,080 mm, the largest page a PDF can have. Use a smaller margin."; when every chosen page is already the size: "All 3 chosen pages are already A4. Choose another size, add a margin, or choose other pages." At Run a refusal ends "Nothing was added."

**How it is built.**

- Every chosen page is planned before anything changes, on the page as the reader sees it (its visible box, its own rotation and `/UserUnit`), so a refusal names its page and changes nothing.
- Resize: the page's own content is not re-encoded. It is wrapped as marks are (5.16): one scale-and-move, a clip to the page's old visible area, a `q` for each stray `Q`; the original streams unchanged; then what closes it all. The page keeps its own `/Rotate`, so the change is always an upright, uniform scale plus a move, and every coordinate that points into the page moves by the same numbers: comments, links, ink, lines, polygons, callouts, form-field widgets (and the field's text size used when a reader draws the text again, set on the widget so that a field shown on other pages keeps its own), article beads, bookmarks, named destinations, the opening view and every GoTo action (the zoom is left as it was). Border widths scale with the page. Patterns get copies with the same transform. A `/UserUnit` is folded into the scale and dropped: the new page is A4 in real points in every reader.
- Trim: only the page's boxes change (MediaBox = CropBox = the smaller area); the content streams are the source's, byte for byte. **Nothing is removed from the file**: the trimmed parts' text and pictures stay in it; VERA's own viewer no longer shows or reads them, other programs may. A comment, link or form field wholly in a trimmed part is hidden, not removed, and the result says so ("On page 1 a link, comment or form field is now in the trimmed part, hidden but still in the file.").
- Add white space: the boxes grow; the content is clipped to its old visible area, so nothing the page hid before shows in the new margin.
- Every changed page loses its print-production boxes (bleed, trim, art) and its stored thumbnail, because they describe the old page. Everything else is carried: form values, comments, bookmarks, attachments, document properties (Producer becomes "ICAI VERA"; Creator is kept), the XMP metadata, layers, tags. Text stays text.
- Kept and flagged, never kept too little: a page whose drawing closes more than it opens (readers differ on such pages), a page whose drawing cannot be read, an annotation of an unknown kind or one with a measuring scale, a page with viewports, and a pattern that a form without resources of its own would take. The result names the page: "Check page 2: its content could not be read exactly, so it may look different." / "Check page 1: a comment or measurement on it may not have resized exactly."
- A signed file gets the general signed-file warning before the run; the result is marked "Signature no longer valid".

**Result and record.** Default name `<name>_resized.pdf`, `<name>_cropped.pdf` or `<name>_with_margins.pdf`. The message: "7 pages resized to A4; 1 was already A4 and is unchanged.", "8 pages trimmed; the trimmed parts are hidden, not removed.", "Margins added to 8 pages.", then one sentence per thing to check; with nothing to change, "Nothing to change: every chosen page is already A4. Nothing was added." Record (counts and choices only): summary "Resized 7 pages to A4, 1 already A4" ("; 1 page to check" when there is one), or "Trimmed 8 pages: left 15 mm, top 20 mm, right 10 mm, bottom 10 mm, mirrored on even pages"; metadata `mode`, `size` (paper name or "200 × 300 mm"), `direction`, `enlarge`, `marginMm`, or `leftMm`, `topMm`, `rightMm`, `bottomMm`, `mirror`; `pages` ("all" or "2-5"), `changed`, `unchanged`, `pagesToCheck` (`activity.ts` keeps only these). Progress goes page by page ("Resizing page n of N…"), then "Writing the file…"; Stop takes effect between pages.

Measured on a machine shared with other work: the library resized a 500-page mixed file (300 pages changed, 200 already A4) in 1.9 s in Node; in the application 500 pages took 9.6–10.0 s from Run to the result row, and a confirmed Stop ended a 500-page trim in 0.36–0.75 s, adding nothing.

## 6. Where results go

Each result can be sent to any of the three destinations, more than once.

### Save to computer

- **One result**: a save dialog; the file is saved as a PDF (or its own type for images, Excel and Word).
- **Several results**: the user picks a folder once, and each result is saved into it as its own file. An existing file of the same name is never overwritten silently; the new file gets a numbered suffix such as `name (2).pdf`.
- "Save as one zip" is offered as an extra for several results, for emailing. It is never the default.

The row is marked "Saved to computer" only when the save is known to have completed; a cancelled dialog changes nothing. Any file in the list, not only a result, can be saved to the computer; the activity record then reads "Copy of a file from the computer" or "from the Evidence Vault" (and "without its password" for a protected file; ", converted from TIFF to PDF" for a converted TIFF).

The folder window: the user's own Desktop, Documents and Downloads folders, and anything inside them, can be chosen.
