Any other folder that Chromium treats as restricted (the profile root, AppData, Windows, Program Files) is refused by a listener in `electron/main.cjs`; the pick then ends as "no folder" and a quiet note appears once: "Nothing was saved. No folder was chosen. Windows and program folders cannot be used for saving; choose another folder, such as Desktop or Documents." The note also appears if the folder window ends without a folder for any other reason, and it is withdrawn if a folder is then chosen. Only the latest click's save gives the note, and Save As and zip saves never do. As safety nets: if the save or folder window does not answer within 10 seconds, the Save button is released so that it can be pressed again (the screen is never blocked as a whole); and if the browser refuses to open a second picker, the message "A save window is already open. Finish or close it, then try again. If no save window is open, restart the application." is shown, nothing is saved, nothing is marked, and the file is never silently downloaded instead. Verified with the real folder window in a throw-away probe; the owner is to confirm it through the application (section 9).

If the pickers are not available, the file is sent as an ordinary download. A download cannot tell the application whether it was saved (Electron may show its own Save As window, which can be cancelled). The row's badge then reads "Download started" (hint: "A download was started, because no save dialog was available. PDF Tools cannot tell if it was kept, or where."; phase 1 called it "Sent to download"), and the activity record says "Computer, by download (completion not confirmed)".

How it is built **(design call)**: the application's present downloads cannot tell whether a save completed, and its export channel forces a Word file name. The browser's save-file and folder pickers are used, with the download fallback above. They were found to be present in the development build and when the page loads from `file://`. No main-process save handler was added; the only main-process change is the folder-access listener described above (section 8). The real native windows are not covered by the automated test.

### Add to Evidence Vault

A small form for the ticked results: a name for each (default: the result name), one evidence type for all (the vault's existing five types, default "Document"), and an optional "Workpaper reference". This destination takes results only; a file added from the computer or the vault is already a file the user has, so it is left out and the dialog says how many were left out. With no result ticked, the destination is unavailable and says "Only results can be added to the Evidence Vault from here. Upload originals in the Evidence Vault itself." Saving uses the vault's existing upload path, so the file belongs to the current engagement, is recorded as uploaded by the current user, starts in draft, and appears in the Evidence Vault like any other upload. Files are sent one at a time, with progress and a Stop that finishes the current file. The shared upload code shows its own "File uploaded successfully" message for each file, with no file name.

The row is then marked "In Evidence Vault" (its hint names the vault record). The form warns when this engagement's vault already has a file of the same name, and when the result is already in the vault ("Adding it again keeps a second copy"), but does not block **(design call)**. Names repeated within one batch get " (2)", " (3)".

### Attach to working paper

In this application a working paper attachment is a file on an Audit Execution programme, on one of its sections, or on a working box inside a section, and it always points at a vault file. So this destination saves to the Evidence Vault first (same form as above) and then attaches.

If the result is already in the Evidence Vault, that vault record is attached and no second copy is uploaded. Attaching the same result to a second programme, section or box also reuses the one record. Before reusing a vault record, the application checks that it still exists in the vault list. If it has been deleted in the Evidence Vault screen, the "In Evidence Vault" mark is cleared and the vault form is shown first. Like Add to Evidence Vault, this destination takes results only.

The user picks the programme, then optionally a section, then optionally a box. If the engagement has no audit programme, the option is disabled with an explanation. Sections and boxes marked locked are shown but cannot be chosen **(design call: the main process does not enforce these locks today, and PDF Tools should not be a way to add material to locked work)**.

If the vault save succeeds and the attachment fails, the file stays in the vault and the row is marked "In Evidence Vault". The user is told the attachment failed and can retry it from the same row, or attach from Audit Execution. The vault record is not rolled back, because it is valid evidence.

Existing behaviour to be aware of, unchanged here: in Audit Execution, removing the last attachment that points at a vault file also deletes that vault file.

### Excel and Word results (phase 3)

Workbooks and Word documents go to the same three destinations with nothing changed outside PDF Tools: Save to computer keeps their own type (`saveToComputer.ts` already knew `.xlsx` and `.docx`; one result through the Save window, several through the folder window or one zip); Add to Evidence Vault and Attach to working paper use the vault's upload path as for PDFs. The integration lane checked in the real application that the vault and the working paper take them with the right name, extension and type, and that the stored bytes equal the result's (scenarios P3-5 to P3-13, section 9). The reports of Document check and Compare, and Make searchable's result, are PDFs like any other.

### File names

A name is cleaned before use: characters the vault rejects (`\ / : * ? " < > |`) are replaced with `_`, trailing dots and spaces are removed, the right extension is ensured (case-insensitive), and an empty name falls back to `document`. Control characters and the line and paragraph separators (U+2028, U+2029) are replaced too. If a name cannot be numbered, it falls back to "result (n)". Names longer than 120 characters are cut in the middle, keeping their start and their end ("…Tax Aud…ith Annexures for AY 2025-26 - final copy v3 revised_pages_1-2.pdf"); a name cut before is cut again at the same place, never with a second "…". A name already in the list gets " (2)", " (3)" without regard to case, as Windows does. The same cleaned name is used for all three destinations. Phase 3 default names (contract decision 16): `<name>.xlsx`, `<name>.docx`, `<name>_check.pdf`, `<later name>_changes.pdf` and `<name>_searchable.pdf`; a second workbook of the same name in the list gets " (2)" (`TB (2).xlsx`).

### Record of what was done

Every send to a destination writes one PDF Tools activity-log entry for the engagement **(design call)**:

- The Audit Trail screen shows only an entry's action, entity and details text. So the details text carries the readable record: operation (for example "Merged 3 files", "Compressed, Balanced", "Compressed to fit 5 MB", "Redacted 14 areas on 3 pages"), destination, source file names with origin, result name, page count, "Still in the file: …" when the result carries comments, attachments or properties (5.1), and the first 12 characters of the result's SHA-256.
- The entry's metadata holds the same facts in structured form, with the full SHA-256, the vault record ids of any vault sources, and `carried` (counts and property names). The screen does not display metadata today and is not changed.
- Passwords and redacted values never appear in either.
- A vault save also produces the vault's own "Uploaded" entry, because the existing upload path is reused unchanged. Two entries for a vault save are accepted.
- The vault stores no hash of its files. The SHA-256 exists only in this log entry; it lets a reviewer check a stored file against what PDF Tools produced.
- A failed log write is reported to the user as a warning; it does not undo the save.
- A result made from earlier results lists its whole chain. The details text reads `Sources: … Earlier steps: <what was done> → <result name>; … (and N earlier steps). Original sources: <name> [vault|computer|result]; …`. The last 10 earlier steps are listed and the rest are counted. The entry's metadata holds `earlierSteps`, `earlierStepsNotListed` and `originalSources`, with the vault record id of each original source that came from the vault. So a vault file that was compressed and then numbered is traceable to the vault record it started from; a redacted, watermarked and protected bundle lists every step.
- Flags, each also a metadata key, with the note the readable summary ends with: `formFieldsDropped` ("; form fields not carried"), `bookmarksDropped` ("; some bookmarks not carried"), `signatureInvalid` ("; signature no longer valid"), `sharedResourcesKept` ("; may still contain images or fonts of left-out pages", for the reasons listed in section 5.3), `protectionRemoved` ("; protection removed", 5.1), `markMayBeMisplaced` ("; a mark may be misplaced", Watermark and Page numbers), `redactedValuesLeftVisible` ("; some chosen values left visible") and `textLayerIncomplete` ("; some text on redacted pages is not searchable").
- Each phase 2 tool's summary and metadata are given in its section: Split into several files (5.3), Compress Fit to a size (5.5), Redact (5.9), Bookmarks (5.10), Organise pages (5.15), Watermark (5.16), Page numbers with header and footer (5.17), Protect with password (5.18), Resize or crop pages (5.19). A Redact, Protect, Resize or crop, Watermark or Page numbers record keeps only the keys its section lists, each of the expected shape; anything else is dropped from the entry, also in the earlier steps of a later result (`activity.ts`). A Redact entry never holds a redacted value, a typed term, a custom replacement token or masked text; a Protect entry never holds the password or anything about it except the algorithm; a Watermark or Page numbers entry never holds a text the user typed, nor the logo's file name. The password boxes of Protect and of the prompt for a protected file share one rule: their values are wiped as they leave the screen.
- Phase 3 records (`activity.ts` keeps only these keys for each tool, each of the expected shape, the same as each tool's `RECORD_KEYS`; a test holds the two together). They hold counts and one choice at most: never a cell, a heading, a signer's name, a document property, a word that differs or a recognised word.
  - PDF to Excel: "Converted to Excel: 3 pages, 1 sheet"; `sheetMode` (`one-sheet` or `per-page`), `pages`, `sheets`, `rows`, `numberCells`, `tablesRuled`, `tablesAligned`, `pagesAsText`, `pagesNoText` (pages with little or no text) and, since 20 November 2026, `pagesFromScan` (pages typed from a scan's recognised text; written only when above 0).
  - PDF to Word: "Converted to Word: 12 pages"; `pages`, `paragraphs`, `headings`, `tables`, `pagesNoText` and, since 20 November 2026, `pagesFromScan` (written only when above 0).
  - Document check: "Document check: 12 pages"; `pages`, `signatures`, `pagesWithText`, `pagesScanned`, `protection` (`none`, `password` or `restrictions`).
  - Compare: "Compared 2 files: 7 of 42 pages differ", with, when it applies, "; 1 page only in the earlier version, 2 pages only in the later version"; `pagesEarlier`, `pagesLater`, `pagesDiffer`, `pagesOnlyEarlier`, `pagesOnlyLater`, `wordsAdded`, `wordsRemoved`, `wordsChanged`, `pagesWithoutText`, `pagesComparedCoarsely`.
  - Make searchable: "Made searchable: 8 of 12 pages recognised"; `pages`, `pagesRecognised`, `pagesSkipped` (pages that already had text, were blank, or failed), `pagesTimedOut`, `words`, `lowConfidenceWords`, `numbersToCheck`, `pagesFaint`, `charactersDropped`, and, since 20 November 2026, `wordsLeftOut` (words the engine doubted and left out) and `pagesPartlyRead`: eleven counts; `pagesRecognised + pagesSkipped + pagesTimedOut = pages`. The build contract had named a mean and a lowest confidence; they were replaced by counts after the owner's decision of 18 November 2026 (5.12).
  - Document check and Compare do not set `protectionRemoved` (their report is not a copy of the source); the other three do when a source was opened with its password.
- If the engagement is changed while an upload is still running, the upload finishes in the engagement it was started for, and its PDF Tools entry is still written there, silently, with that engagement's identity. A file must not exist in the vault without its provenance line. The user sees no message about it.
- Computer saves are logged too: "Destination: Computer.", or "Computer, by download (completion not confirmed)", with ", in one zip file" where that applies.
- Runs whose results are never saved are not recorded **(design call)**.

## 7. Roles, engagement and security

- The tab requires a signed-in user and a selected engagement; that is unchanged.
- Anyone who can open the engagement can use the tools and save to its vault. That is the existing vault rule, enforced in the main process (`electron/sqlite-authorization.cjs`), and this feature adds no path around it.
- Vault input is read through the existing `file-download` channel and vault output through the existing insert and `file-save` channels. Both already refuse other engagements' files.
- Attaching uses the existing `audit_program_attachments` insert. Removing an attachment later remains partner/admin only, as today.
- Switching engagement or signing out clears the file list, results and any held passwords before the new context is shown; with unsaved results the application asks first (section 4). The question is asked by `src/contexts/EngagementContext.tsx` through a guard PDF Tools registers once, beside the list's other module-level protections (`leaveGuard.ts`, `useWorkingList.ts`); it returns no question when nothing is unsaved, and the clearing code itself is unchanged.
- Long-running steps (Redact's finding and rebuilding, Protect, Organise pages, the TIFF conversion, Split into several files) capture the list's context key and session when Run is pressed and check them before every slow step and before adding anything; the Redact dialog's proposals, the page viewer and its Find, and a protected row's unprotected preview bytes are dropped on a change of engagement or user. Redacted values, typed terms, search words and passwords exist only in the dialog's memory while it is open and are never written to the activity log, localStorage, the console or a message that can outlive an engagement switch.
- All processing is local. No tool makes a network request. Phase 2 adds no main-process code, IPC channel, native window, network use or dependency. Its only renderer-side additions of note are a module worker that reads bookmarks off the window thread (5.10) and lazily loaded parts for each phase 2 tool. The application test records the renderer's network requests and reports any made during PDF Tools steps; it tolerates requests for font files to Google Fonts (section 12), which are not made by PDF Tools. Network use by the main process is not observed by it. The lanes' drives found no request from a PDF Tools step; the only requests seen were the application's own font requests to Google Fonts at start-up and when a character outside the loaded font subset was shown.
- The JavaScript engine keeps, process-wide, the last text any regular expression matched. PDF Tools clears it when the engagement or user changes, after every run, when a destination batch settles and when a dialog or the Find box closes, because Redact's patterns and other tools match document text (other parts of the application can leave their own text there; that is outside PDF Tools). The bookmark reader keeps each file's titles in memory while the file is in the list, as thumbnails are kept.
- Phase 3 adds no main-process code, IPC channel, native window or network use, and writes nothing to localStorage. Its renderer-side additions of note are two background workers, one that writes Excel workbooks (5.11) and the recognition engine's (5.12, with its cache switched off and local paths only), and the recognition engine's files under `public/pdf-tools/ocr/`. Excel and Word results are held in memory like every other result until the user saves or sends them.
- The only thing phase 2 writes to localStorage is the Redact profile name (5.9). The lanes' drives found no password, typed term, search word or value in the database, profile files, storage or console.

## 8. How it is built

Decided:

- All new code lives in new files: UI under `src/components/pdf-tools/`, and the PDF operations as plain functions under `src/lib/pdf-tools/` with no React or Electron dependency, so they can be unit-tested.
- The inline `PDFTools` component and the `pdf-lib` import were removed from `src/pages/AuditTools.tsx` in phase 1. The tab mounts a small component, `src/components/pdf-tools/PdfToolsMount.tsx`, which loads the screen lazily, shows the loading outline (section 4) and contains a failure to load the screen. Apart from the removal, the edit to the page is one import line and one mount line, because other parallel work adds tools to the same page. `main` rewrote `AuditTools.tsx` in PR #114; the mount and the tab id `pdf` are unchanged.
- Existing hooks are reused as they are: `useEvidenceFiles`, `useAuditExecution` and its section, box and attachment hooks, `useActivityLogs`, `useEngagement`, `useAuth`.
- Every reading and writing of a PDF document by the tools other than Redact passes `pace.ts`: `loadPaced` and `savePaced` replace pdf-lib's count of objects between waits with a clock (a turn at least every 50 ms of work), and `withClockWaits` does the same for the second library used by Protect and unlock; `PARSE_OBJECTS_PER_TICK` (1,500 objects) moved there and is still used where the clock cannot reach. pdf-lib cuts object streams by its own count, so the bytes are the same either way: shown for nine tools on a 320-page ledger by SHA-256, again after the clock pacing for twelve runs on the ledger and a 2,000-page file, and for inspect, Compress's save, Images to PDF, TIFF to PDF, Protect (random bytes seeded for the comparison), unlock, the Watermark scan and the Bookmarks reader, two rounds each. If a later version of either library changes the parts `pace.ts` reaches into, it falls back to the library's own call, paced by count. Redact's rounds and proof use the same pace; the check's own load still waits every 1,500 objects.
- Each phase 2 tool follows one step contract (`toolStep.ts`: a run module exports `BUILT`, its options type and `step`; `toolModules.ts` reads the switches and loads each module on first use). A tool whose `BUILT` is `false` is shown as "soon" and cannot be opened from the toolbar; at revision 8 every phase 2 tool, Redact included, has `BUILT` `true`. Phase 3 follows the same contract: at revision 9 PDF to Excel, PDF to Word, Document check and Compare have `BUILT` `true`, and so has Make searchable (`ocr/ocrRun.ts`); no tool is shown as "soon".

**Shared files.** These files outside PDF Tools are changed by the feature. Parallel branches edit them too, so each change is kept to the lines listed.

| File | Phase | Change | Approval |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `src/pages/AuditTools.tsx` | 1 | inline PDF tab removed; one import and one mount line | the lead's instructions (T-031) |
| `electron/main.cjs` | 1 | one block of about 20 lines (beginning `const fileAccessSession`) so that the folder window can return the user's Desktop, Documents and Downloads folders (section 6) | owner decision, 14 November 2026 |
| `package.json`, `package-lock.json` | 1 | `pdfjs-dist` 5.4.296 and `@cantoo/pdf-lib` 2.11.1, one script | T-031 |
| `src/config/buildMessage.ts`, `src/lib/buildInfoLabel.test.ts` | 2 | start-up label "PDF Tools phase 2", no PR number | owner decision, 14 November 2026 |
| `src/contexts/EngagementContext.tsx` | 2 | `registerEngagementLeaveGuard`; `setCurrentEngagement` and `clearEngagement` ask the registered guards and return whether the change went ahead (about 30 lines) | owner's yes, 15 November 2026 |
| `src/components/layout/Header.tsx` | 2 | Log Out and "View All Engagements" stop when the user chooses to stay (2 lines) | owner's yes, 15 November 2026 |
| `src/pages/Engagements.tsx` | 2 | no "Switched" message when the user chooses to stay (1 line) | added to complete the change; the owner was told and did not ask for it to be removed (15 November 2026) |
| `src/pages/SelectEngagement.tsx` | 2 | choosing an engagement and signing out stop when the user chooses to stay (2 lines) | as for `Engagements.tsx` |
| `scripts/test-pdf-tools-ui.cjs`, `scripts/pdf-tools-electron-entry.cjs` | 1, 2 and 3 | the application test; in phase 2 the entry script gains key events sent from the main process, used only when the test sets `PDF_TOOLS_TEST_KEYS`; in phase 3 the test gains scenarios P3-1 to P3-33, and its `mouseClick` waits until the element has stopped moving (section 9) | — |
| `src/config/buildMessage.ts`, `src/lib/buildInfoLabel.test.ts` | 3 | start-up label "PDF Tools phase 3" | lead ruling, 17 November 2026, under the owner's phase 2 rule that each pull request updates the label |
| `package.json`, `package-lock.json` | 3 | the recognition engine's three packages under `devDependencies` (`@tesseract.js-data/eng` 1.0.0, `tesseract.js` 7.0.0, `tesseract.js-core` 7.0.0) and one script entry, `"sync:ocr-assets": "node scripts/sync-ocr-assets.mjs"`, added by hand; the lockfile is the committed lockfile plus only the entries for these packages (11 packages added, three entries' flags changed, none removed, no version changed), proved with `npx -y npm@11.6.2 ci --no-audit --no-fund` in a scratch copy (1,206 packages added, the normal postinstall run, the lockfile unchanged afterwards). npm's own lockfile output was not used: on the committed lockfile npm 11.6.2 also removes 3 and changes 77 unrelated entries, as it does with no change at all | owner decisions of 17 and 18 November 2026 (D-013); the lockfile method a **lead ruling** of 18 November 2026 |
| `scripts/sync-ocr-assets.mjs` (new), `public/pdf-tools/ocr/` (new) | 3 | the script copies the engine's files and the English data into `public/pdf-tools/ocr/`, as `scripts/sync-pdfjs-assets.mjs` does for pdf.js; it is run by hand and its output is committed: 8 files, 14,777,799 bytes (`worker.min.js`, its licence note, three engine files, `eng/eng.traineddata.gz`, `LICENSE`, `VERSION`) | as for `package.json` |

Not changed by phase 2: `electron/`, `sqlite/`, `src/App.tsx`, `src/pages/AuditTools.tsx`, `src/index.css`, `src/components/ui/`, `src/lib/gstinValidation.ts` (imported, not changed), `package.json` and the lockfile. Before phase 2, the owner had decided on 13 November 2026 that `EngagementContext.tsx` is not edited; his yes of 15 November 2026 covers the change above only.

Not changed by phase 3: `electron/`, `sqlite/`, `src/App.tsx`, `src/pages/`, `src/contexts/`, `src/index.css`, `src/components/ui/`, `vite.config.ts` and the packaging setting `build.files`. Outside the PDF Tools folders, git status at the final pass shows only `src/config/buildMessage.ts`, `src/lib/buildInfoLabel.test.ts`, `scripts/test-pdf-tools-ui.cjs`, `package.json` and `package-lock.json` changed, and `scripts/sync-ocr-assets.mjs` and `public/pdf-tools/ocr/` new (with the untracked `Vera Tools Meeting.docx`, which is not part of this work). The ten package folders copied into the untracked `node_modules` are not part of the change (below).

**Dependencies.** Each is added by hand, as one line in `package.json` and the matching lines in `package-lock.json`, and then checked with `npx -y npm@11.6.2 ci`, which refuses a lockfile that is out of step. `npm install` is not used because it rewrites hundreds of unrelated lockfile lines. The build machine has Node 24.15.0, not the pinned 24.12.0; the pinned npm 11.6.2 was used for every install. Nothing else is upgraded.

- Phase 1: a page-rendering library for thumbnails, PDF to images, the viewer and Redact. `pdfjs-dist` 5.4.296 is declared in `package.json` at that exact version. Its worker and data files are copied into `public/pdf-tools/pdfjs/` (195 files, about 2.8 MB) by `scripts/sync-pdfjs-assets.mjs`, so they load from `file://`.
- Phase 1: a way to open password-protected files and write them out unprotected; `pdf-lib` cannot. A short trial of candidate libraries against test files protected with each common scheme (40-bit and 128-bit RC4, 128-bit and 256-bit AES), running offline in the packaged application, with licences checked:
  - **Chosen (2026-11-13): `@cantoo/pdf-lib` 2.11.1, MIT** (its dependencies `fflate`, `culori`, `node-html-better-parser`, `html-entities` are MIT and `tslib` is 0BSD). It is used to decrypt (`PDFDocument.load(bytes, { password, updateMetadata: false })`, then `save()`; the resulting bytes go to `pdf-lib` 1.17.1 as usual) and, in phase 2, to encrypt (5.18). A wrong password throws `Error('Password incorrect')`. A protected file loaded with no password throws `EncryptedPDFError`, and with `ignoreEncryption: true` it reports `isEncrypted`. Two fixes are needed after loading, both proven in the trial: the trailer `/Info` link is dropped on decrypt, so it is taken from an `ignoreEncryption` load and restored, or title and author are lost; and the old security dictionary, whose `/O` and `/U` entries are password checks, is left behind as an unused object, so it is deleted along with stale `XRef` and `ObjStm` objects. The tests check that no `/Filter /Standard` dictionary remains. Pure JavaScript, one lazy chunk of about 549 kB (237 kB gzip), no wasm or worker.
  - The test files were a 3-page text PDF made with `pdf-lib`, protected with a test password by an open-source PDF toolkit and, separately, by `@cantoo/pdf-lib`. `pdfjs-dist` 5.4.296 refused each file with no password or a wrong password, and opened it with the right one. To pass, the right password had to give bytes that `pdf-lib` 1.17.1 loads without `ignoreEncryption`, with 3 pages, the original text and no `/Encrypt`, and a wrong password had to be refused, with networking blocked (Node with network calls stubbed; Electron with offline emulation and every request logged, all `file://`) and from a Vite production build opened from `file://` with the application's `webPreferences` and its `fs`/`path`/`crypto` externals.
  - Also tried: a WebAssembly build of an open-source PDF toolkit (ISC licence; its core Apache-2.0) passed all four schemes and is the fallback; it needs an empty-module shim for its CommonJS loader under the application's Vite externals, and a wrong password can only be read by redirecting `console.error`. A threaded WebAssembly build of the same toolkit failed all four: it needs `SharedArrayBuffer` and `crossOriginIsolated`, which a `file://` page does not have.
- Phase 2: **no new dependency** (`package.json` and the lockfile are unchanged). Every phase 2 tool uses `pdf-lib` 1.17.1, `pdfjs-dist` 5.4.296 and, for Protect, `@cantoo/pdf-lib` 2.11.1's `encrypt()`, lazily loaded as for unlocking. The TIFF reader and its decoders are written for the module. The GSTIN check character comes from the existing `src/lib/gstinValidation.ts`. `@pdf-lib/fontkit` is not installed, so no TrueType font can be embedded: every text phase 2 writes uses the standard PDF fonts (Latin characters only).
- Phase 3, PDF to Excel, PDF to Word, Document check and Compare: **no new dependency**. They use `pdf-lib` 1.17.1, `pdfjs-dist` 5.4.296 (through the existing renderer), and two packages already in the application: `exceljs` for the workbook and `docx` for the Word package. `diff` is not a direct dependency, so Compare has its own word diff.
- Phase 3, Make searchable: an offline recognition engine and its English data, chosen in the engine trial of 17–18 November 2026 against the build contract's conditions (Apache-2.0 or similar licence, no AGPL engine; runs in a worker; loads from `file://` in the packaged application; nothing cached on disk). Added as three exact versions under `devDependencies`: `tesseract.js` 7.0.0 (Apache-2.0), `tesseract.js-core` 7.0.0 (Apache-2.0) and `@tesseract.js-data/eng` 1.0.0 (the "best, integer" English data from the Tesseract project, Apache-2.0). They are build-time only: the library's own code is bundled by Vite into a part of about 15 KB loaded when first asked for, and its worker, engine and data files are copied into `public/pdf-tools/ocr/` by `scripts/sync-ocr-assets.mjs` and committed, as the pdf.js files are, so nothing is needed from `node_modules` at run time (under `dependencies` the installer would carry about 65 MB more). The lockfile gains 11 packages: the nine development-only ones (`tesseract.js`, `tesseract.js-core`, `@tesseract.js-data/eng`, `bmp-js`, `idb-keyval`, `is-url`, `opencollective-postinstall`, `wasm-feature-detect`, `zlibjs`) and, marked optional, `encoding` 0.1.14 with its own `iconv-lite` 0.7.3, which `npm ci` asks for as the optional peer of `node-fetch` (already locked, used by `tesseract.js`); none of the eleven is inside `app.asar`. How the lockfile was made and proved: the shared-files table above. The "best" floating-point data was refused (it fails with the engine file the worker picks by itself, is four times larger and 2 to 8 times slower), and the "fast" data too (it read the invented PAN-shaped code wrongly on 11 of 12 pages, writing the letter O for zero). No install was run in the repository: the ten package folders of the scratch install (`tesseract.js`, `tesseract.js-core`, `@tesseract.js-data`, `bmp-js`, `idb-keyval`, `is-url`, `opencollective-postinstall`, `wasm-feature-detect`, `zlibjs`, `encoding`; 238 files, about 65 MB) were copied into its untracked `node_modules`, and anyone else gets them with `npm ci`.
- Every dependency's worker and data files must load in the packaged application, which runs from `file://`. This is proven in a packaged build before the phase is called done. For Make searchable the engine trial proved it from inside `app.asar` of a packaged build of `main` at `8dc2165` with name resolution switched off; the packaged build of the phase 3 tree, in the final pass, read the engine's files from inside the package (P3-27 to P3-33 passed there), with no entry of any recognition package under `node_modules` in `app.asar` (section 9).

**Phase 2 parts**, corrected against the working tree:

- Phase 1 files changed: under `src/lib/pdf-tools/` `types.ts`, `resultNames.ts`, `activity.ts`, `workingList.ts`, `inspect.ts` (attachments, carried content, the pace constant), `organise.ts` (Split into several files, pace), `pageCopy.ts` (the two phase 1 fixes of 5.3 and the page mapping every tool shares to re-point bookmarks), `pageNumbers.ts`, `compress.ts` (colour profiles, messages, pace), `imagesToPdf.ts`, `fileNames.ts` (middle cut), `unlock.ts` and their tests; under `src/components/pdf-tools/` `PdfTools.tsx`, `PdfToolsMount.tsx`, `ToolBar.tsx`, `FileList.tsx`, `PagePanel.tsx`, `Thumb.tsx`, `HintButton.tsx`, `runTool.ts`, `toolRules.ts`, `toolIcons.ts`, `toolOptions.ts`, `destinations.ts`, `savePlan.ts`, `intake.ts`, `intakeRunner.ts`, `useFileIntake.ts`, `useWorkingList.ts`, `vaultBytes.ts`, `fileGroups.ts`, `format.ts`, `pdfRenderer.ts`, `renderQueue.ts`, `docOpener.ts` (the memory-leak fix), and in `dialogs/` the frame, every phase 1 tool dialog, the password dialog, the destination dialogs and the vault picker, `carryNotes.ts`, `fieldProps.ts`, `useToolRun.ts` and `usePageNumberPreview.ts`.
- New parts: the step contract (`toolStep.ts`, `toolModules.ts`), the key guard (`dialogKeys.ts`, `useDialogKeys.ts`), the engagement question (`leaveGuard.ts`), the carried-content sentence (`carriedNotice.ts`), the protection notice (`dialogs/protectionNotice.ts`), quiet focus (`quietFocus.ts`), the shared preview cache (`thumbCache.ts`), long names (`MiddleName.tsx`), the order list of Merge and Images to PDF (`dialogs/OrderList.tsx`, `dialogs/fileOrder.ts`), and per tool `viewer/`, `bookmarks/` and `bookmarksRun.ts`, `organise/` and `organiseRun.ts`, `marks/` and `watermarkRun.ts`, `protect/` and `protectRun.ts`, `pagebox/` and `pageBoxRun.ts`, `splitForm.ts` and `splitRun.ts`, `compressFit.ts`, `redact/`; in the library `outline.ts`, `bookmarks.ts`, `pageOrder.ts`, `marks.ts`, `watermark.ts`, `protect.ts`, `pageBox.ts`, `splitPlan.ts`, `compressTarget.ts`, `tiff.ts`, `tiffCodecs.ts`, `matchedText.ts`, `redactPatterns.ts`, `pageText.ts`, `redactFind.ts`, `redactBuild.ts`, `redactCheck.ts`, each with tests and generated test files.

**Phase 3 parts** (working tree of 18 November 2026; all new files have tests beside them, and the test files are generated inside the tests):

- **The shared page-layout reader** (frozen first, so that the Excel and Word lanes could build against it): `src/lib/pdf-tools/layout.ts` (lines, columns from the clear strips between cells, ruled and aligned tables, reading order, two-column text, the under-20-characters rule), `rulingLines.ts` (the lines drawn on a page, read from its content with the page's matrix, one level into forms; thin filled boxes count as lines), `src/components/pdf-tools/convert/pageWordsReader.ts` (words and their boxes from the existing renderer's text and measure, and the page's lines; it gives the window a turn every 128 words), and the invented test files `testConvertPdf.ts` (six fixtures, with each word's box recorded from the same draw calls, so the Excel and Word tests need no pdf.js). On a dense test page of 1,120 words the layout took 21–53 ms. Limits that keep a strange page from holding the window: content over 3,000,000 characters or more than 20,000 line pieces gives no lines; ruled-table finding gives up past 20,000 pieces or 40,000 cells.
- **PDF to Excel:** `excelNumbers.ts` (the number rules), `excelBuild.ts` (the plan, the table references and the consistency check, and the workbook), `convert/excelRun.ts`, `convert/excelForm.ts`, `convert/useExcelPreview.ts`, `convert/ExcelPreview.tsx`, `dialogs/PdfToExcelDialog.tsx`, and **the Excel write worker** `convert/excelWrite.worker.ts`. The plan runs on the window's thread in turns (its longest task 81–133 ms on 500 pages); the packed sheets are copied to the worker (4–12 ms), which writes the workbook with `exceljs` and replies with the bytes or a failure, never a cell; it says it is alive every second. The worker imports `exceljs` by name: the application's build makes a worker one file, and with only the library's lazy import the whole build stopped ("UMD and IIFE output formats are not supported for code-splitting builds"), a defect first seen when the engine trial and the integration lane built the tree; the window's own code still loads `exceljs` only if it must write without a worker. `excelBuild.ts` imports only types from `pace.ts`, so the worker does not carry a second copy of `pdf-lib`.
- **The page-reading engine after the quality rework** (19 and 20 November 2026): the shared `layout.ts` and `rulingLines.ts` grew (true cells, strokes, shades, picture boxes, text runs with their font and colour), and four files are new: `pdfFontLook.ts`, `pdfPictures.ts`, `wordLook.ts` and `convert/textMeasure.ts`. `wordBuild.ts`, `excelBuild.ts`, `convert/pageWordsReader.ts`, `convert/wordRun.ts` and `convert/excelRun.ts` were extended. The interface of `layout.ts`, frozen in the build contract, was unfrozen for the rework. No dependency was added, and no main-process code, IPC or network call. The scan fix of 20 November 2026 changed `rulingLines.ts` (it follows the text rendering mode and marks invisible runs), `layout.ts` (`Word.hidden`, `PageLayout.scan`: the picture under invisible words is left out), `convert/pageWordsReader.ts`, `wordBuild.ts` and `excelBuild.ts` (`pagesFromScan`; in `wordBuild.ts` also the escaping of attribute values and six-digit colours), `convert/wordForm.ts`, `convert/excelForm.ts`, `activity.ts`, and, for Make searchable, `ocrQuality.ts`, `ocr/ocrForm.ts` and `ocr/ocrEngine.ts`; a new test file `convert/scanPages.test.ts`; no dependency, main-process code, IPC or network call.
- **Opening PDF Tools** (lead's change, 20 November 2026): `toolModules.ts` now holds the list of built tools as a plain table (`toolFlags`, all built), and `PdfTools.tsx` no longer loads it. Before, opening the tab waited until every tool's code had been loaded to read each tool's `BUILT` (Redact, the converters, the recognition engine's wrapper, the libraries), so the section showed late, and later with phase 3. The tests still hold each tool's module to `BUILT` true. Measured by the quality lane in scratch builds of the application: the first open of PDF Tools took about 200 ms after the change (about 300 ms before), and the first open of a tool's dialog 60 to 300 ms. A lag of seconds could not be reproduced there; the lane's reading is that what the owner still sees is the development server compiling the section's modules the first time they are used, which a built copy does not do. That reading is not proved.
- **PDF to Word:** `wordBuild.ts`, `convert/wordRun.ts`, `convert/wordForm.ts`, `dialogs/PdfToWordDialog.tsx`. **The paced Word writer:** the `docx` library writes the body in one stretch (2.45 s for 500 pages, measured by the lane), so it writes only the package (styles, settings, relationships, properties) around a marker paragraph, and PDF Tools writes the body's XML itself, part by part and row by row in turns, encoded in slices of 64 K characters; `jszip` then puts the body in place and zips in slices. Measured by the lane after the change: longest task 96–127 ms at 500 pages, 113–226 ms at 1,000 pages. The XML was checked side by side with what `docx` writes. The plan runs in turns too.
- **Document check:** `documentCheck.ts` (one paced read of the file: page sizes, protection, signatures, text classes, properties), `checkReport.ts` (the report writer), `review/documentCheckRun.ts`, `review/documentCheckForm.ts`, `dialogs/DocumentCheckDialog.tsx`.
- **Compare:** **the own word diff** `compareText.ts` (a linear-memory Myers diff written as `pace.ts` generators, so that every step can give the window a turn; step budgets; the coarse path for very long pages; page pairing; the line-end hyphen rule), `compareReport.ts`, `testComparePdf.ts`, `review/compareRun.ts`, `review/compareForm.ts`, `dialogs/CompareDialog.tsx`.
- **Make searchable:** `ocrTypes.ts` (the engine interface, the 300 dpi drawing, the 60 s limit, the time range), `ocrPlan.ts` (which pages; `pageHasDrawing` reads a page's own content for anything but text), `ocrLayer.ts`, `ocrQuality.ts`, `ocrReadable.ts` (the words the engine doubts are left out), `ocr/ocrForm.ts`, `testOcrPdf.ts`; **the OCR engine** `ocr/ocrEngine.ts`, on `tesseract.js` with the trial's settings, one worker, a 30 s start-up limit and an error handler: because the library gives no handle on its worker until it has started, and never answers when it fails, the engine catches the worker as it is made and ends it on a failed start; the per-page limit and Stop end the worker at once and never wait for the library's answer; `ocr/ocrRender.ts` (each page drawn by the existing renderer, whole, as shown, on white, at 300 dpi within the canvas limits, as a run job); `ocr/ocrRun.ts` (the run, `BUILT = true`); `dialogs/OcrDialog.tsx`; `scripts/sync-ocr-assets.mjs`; and a test of the copied files, `ocr/ocrAssets.test.ts`. Producer is set to "ICAI VERA" and the source's Creator kept.
- **The integration:** no preview pulses behind an open dialog (`Thumb.tsx`); `officeFiles.ts` (the row's label, tile letters, extension, tip and the one refusal sentence), the office tile in `Thumb.tsx` and `FileList.tsx`, no viewer for an office row in `PdfTools.tsx`, the Word icon in `dialogs/DestinationFrame.tsx`, the file chip in `dialogs/ToolDialog.tsx`, the refusal in `toolRules.ts` and `runTool.ts`, `protectionRemoved` in `toolStep.ts`, the record keys in `activity.ts`, the names in `resultNames.ts`, the types in `types.ts`, the toolbar in `ToolBar.tsx`, `toolIcons.ts` and `toolModules.ts`.

Left to the builder:

- Component breakdown inside `src/components/pdf-tools/`, state management, and exact wording of messages.
- Whether long operations move to a background worker. Developer choice, still open for the owner to change: opening a password-protected file and Protect run on the main thread in slices (section 11); other operations show progress and can be cancelled.
- How digital signatures and restrictions are detected. As built: a file is treated as signed when its form has the signature flags set or the text `/ByteRange` appears in it; a file "carries restrictions" when it is protected but opens without a password. Form fields, bookmarks, links between pages and attachments are detected when a file is added and on every result; a push button is not counted as a form field.

## 9. Testing

**Unit tests** (`src/lib/pdf-tools/*.test.ts` and `src/components/pdf-tools/**/*.test.ts`, run by `npx vitest run src/lib/pdf-tools src/components/pdf-tools`; on this machine vitest finds the tests only when started from the upper-case path `C:\Users\ASUS\Desktop\audit-assistant-pro-electron`). Test files are generated inside the tests; no binary fixture is committed. Minimum cases for phase 1:

- Page-list parsing: valid lists, spaces, reversed range, zero, beyond page count, non-numeric, empty, duplicates.
- Merge: page count is the sum; order follows input order; images become pages; single-file and empty inputs are refused.
- Split: each mode's page counts and page order; N boundaries; names of parts.
- Delete pages: remaining count and order; delete-all refused.
- Compress: an image is replaced only when smaller; each level no larger than the one above; no-gain reported; page count and text unchanged.
- Page numbers: every format; start number; page range; position on portrait, landscape and rotated pages; existing content still present.
- Images to PDF: one page per image; page size options; undecodable image refused.
- Password: right password opens; wrong password refused; result opens without a password; password absent from the result and from log text.
- Rejections: non-PDF bytes, zero-length file.
- File-name cleaning: each forbidden character, trailing dot and space, missing and upper-case extension, empty name.

Phase 2 adds, beside each module, most written before the code:

- Redaction: for each kind, valid examples found as sure, near-misses as possible or not at all, a seeded corpus of 5,000 ordinary audit lines with no sure match, overlap cases for every precedence rule, an independent check-digit oracle; positions in several fonts, operators, kerning and turned and cropped pages; the rebuild, the text layer and what a rebuilt page keeps; the guarantee on a file that hides one value in 44 places; every recipe of the independent tests B1–B8 and R1–R8 rebuilt as a failing test first, then fixed, with two variations each; from fix waves 5 and 6, each failing first: the readings of a page turn, the unreadable glyph standing for letters of a typed value, the whole-run cover, account keys and covered digit groups, the label rules, the find paced by the clock, the confirmation sentences and the busy-machine retries; the proof by drawing; the matching rule on the testers' written forms; the silence watch; refusals and extra boxes on ordinary documents.
- Bookmarks: reading cases (nesting, every kind of destination, every title encoding, a page outside the page tree, broken counts, loops, 50,000 bookmarks, 200 levels, damaged outlines), each compared with pdf.js; writing; removed and renamed titles absent from the saved bytes; carried by every tool, checked by the text of the page each bookmark opens; the reading pace.
- Organise pages: order, rotation, removal; links, fields and bookmarks follow; the page-by-page check.
- Marks: watermark, stamp, logo, header and footer on pages turned 0/90/180/270 and cropped; opacity; behind and in front; tokens; number styles; refusals.
- Protect: AES-256 `/V 5 /R 6`, `/P -4`, no clear text in the raw file, refused without and with a wrong password by pdf.js, opened by the application's unlock code, non-Latin passwords, the password absent from the result, record and messages; progress in slices.
- Resize or crop pages: geometry; pdf.js render comparisons (0 px off on every page of a mixed bundle, ink outside the content area 0); hit areas of annotations and links; every kind of destination; 500 pages.
- Split into several files: one file per range (gaps, overlaps, a range twice, the 500 limit); at bookmarks (nesting, page order, pages before the first bookmark, duplicates, Hindi and illegal-character titles); each file byte for byte as One range of pages makes it.
- Compress: the levels' bytes unchanged (golden SHA-256 test); colour profiles; Fit to a size (the ladder, the floor, every outcome); messages.
- TIFF: every compression, colour type, layout and orientation pixel for pixel; pdf.js rendering of the PDFs made; hostile and fuzzed files (only refusals with a sentence).
- Page viewer: zoom, fit and cap arithmetic; the cache and cancel rules; Find's matching, highlight geometry against pdf.js on rotated, cropped and offset pages, reading order, the cap and the memory budget; go to page.
- The key guard's decisions; the step contract; the activity allow-list (no value, term, token or password hint reaches an entry); attachments and carried content; the engagement question; the four phase 1 fixes, each seen failing first.

Reviewers also ran mutation checks (a deliberate fault in a scratch copy, which the tests must catch) on each library and screen lane. Totals: full unit suite (`npx vitest run src`) in the final pass on a clean copy, 16 November 2026: 151 files, 3,195 tests, 3,195 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (the run before the commit, after the owner's hand-check changes; 3,194 of 3,194 on the earlier clean copy). Before it, after Redact was switched on, the 23 test files of Redact and the shared tool rules passed 735 of 735. An earlier whole run that day (150 files, 3,016 tests: 3,008 passed, 8 failed on time limits with the machine at about 20 times its capacity, each passing alone) was taken before the last Redact fixes and before the switch-on. Lessons: mutation checks are run only in scratch copies, and `src/` is searched for the word `MUTATION` before verification and before the commit; two time-dependent Compress tests (`compressTarget.test.ts`, which compares the sizes of two saves made in different seconds, and `compressFit.test.ts`'s 60 s limit) failed once under full load and passed when run again; load-sensitive tests now use pinned dates and limits on processor time.

Phase 3 adds, beside each module (all files invented and made with pdf-lib inside the tests; counts as each lane last reported them, run on the lane's own files from the upper-case path while other lanes worked in the same tree):

| Lane | Tests | What they cover |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Shared page-layout reader | 68 (`layout` 46, `rulingLines` 12, `pageWordsReader` 10) | rows, column starts, the 80-column cap, ruled against aligned tables, merged headings, two-line narrations, reading order and two-column text, the under-20-characters rule, on six fixtures (a 3-page bank statement with and without lines, a trial balance with bracketed negatives and Indian grouping, a two-column letter, a merged heading, a rotated page); the same fixtures read from their bytes by pdf.js giving the same tables; lines read back equal to the lines drawn; Stop |
| Document check | 46 (`documentCheck` 24, `checkReport` 8, form 4, run 10) | invented signature dictionaries (with and without a name, covering the whole file or not, outside the form, an empty field), a protected file opened through the unlock code, restrictions, a full-page scan, a scan carrying a page number, mixed page sizes, properties empty, filled and only in XMP, bookmarks, a form field, an attachment; the report's page count, size, fonts, margins and footer, no title or author, never the word "valid"; Stop, a stale list, the record's keys and no fixture word in it |
| Compare | 64 (`compareText` 29, `compareReport` 7, form 10, run 18) | a 12-page draft and signed pair differing on two pages with one page re-wrapped (no difference), an inserted page, a removed page, 12 pages against 13, identical files, a 6,200-word page, a scanned file, a Devanagari word; the line-end hyphen in both versions and both directions; Stop while reading and while comparing |
| PDF to Excel | 228 (`excelNumbers` 154, `excelBuild` 30, `excelRun` 23, form 16, `useExcelPreview` 5) | `parseAmount` as a table of 117 cases plus 17 format and 14 display cases; both sheet modes; the table references (fewer columns, more columns, a different table, one sheet per page); the A1 fallback; amounts in their columns on every fixture; workbooks read back with exceljs (cell types, values, formats, sheet names) and `styles.xml` checked raw; the worker hand-off (reply, fallback, failure, Stop, the silence limit) |
| PDF to Word | 67 (`wordBuild` 41, form 10, run 16) | headings by size, paragraph joining, the table threshold, page breaks, empty and little-text pages; the `.docx` unzipped and `word/document.xml`, `styles.xml` and the properties checked (no media, no colour, no title or author); the two-column letter in reading order; the trial balance as one table equal to the drawn rows; Stop |
| Make searchable (parts A and B, at the engine lane's hand-back) | 88 (`ocrEngine` 19, `ocrRender` 4, `ocrRun` 16, `ocrAssets` 4, form 12, `ocrPlan` 10, `ocrTypes`, `ocrLayer` and `ocrQuality` 23) | the text layer read back with pdf.js on upright, `/Rotate 90`, cropped and 2°-skewed pages and on the trial's own 12 skewed words; the page's content byte for byte; the layer's operators; ruling marks and unprintable characters counted; the page plan on real files read with pdf.js; the engine with every mandatory setting, the start-up limit, a failed start, the per-page limit and Stop against a stand-in worker; the page drawing; the run (plan, layer, record, Stop, a stale list, a file with no page tree); the message, the record and the faint rule; then, in the final lane, a failed page, three failures in a row and every page failing (7 tests red, then green), and Document check's counts held to the plan of the same file |
| Integration | no separate count | `officeFiles.test.ts` (new) and the changed tool-rule, module, step, run, record and label tests, all inside the whole suite below |

At the build lanes' hand-backs their own files held 515 tests (Make searchable part A's 42 included), all passing; at the engine lane's hand-back the Make searchable files held 88. Each lane's last type check was the baseline of 49 diagnostics with none under pdf-tools in its own files, and ESLint exited 0 on its own files. How they were written, said plainly by the lanes: tests were written before the code, except that the shared reader's first draft of `layout.ts` came before its tests (its tests then failed in four places and changed the design twice); some test files were written first but not run to see them fail before the code was written: `checkReport.test.ts` (Document check), `compareText.test.ts` and `compareForm.test.ts` (Compare), `wordForm.test.ts` (PDF to Word), `ocrLayer.test.ts` and `ocrQuality.test.ts` (Make searchable part A; `ocrTypes.ts` was written before its test), and `ocrRender.test.ts` and `ocrRun.test.ts` (part B). The PDF to Excel lane saw every one of its files fail first. Only the shared reader's lane ran deliberate faults in a scratch copy: 34 rules broken one at a time, 33 caught by a test; the one not caught (amounts set aside when they lie across a gap) gives the same table either way on every case built. One Compare timing bound was raised from 50 to 100 ms after it failed once at 50.26 ms with four test files running beside it (it passed three times of three alone). No dialog has a component test, because the repository has no DOM test environment; their logic is tested in the form modules and the dialogs were checked in the real application. Whole unit suite in the final pass (`npx vitest run src`, on base `1df122c` and on the old base alike): 180 files, 3,783 tests, 3,783 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped.

**Application test** (`scripts/test-pdf-tools-ui.cjs`, run with `npm run test:pdf-tools-ui`, which builds first; it starts the real application through `scripts/pdf-tools-electron-entry.cjs` in an isolated temporary profile with synthetic users and data, and drives the screen over the DevTools protocol of the application's browser engine, with keys that menus and window listeners handle sent from the main process). Phase 1's 32 checks: opening PDF Tools in an engagement; adding files from the computer (thumbnails, a protected PDF and its password); Merge; PDF to images; Escape during a long run asks before stopping; Add to Evidence Vault (the record, the stored bytes, and the SHA-256 in the activity entry); Attach to working paper (the same vault file, no second record); Add from Evidence Vault; the PDF Tools activity entries; the password appearing nowhere (database, profile files, console); isolation between two engagements; after a restart, persistence, Split, Delete pages, Compress, Page numbers and Images to PDF, each result read back as bytes and checked; Save to computer marking and its activity entry, and the download fallback; Enter and Cancel during a long run; an engagement change while PDF Tools is closed and during a vault upload; throughout, the renderer's network requests and errors.

Phase 2 adds, on the same synthetic profile: the toolbar's order and the usable and "soon" tools; the page viewer (corner icon, keyboard, pixel size, next and previous, zoom, the page choice in step with the panel, Escape, closing on an engagement change); Bookmarks; Merge with a bookmark per file and with the order changed in the dialog; Split carrying bookmarks; Organise pages by keyboard; Watermark and stamp; header and footer with tokens; Protect (refused without the password and opened with it; the password in no profile file; a protected result refused as input); the key guard from several focus positions; the layout at 1280 × 720; the loading outline and the tool spinner; a click on a page or a row thumbnail opening the viewer; the carried-content sentence, Bookmarks on a large file, the protection wording, the quiet tooltip and long names; TIFF intake, conversion, the HEIC refusal and results pixel for pixel; Split into several files and at bookmarks; Resize or crop pages (results read back from the bytes, the keyboard crop frame, a refusal naming its page); Compress Fit to a size; Find and go to page with keys sent from the main process; the Stop focus after a confirmed Stop; and the engagement question (asked from three routes with the exact sentence, Stay keeps everything, Change engagement clears, nothing unsaved asks nothing; the question is the application's own dialog, so the test answers it as a user does); "Discard your changes?" when Organise pages closes with changes; and the activity record holding no typed text (four checks). A separate check in the real window (62 of 62 on 8 routes) showed that typing works after every answer. Redact has ten checks, RD-1 to RD-8, on two statements the test makes itself (two pages and 16 pages, with the invented PAN `QZXCV0000W` on every page and an invented `example.com` address on page 1; no native save window): the find lists the PAN in 2 places and the address, all ticked, and the button reads "Redact 3 areas", with Balanced and Searchable chosen (RD-1); a typed word that no file holds gives "Not found: 1 typed word." and a marked chip, the areas stay 3 and Redact stays available (RD-2); the confirmation, with the "Not found" line again (RD-3); the Searchable run adds the result with its message and keeps the original (RD-4); the result read with pdf.js has two picture pages, neither value in any page's text nor in the bytes, and the other words still found (RD-5); the Audit Trail record holds the counts and exactly the twelve options, and no value anywhere in the database (RD-6); Stop during a run on the 16-page statement gives "Stopped. Nothing was added." and no result (RD-7); after the application closed, the PAN, the address and the typed word are in no file of the profile and in no console message (RD-8, three checks). The toolbar check now expects Redact usable. The test has 145 checks. On 16 November 2026, on a scratch build of this tree with Redact switched on, the second whole run passed 143 of 143; the first stopped at 51 passed, when a Protect Stop check under a slowed processor ran out of its time limit while a type check ran beside it (that check passed when run alone). In the final pass on a clean copy: 145 of 145 on a scratch build after the owner's two changes of 16 November 2026 (143 of 143 on the clean copy before the two checks for them, RD-9 and RD-10, were added).

`PDF_TOOLS_TEST_APP=<path to the packaged ICAI-VERA.exe> node scripts/test-pdf-tools-ui.cjs` runs the same checks against a packaged build, still on the temporary profile (the download check is skipped there). The test cannot drive the native save and folder windows, Save as ZIP, or dragging files in from the desktop; it replaces the browser's pickers with stubs that keep what is written. It does not observe the main process's own network use; a packaged build checks its update feed at start-up.

Phase 3 adds scenarios P3-1 to P3-33 (`PDF_TOOLS_TEST_ONLY=phase3` runs them alone), on files the test makes itself: the toolbar (18 tools in their groups, Make searchable a usable button in Convert, nothing "soon", three rows at 1280 × 720) (P3-1); the PDF to Excel dialog and its preview (P3-2); a run with all pages on one sheet and its row (P3-3); a ticked office row disabling all 18 tools with the reason, and its tile (P3-4); the workbook saved to the computer and read back with a number cell, and the save recorded (P3-5, P3-6); one sheet per page (P3-7); both workbooks written by the worker (P3-8); PDF to Word end to end, the saved `.docx` unzipped with its heading as Heading 1 (P3-9, P3-10); the two office rows saved together to a folder and as one zip (P3-11); the workbook into the Evidence Vault and the Word document onto a working paper (P3-12, P3-13); Document check on a signed file and on a file opened with its password (P3-14 to P3-16); Compare on a draft and a later version, and the same version twice (P3-17 to P3-19); a scanned file blocking Run in both converters (P3-20); a 500-page statement through both converters, with no window task over the 0.3 s aim and no preview pulsing behind the dialog (P3-21 to P3-24); Stop while each converter reads the 500 pages, asked first and then confirmed (P3-25); nothing from inside a document in any record of the four tools (P3-26). For Make searchable, on a three-page scan the test draws in the window itself (a ruled ledger page for an invented trader with the invented code `QZXCV0000W`, a page of real text, a blank scanned page): offered for one PDF only, and a file with no page to recognise refused with its sentence (P3-27); the result row and the message's exact parts, never a percentage (P3-28); no window task over 300 ms during recognition (P3-29); the result read back with pdf.js: the total, the code and the payee found, the text page as before, the blank page empty, page 1's picture the same bytes (P3-30); the record, exactly the eleven counts (nine until 20 November 2026), and no recognised word in a record, the database or the console (P3-31); scan, then searchable, then PDF to Excel, with the total a number cell (P3-32); Stop inside page 1 (P3-33). In every run the engine read the 6 amounts and the total exactly and said "2 numbers to check on page 1." `PDF_TOOLS_TEST_OCR_TIMING=1` adds a six-page timing run (notes only). The first run of P3-1 to P3-26 in the real application, on 18 November 2026, from a scratch production build with an isolated profile, passed 41 of 41 alone; the first whole run passed 140 of 144 and stopped in phase 3 (the toolbar's four rows failed INT-12, and the scenario assumed engagement A was current when the earlier scenarios had left engagement B, which failed P3-6 and P3-12 and stopped P3-13; the scenario now switches to A first). Defects found in the real application and fixed: the application build failed on the Excel write worker (section 8); the page previews behind an open dialog made long runs slow (below); the toolbar's fourth row (section 4); the preview said "Showing the first 30 of 32 rows and 6 of 6 columns." (it now names only what is cut); the preview pushed the Sheets choice half out of view at 1280 × 720 (now at most 300 px or 28% of the window high); the vault form showed a workbook as "Image · 8.0 KB" (now "Excel workbook" or "Word document"). The whole application test had **178 checks** (145 + 33) at the final pass of 18 November 2026 and has **180** since the quality rework added P3-34 and P3-35 (section 9, "Conversion quality"), and **181** since the scan fix added P3-36: a scan made searchable, then turned into Word and into Excel, the saved files read back (no picture of the scan in either, a blank scanned page, which has no words, keeping its picture) and both scan sentences shown. In the final pass it passed 178 of 178 on base `1df122c` (and 178 of 178 on the old base `8dc2165` before `main` moved), and 169 of 169, with 4 skipped by design, against the packaged build. **It must not be run with TEMP pointed at a long path:** the test's long-named fixture then lies at a path over 260 characters, Chromium's file input leaves it out without a word, and a scenario waits for five files and stops (this cost the final lane two whole runs; with TEMP left alone the same tree gave 178 of 178).

**UX-3 and UX-4: the test's own timing.** UX-4 (Images to PDF: the order arrows by mouse) failed in three of five whole runs of the integration lane, and phase 2's workspace notes had called its cousin UX-3 (Merge order by mouse) sensitive to a busy machine. Neither was the machine, nor a product fault: the test's `mouseClick` read an arrow's place while the dialog was still growing into place (its 200 ms opening animation), so the press landed on the row beside the 28 px button (in the failing step the dialog was 638 × 648 px when read and 672 × 682 px when settled). It is on `main` too: 73 of 75 repeats passed on a build of `main` at `8dc2165` and 70 of 75 on phase 3 before the fix. `mouseClick` now waits, for at most a second, until nothing around the element is still being animated into place and its place has held for two frames (`scripts/test-pdf-tools-ui.cjs`); after that 75 of 75 on phase 3 and 25 of 25 on `main`, and UX-4 passed in every whole run of the final pass. What each check proves is unchanged; one fix covers both, because both use `mouseClick`.

Measured in the real application (the 500-page statement with 15,000 entries, a DevTools trace, processor time of the window's own thread). In the integration lane's sixth run PDF to Excel took 85.4 s and PDF to Word 25.9 s, with longest window tasks of 104.4 ms and 89.6 ms. The cause was not PDF to Excel: a conversion started straight after a long file was added took two to three times longer whichever converter ran first (PDF to Word run first: 80 s), because the page previews in view behind the dialog were still waiting (a run's page reads go first) and each waiting box pulsed, so every turn the run gave the window paid for a frame of them. That was so in phase 2 for every long run. No box pulses behind an open dialog now (`Thumb.tsx`; P3-21 checks it); straight after adding, the same run then took 26.2 s. Final pass, base `1df122c`: PDF to Excel 28.9 s, longest window task 68.4 ms; PDF to Word 19.5 s, 52.2 ms; Stop while reading "Stopped. Nothing was added." 130 ms and 95 ms after Stop was pressed, the question included, with nothing added; from the packaged build 28.1 s and 15.2 s, longest tasks 84.3 ms and 71.8 ms, Stop 130 ms and 88 ms. The workbook (559,740 bytes, 16,000 rows) was written by the worker every time. Make searchable, final pass: a three-page scan with two pages to recognise took 3.4 s (1.9 s the ledger page, 0.6 s the blank one), longest task 62.3 ms, Stop 26 ms after it was confirmed; six clean drawn A4 ledger pages, five recognised, took 36.2 s, 3.3 to 9.5 s a page, longest task 138.4 ms (old base only). In the engine lane's copy: a six-page invented scan 61.2 s (50.6 s from the packaged form), 3.1 to 15.0 s a page, memory peak 595 MB; a 30-page scan 5.7 minutes, 6.2 to 19.1 s a page (mean 10.2), one worker throughout, memory peak 688 MB working set and 231 MB afterwards. No window task reached 0.3 s in any run, by processor time or on the clock.
