T-003 28 of 28 and ESLint exit 0 were recorded on scratch builds earlier on 16 November 2026, before the last Redact fixes. The final pass on a clean copy: unit suite 151 files, 3,195 tests, 3,195 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (the run before the commit, after the owner's hand-check changes; 3,194 of 3,194 on the earlier clean copy); `npm run build` passed (exit 0); ESLint 317 of the 319 changed code files clean; the run exits 1 on one error that is on `main` unchanged (`src/pages/Engagements.tsx:156`), and `src/contexts/EngagementContext.tsx` has five warnings, three of them new (exports beside a component); T-003 28 of 28; `npm run test:native` passed (Electron 40.9.1); packaged build built (exit 0, installer 161.1 MB, not installed); the application test against the packaged application passed 134 of 134, with 4 scenarios (9 checks) skipped by design; `npm run test:fsg` and `npm run test:fsg-ui` 286 of 286, and 100 of 100 on a quiet machine (under heavy load `test:fsg-ui` stopped at its restart step in three runs; the script is `main`'s, unchanged, and passes on `main` on a quiet machine too).

Phase 3 (baselines before it, recorded in the build contract on `8dc2165`: type check 49 diagnostics, none under pdf-tools; whole unit suite 3,195 passed; application test 145 of 145). In the final pass on base `1df122c` (a clean copy byte-identical to the working tree, 1,501 files): type check 49 diagnostics, the same 49 lines as a clean copy of `1df122c`, 0 under pdf-tools (the three development dependencies add none); ESLint on the 90 changed or added code files under `src` and `scripts`, exit 0; `npm run build` passed; `node --test scripts/test-t003-authorization.cjs` 28 of 28; `npm run test:native` passed (Electron 40.9.1); `npm run test:fsg` 331 of 331; `scripts/test-fsg-ui.cjs` 139 of 139 on a re-run: its first run ended after 89 s with exit code -1073740791 and no failed check (117 checks printed as passed), as phase 2's final pass and the engine lane each saw once from a Node harness; it is not a PDF Tools file and the cause was not found; `grep -rn MUTATION src` and a search for scratch paths in `src` and `scripts`: nothing found; the lockfile's `ci` proof was not repeated, because `package.json` and the lockfile are byte-identical to the pair it passed on. Packaged build (`npm run electron:build:win -- --publish never`): built, installer `ICAI-VERA-0.9.1.exe` 181,803,975 bytes (181.8 MB; phase 2: 161.1 MB), **not installed**; inside `app.asar` the recognition files are present twice, 8 files and 14,777,799 bytes each in `dist/pdf-tools/ocr` and `public/pdf-tools/ocr` (the accepted spare copy), and no recognition package under `node_modules`; the application test against `win-unpacked` 169 of 169, 4 skipped by design (the same 9 checks as in phase 2), P3-27 to P3-33 passing with the engine's files read from inside the package. Measured on the old base `8dc2165` only, not repeated on `1df122c`: the UX-4 repeat runs, the six-page A4 timing and the screenshots; none of the 34 files that changed under the branch is a PDF Tools file.

**Conversion quality, tested on three sets** (T-064, 19 and 20 November 2026; the quality lanes' own reports, kept in the git-ignored workspace, not in the repository). The owner's rule is that the converters are judged on their output over many kinds of documents, so each round converted the same three sets and compared every result with its source page. The sets, by kind and count only:

- **The owner's 3 real files.** Never named, quoted or copied into the repository; used on this computer in a scratch folder only.
- **30 invented documents** of the kinds chartered accountants use (statements, ledgers, bank statements, returns, notes, forms, letters, certificates), made by a deterministic script with their truth files. A third (10) was held back and was not looked at while the rules were built.
- **61 public PDFs**, among them annual-report pages of 12 companies and tax, company-law, gazette and banking documents. They were downloaded for testing only and are kept outside the repository. Pages of five reports not used while building were cut as a set never seen (24 pages).

For Word the measure is "words in place": the share of a page's words that land within 6 pt of their place on the page, taken from Word's own printing of the result, with a picture likeness and whether the file kept its page count. For Excel it is whether every number is a number cell, whether the texts equal the truth file (the invented set only) and how many totals tally. The lanes and the lead also looked at many results by eye, side by side with the source pages, printed through Word and Excel on this computer.

| Words in place, mean per set | Before the rework | Round 1 | Round 2 | Round 3 | Files keeping their page count, round 3 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| The owner's 3 files | 0.00 | 0.84 | 0.86 | 0.86 | 3 of 3 |
| 20 invented documents (development) | 0.00 | 0.84 | 0.88 | 0.89 | 20 of 20 |
| 10 invented documents (held back) | 0.00 | 0.81 | 0.87 | 0.93 | 10 of 10 |
| 43 annual-report pages | 0.00 | 0.54 | 0.80 | 0.89 | 43 of 43 |
| 50 public files (4 pages each) | 0.00 | 0.52 | 0.64 | 0.73 | 44 of 50 |
| 24 annual-report pages never seen while building | not measured | not measured | not measured | 0.78 | 20 of 24 |

Excel, corpus: every numeric cell of the truth files stayed a number cell in every round (1.000); texts equal to the truth 0.84 → 0.88 (development) and 0.85 → 0.855 (held back); totals tallied 93 of 121 and 43 of 51 (unchanged by round 3); the totals check is rough, so those counts compare rounds and are not a pass rate.

**These scores are a measure, not a promise.** They count words, not whether a page looks right. They are harsh in places (a paragraph that Word re-wraps counts as misplaced; a page drawn turned scores 0 by construction) and cannot see a missing picture or a wrong colour. The owner judges by eye, and his hand check of the reworked output has not been done. The pages that still differ are in 11b.

**How the rework was tested.** New unit tests: 27 in round 1 (3 files), 39 in round 2 (nine files changed or added), 41 in round 3, and one by the lead for the opening of the tab: the unit suite grew from 180 files and 3,783 tests to 189 files and 3,891. The lanes said plainly that tests came after the code in round 1 (none seen failing first); in round 2, 29 of the 39 were seen failing first (6 pins that held the earlier behaviour by design, 4 not seen failing); in round 3, 24 of 41 (12 guards by design, 5 not seen failing). Existing expectations were changed in a few files, each named in the lane's report (the dialog sentence, a frame becoming a box anchored to the page, a picture's size). Each round was also run through the real application.

**The lane's closing figures of 20 November 2026**, on the frozen tree (before the scan fix; the independent final pass of the same morning on the same tree gave 190 test files, 3,915 tests and 180 of 180, kept here as history, and the final figures are in the next paragraph): unit suite `npx vitest run src` 3,915 tests passed in the Make searchable lane's run of 20 November 2026 (190 test files in the source inventory; the quality lanes' own run before it had 189 files and 3,891 tests); type check 49 diagnostics, none under pdf-tools; ESLint clean on the 101 changed PDF Tools files and scripts; `grep -rn MUTATION src` nothing; the whole application test 180 of 180 (P3-34 and P3-35 convert an invented form with boxes, a turned label and pictures to Word and to Excel in the real window and read the saved files back: one section and one table in the Word file, a merged title, a turned merged label, a number cell and the picture in the workbook). A 500-page statement in the real window: PDF to Excel 33.3 s (longest window task 120 ms), PDF to Word 26.4 s (57 ms); Stop landed after 162 ms and 119 ms and nothing was added. The first build took 28.9 s and 19.5 s; after round 1 the same test gave 47.4 s and 39.9 s and after round 2 50.7 s and 45.2 s, which the round 3 lane showed was largely the machine: round 2's code on a quiet machine on the same day took 34.2 s and 31.1 s. About 23 s of Excel's 31 s of reading is waiting for the page renderer's text, which is not one of the conversion files.

**Final figures of 20 November 2026** (the SCANFIX lane, on the frozen tree after the scan fix; the lead then re-ran the unit suite himself; they replace the lanes' figures above): unit suite 191 test files, 3,938 tests, all passed, none skipped; type check 49, none under PDF Tools; ESLint clean on 106 changed files; application test 181 of 181 (the second run: the first stopped at the retry step of scenario INT-4 on a busy machine, and the re-run passed); 500-page statement: PDF to Excel 33.5 s, PDF to Word 26.1 s. `grep -rn MUTATION src` nothing. History, kept: before the scan fix the independent final pass gave unit suite 190 files, 3,915 tests, application test 180 of 180, and a 500-page statement in 31.9 s (Excel) and 25.8 s (Word) on a quiet machine, and found the fault described below. Tests of the scan fix: the rule's 15 tests (new file `convert/scanPages.test.ts`) were written first, 14 seen failing and the 15th a guard for born-digital pages; 3 tests of the "little read" sentence seen failing first; the engine's notes: the expectation was changed first and seen failing; the escaping of attribute values: 3 tests, 2 seen failing and 1 guard; the tests added in `wordRun`, `excelRun` and `activity` were written after the code and not seen failing. 3,938 against 3,915 is 23 more tests and one more file. Six born-digital files, converted before and after, gave the same Word and Excel entries for entry (12 of 12 files identical but for the creation time inside each); the 8 invented scans now carry no scan picture.

**Make searchable after the owner's trial** (20 November 2026; T-064, D-013, GAP-23). The measure: "found" is the share of the real words that are in the text layer; "right share" is the share of the layer's words that are real; the fourth column is what a text extraction tool (pdftotext, in its default mode) finds. Truth was typed from the picture for the real two-language scan (two pages, 416 real words) and taken from the page's own text for the others (invented documents and public text pages, each drawn as a picture three ways: clean at 300 dpi, an office scan at 200 dpi that is askew and noisy, and a faint noisy photocopy). Before and after, with the same engine and settings:

| Set, by kind | Found | Right share | Found by the extraction tool | Text more than 1.5 times too tall |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Real two-language scan, 2 pages | 92.3% to 94.7% | 51.8% to 94.7% | 47.8% to 91.3% | median size 11.3 and 12.2 pt to 9.4 pt on a page printed at about 10 pt; largest 70 pt to 15 pt |
| Clean pages at 300 dpi, 10 | 95.2% to 95.4% | 97.7% to 98.8% | 94.4% to 95.3% | 195 to 4 of 3,300 words |
| Office scans at 200 dpi, askew, noisy, 10 | 92.2% to 91.9% | 91.9% to 94.3% | 75.5% to 78.0% | 689 to 1 of 3,180 |
| Faint photocopies, 10 | 32.1% to 31.8% | 64.4% to 82.8% | 29.1% to 30.9% | 52 to 1 (text too small: 11 to 38) |
| Two-language public pages, 6 pictures | 88.5% to 86.7% | 32.3% to 79.8% | 80.0% to 82.6% | 25 to 0 |
| First trial's 8 good pictures | 99.0% to 99.3% | 98.8% to 99.0% | 90.0% to 90.0% | |
| First trial's 2 faint photocopies | 82.4% to 83.0% | 81.9% to 88.5% | 82.6% to 82.8% | |

On the real scan the message went from "132 numbers to check" and five faint pages to "40 numbers to check on pages 1, 2, 3, 4 and 5", with parts of all five pages named as left out and no page called faint (the record: 1,116 words, 933 left out). The pages are not faint: half of each is in another script.

**The small losses, honestly.** The rule leaves out some right words with the wrong ones: on the two checked pages of the real scan 4 right words with 375 wrong ones; on 10 clean pages 14 right with 45 wrong (of 3,300 right); on 10 office scans at 200 dpi 32 right with 245 wrong (of 3,220 right). On those office scans the share found falls a little (92.2% to 91.9%) and the numbers found in the layer from 513 to 496 (as the extraction tool reads them, 468 to 493); on the first trial's faint ledger photocopy numbers went from 92 to 84 of 164. Nine short nonsense marks with digits stay on one page of the real scan, because a number is trusted further than a word; they are among the "numbers to check". The extraction tool in its default mode still scrambles the order of a tilted page (it rebuilds reading order from positions; other readers are right): not caused by this change and not fixed by it.

**How it was proven, and what was not.** The figures for the owner's file come from a harness, not from the application's window: it runs the same engine package, engine files, English data and settings, and the product's own modules for the layer, the figures and the message, with the page pictures drawn by another library than the application's. It reproduced his file closely (his layer held 169 and 576 words on two pages, the harness's "before" 171 and 570). The application itself was proven on invented scans only: the application test passed 180 of 180 in 534 s on a scratch build, P3-31 now holding eleven counts. Every rule has a unit test seen failing first except one (the rule for a word that does not stand on its line: the test was written first, but the code was typed before it was run); no mutation testing. Readers checked: the reader inside Google Chrome and Microsoft Edge (through its library), pdf.js (the application's viewer and the unit tests) and pdftotext; Adobe Acrobat Reader was not available. Closing checks: OCR unit tests 147 passed (11 files); whole unit suite 3,915 passed; type check 49 (the baseline), none under PDF Tools; ESLint exit 0 on the 16 changed files; no `MUTATION` in `src`. A page took the same time as before (the engine and its settings are unchanged); the application test's six A4 pages took 36.2 s before and 31.6 s after, longest window task 90 ms. The independent final pass of that day found the fault described next; its fix and the final figures are above and below.

**Scans through the real application, to Word and to Excel** (T-064, 20 November 2026; the independent final pass, then the SCANFIX lane). Eight invented one-page A4 scans (a letter, a balance sheet, a ruled bank statement, a form with boxes, a tax invoice, a faint photocopy of a ledger, a trial balance and a two-column newsletter; drawn and degraded for the test, none from a real document) were added in the real application, made searchable, saved, and turned into Word and into Excel from the result. No crash, no empty output, no timeout; every run closed its dialog and added one result; the application read the same as the harness (found share within 4 points on every file). "Found" is the share of the real words that are in the result; "amounts right" counts the amounts of the truth present exactly. The figures are the final pass's, taken before the fix, which changes which pictures a Word page keeps and not which words are typed; the last column is what the SCANFIX lane saw on the Word page printed by Word after the fix (it kept no new scores).

| Scan, by kind | Real words / amounts | Words found | Right share of the layer | Amounts right by Make searchable (wrong) | Excel: amounts as numbers / as text / missing | Word page after the fix |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Audit letter, 200 dpi, askew | 512 / 0 | 99.2% | 99.8% | - | no amounts; no table, text in column A (right for a letter) | Readable: yes |
| Balance sheet, clean 300 dpi | 257 / 46 | 93.4% | 99.2% | 46 (0) | 46 / 0 / 0, none wrong | Readable: yes |
| Ruled bank statement, 200 dpi, askew | 354 / 62 | 68.1% | 60.4% | 28 (3) | 37 / 2 / 23; 6 number cells not in the document | Readable as far as it was read; about a third of the words are missing or misread |
| Form with boxes, 200 dpi, askew | 343 / 0 | 71.1% | 91.7% | - | no amounts; 2 number cells not in the document | Readable: yes, for labels and values |
| Tax invoice, 200 dpi, askew | 277 / 39 | 96.8% | 97.8% | 34 (2) | 34 / 0 / 5; 3 wrong | Readable: yes, with two wrong amounts as recognised |
| Ledger, faint photocopy | 353 / 40 | 3.7% | 35.1% | 0 | 0 / 0 / 40 | Nearly empty page (13 real words) |
| Trial balance, 200 dpi, askew | 181 / 39 | 98.3% | 98.9% | 38 (0) | 0 / 38 / 1; no table, text in one column | Readable: yes |
| Two-column newsletter, clean 300 dpi | 471 / 0 | 96.0% | 100% | - | no amounts; read as a table of 8 columns | Readable: yes |

**What the scans showed, and what changed.** Before the fix every one of the 8 Word pages showed the doubled print (the scan behind, the recognised text typed on top, never exactly on the picture's own text) and none could be read as a document; the document held exactly the words of the searchable layer, so a count of words found missed it, and it was seen only by looking at the printed page. After the fix the 8 Word pages carry no scan picture and no doubled print, and the result says "N pages are scans: …". PDF to Excel: the balance sheet is usable, the invoice and the bank statement are scattered over 13 to 16 columns and need tidying, the trial balance and the audit letter are one column of text, and the newsletter's two columns are cut into a table of 8 columns (its dialog says the table may need tidying). Make searchable's own message told the truth except for the faint ledger, where it said "The page was recognised. Parts of page 1 could not be read …" (it now says "Little could be read on page 1 …"). A raw scan that has not been made searchable is refused by both converters, "This file has no text to read; it looks scanned. Make it searchable first.", and Run waits. What it costs, plainly: whatever recognition did not read is absent from the Word page, because the scan picture is left out (11b).

**Timings in the application** (a synthetic 320-page, 4.6 MB ledger with an embedded font; from pressing Run to the dialog closing; 1280 × 720; the machine at 100% CPU from other work, so the ratios count, not the seconds). Before: pdf-lib's default pace, and the page panel behind the dialog pulsing hundreds of off-screen preview boxes. After: the pace of section 8 and only boxes near the view pulsing. The bytes made are the same.

| Tool on the ledger | Before | After |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Split, ten ranges of 32 pages | 92.8 s | 21.6 s |
| Split, equal parts of 32 pages | 82.6 s | 7.9 s |
| Merge (the ledger and a 5-page file) | 13.5 s | 4.9 s |
| Delete pages 2–10 | 89.9 s | 16.1 s |
| Organise pages (one page moved) | 181.2 s | 22.8 s |
| Page numbers, every page | 57.6 s | 13.8 s |
| Watermark, every page | 57.7 s | 14.4 s |
| Compress, Balanced (nothing to reduce, so nothing made) | 81.6 s | 5.7 s |

On the same files: the Bookmarks dialog on the ledger, which has no bookmarks, reached "no bookmarks yet" in 3.2–3.4 s instead of 20 s; Protect of the ledger took 21–26 s instead of 172.6 s; the Organise pages grid showed the four visible tiles of six scanned pages at once when the page panel had drawn them (none in 59 s before).

In the final pass of 16 November 2026: Protect, the longest window task was 1.2–1.4 s of processor time in three runs, one stretch inside the library's AES-256 password set-up, with 0.5 s more when the check opens the result; this is over the 0.3 s aim and is a known limit (moving the set-up to a worker is a next task); Stop, Stop during loading ended Merge in 66–75 ms and Organise pages in 260–336 ms of processor time, with "Stopped. Nothing was added." and no row added in 12 of 12 runs.

**User-view review.** On 14–15 November 2026 an independent reviewer, who had not built the tools, used a scratch build of the working tree (taken at 23:51 IST on 14 November) as five kinds of user: an article assistant preparing a tax-audit file, an audit senior working on working papers, a partner sending files to a client or regulator, an office administrator on a small laptop with the keyboard and odd files, and a quality or peer reviewer; plus Redact, which was reachable only as "soon". Real clicks, keys and typing; invented files only (bank statements, a 320-page ledger, scans, phone photos, a signed file, a draft report with comments, a form, damaged files, Hindi and very long names); every result opened in two other PDF readers as well as the application; the machine at 100% CPU. Of 140 questions, 67 were answered well, 37 partly, 24 not answered and 12 wrongly or misleadingly. Fixed since (sections 4 and 5): Compress on scans whose images carry a colour profile (it said "already compact" on a 59 MB scan) and its untrue "a stronger level may still make it smaller", the "1.0 MB → 1.0 MB" display and the level in the result name; Fit to a size; the carried-content sentence; the question before an engagement change; Organise pages' grey tiles on scans; Bookmarks reporting "could not be read" on a large file without bookmarks; a failed thumbnail never retried; the protection wording (a file with restrictions called "password-protected"; results described as protected); the tooltip that contradicted a success; long names losing their end; the Hindi refusal shown as character codes; Protect's long silent steps; small wording rows; Split by several ranges and at bookmarks; go to page in Bookmarks and Find in the viewer; every page to A4; TIFF files. Not changed: the Audit Trail's times, 5½ hours behind IST (application-wide; the owner decided that nothing changes there, section 12); a question when VERA is closed with unsaved results (not made, section 4). The rest are listed as candidates (section 3).

**Manual run and owner hand check.** In `npm run electron:dev` and in a packaged build, with synthetic files: each tool and option, each destination, cancel at each dialog and mid-operation, a protected file, a signed file, a large scanned file, mixed page sizes and rotations. From phase 1, still to be done or confirmed by the owner: Save As and the folder window, cancelling each, and choosing Desktop, Documents and Downloads themselves (accepted; verified with the real window in a probe) as well as a folder inside them, and a folder that must be refused, such as one under AppData; dragging files in from the desktop; a screen-reader pass over the dialogs; the installed package. Results are recorded in the handoff; owner UAT is separate and is not claimed.

For phase 2 (none of it can be done by the automated test, and none has been done yet):

- **Other PDF readers**, on files saved from PDF Tools: open them in Adobe Acrobat Reader, Microsoft Edge and Google Chrome. A protected file asks for its password before showing anything (also in Explorer's preview pane); a wrong password is refused; the right one shows all pages, text, form values, bookmarks (including a Hindi title), links, attachments, page labels and the language; printing and copying are allowed; the document's security properties say 256-bit AES. Repeat with a Hindi password, an accented one and one with a trailing space, and note which readers refuse them (the dialog warns that some do). How the signature panel shows a protected signed file, and how a protected PDF/A file is reported. The Evidence Vault copy, downloaded again, still asks for its password.
- **Bookmarks, Watermark, Page numbers, Organise pages, Resize or crop pages and Split** in a second reader: the bookmark tree after Merge, Split (each file, and at bookmarks), Delete pages, Organise pages and the Bookmarks tool; the watermark's transparency in front and behind; header, footer, stamp and logo on a landscape and a turned page; a resized bundle and a cropped page (and that the trimmed parts are not shown); print one page of each.
- **TIFF** from the firm's own scanner and a portal; **Compress Fit to a size** on a real scan, read at 100%.
- **Redact** (switched on at revision 8): a realistic anonymised document with PAN, Aadhaar, GSTIN, mobile and bank details, a stamp and a signature box; check every proposal, draw a box on a scanned page, run both output forms at 200 dpi, and open both in a second reader: search for each redacted value, copy a paragraph, zoom to 400% into the boxes' edges (no sliver of a character), check the file name, the document properties and the attachments. Also try: a name that runs over a page turn; a Hindi name typed as one of your own words; a bank statement whose account number is written with and without leading zeros; and a typed word the file does not hold (the line "Not found: 1 typed word.").
- **Keys**: with a tool dialog open and with the viewer open, press Ctrl+Y, Ctrl+D, Ctrl+B, Ctrl+/, Alt+Left and Alt+Right: nothing behind it should change; Ctrl+F in the viewer opens Find.
- **The page viewer** on this machine's own 125% display: sharpness from 100% to 400% on a real scan and a long ledger, and memory in Task Manager while paging through 50 pages.
- **The engagement question** from the header, the Engagements page and the selector, with results not saved.
- A screen-reader pass over the new dialogs, the viewer and the toolbar; the dark theme.

For phase 3 (none of it done yet):

- **PDF to Excel** on a real bank statement and a real trial balance (anonymised): open the workbook in Excel; check that amounts add up as numbers, that Indian grouping, brackets and Dr or Cr show as on the page, that dates, account numbers and leading zeros stay as typed, and that a statement over several pages keeps its columns on one sheet; try "One sheet per page".
- **PDF to Word** on a letter, a report with headings and a ruled table, a form with boxes and a page with a picture: open each in Word with its PDF beside it and compare page by page: where text and tables stand, merged cells and borders, pictures, fonts, bold, the rupee sign, tick boxes, and the page count. PDF to Excel the same: merged cells, widths, number cells, a statement over several pages.
- **Document check** on a signed file: the signer, date, reason and place as stated, and the note to verify the signature in Adobe Acrobat Reader, which is then done there; on a file opened with its password, and on a scan.
- **Compare** on a draft and a signed set of financial statements: open the report and check a changed amount, an added sentence, a page inserted in the later version, and a re-wrapped paragraph that is no change.
- **Make searchable** on a real scan from the firm's scanner and a phone photo: search the result in Adobe Acrobat Reader and in VERA's Find, select and copy a line, check every number the message asks to be checked, then use the result in PDF to Excel and Redact.
- The toolbar at 1280 × 720 on this machine's display, and an office row (its tile, its tip, and that no tool can use it).
