## 11. Known limits (phase 1)

These are limits found while building phase 1. "Not yet verified" means no test or manual check has covered it.

**Phase 1 defects found and fixed during phase 2 (T-052).** They are on `main` since PR #111 and are corrected only by the phase 2 branch (owner decision, 14 November 2026: no separate patch to `main`; the phase 2 pull request states them):

- Page numbers were misplaced (measured 86 pt; 12–333 pt in a reviewer's cases) on pages whose own drawing leaves a graphics state open or a transform in place (5.6).
- Every file opened for drawing pages stayed in memory, about 2.8 MB per opening, until PDF Tools was closed (a pdf.js document was never released after a race in `docOpener.ts`). Fixed and tested: 6 of 6 documents alive before the fix, 0 after; memory flat over 50 viewer open-and-close cycles.
- After Split or Delete pages, a popup on a kept page could keep the text of a left-out comment, and replies kept links to removed comments, because stored null entries were not seen (5.3).
- After Split or Delete pages, a link that went by name to a named destination on a left-out page was kept (5.3).

Some limits below are changed by phase 2: Merge can add and carry bookmarks (5.2); Split and Delete pages carry bookmarks (5.3); the tag "May hold left-out pages' images" reads "May contain traces of removed pages"; files can be reordered inside the Merge dialog; the question before an engagement change (section 4). Phase 2's own limits are in 11a.

### Password-protected files

- Opening a password-protected file runs on the main thread, so the screen is busy while it opens. An 83 MB AES-256 scan took about 18.5 s; typical files take under 2 s. A background worker would remove the wait and is a candidate, left open for the owner. It was not done in phase 1 because of the risk of bundling a worker that must load from `file://`.
- Not yet verified: protected files with no `/ID` entry, and non-Latin passwords on files made by other programs that use the older protection (revision 4 or lower). Linearised protected files are covered in the repository tests only by a hand-built equivalent, not by a real file.
- A linearised file protected with a certificate (public-key) handler may be reported as not protected.
- Opening a file that has only an owner password removes its restrictions in the copy (section 5.1).

### Tools

- Merge does not carry form fields, links between pages or attachments. The merge dialog says so when a chosen file has them. Merge also keeps duplicate hidden page dictionaries (size only) and carries signature marks without a form (the result is marked). Split and Delete pages carry form fields of kept pages but not links by name or signature fields as fields (section 5.3). They are not a redaction tool: fonts shared with kept pages and the form's default resources are copied whole, extra resources are kept when a kept page's text, a string or inline image data contains a resource name, a page whose key holds a `#` that the library cannot read exactly keeps everything, and unknown keys in a layer configuration are copied as they are. In these cases, where the code can tell, the result is marked "May contain traces of removed pages".
- Stop works only between steps for Merge, Page numbers and Images to PDF, and between parts or pages for Split and Delete pages. Reading and writing a very large file are single steps that a Stop waits for; the stop then takes effect and nothing is added (section 5.1).
- Page numbers and Compress keep content from an earlier saved revision of the source that is no longer shown (a PDF can hold older versions of its pages after "incremental" saves). Merge, Split and Delete pages do not.
- Names written with lower-case `#xx` escapes can be re-encoded by the PDF library when it saves, so a page may lose that resource and draw differently. This is rare, and it applies to every tool; Split and Delete pages flag it, Organise pages refuses, the others do not.
- Results were compared with the sources in pdf.js only (the reader PDF Tools itself uses), not in Adobe Acrobat Reader or other readers.
- If a file is such that every page has to be kept whole (the "May contain traces of removed pages" case on every page), Split into Equal parts is as large and as slow as it was before per-page resources were added, and the warning is shown.
- An invisible form field whose only widget is on no page is not carried by Split or Delete pages.
- If the engagement is changed while the folder window is open and a folder is then chosen, the files are still written (the user's own save, started in the engagement it was begun in) but nothing is marked or shown.
- "Signature no longer valid" also appears on a part of a split file that holds no signature. The mark is deliberately cautious.
- Compress: the XMP metadata producer is not updated. Images drawn larger than the page lose more than the nominal dpi. Grey scans without a profile are stored as RGB, so a grey scan can come out slightly larger. Images above 64 megapixels are not recompressed. Compressing a signed file invalidates its signature; the screen warns before the run.
- A file that merely contains the text of a signature byte range (`/ByteRange`) is flagged "Signed". This is a cautious false positive: it adds a warning and blocks nothing.
- Images to PDF is slow for many PNG files (one test run with 200 PNG images of 600 × 800 pixels took 22 s), and the screen shows a busy bar rather than a count. The same image added twice is stored twice in the result. HEIC images are refused (5.7).
- PDF to images: the size shown before the run is an estimate and a warning only. The 300 MB working-list limit is enforced on the real bytes during the run, and a run that would exceed it stops and adds nothing. Very large pages are drawn at a reduced dpi, and the screen says so. Out-of-memory and renderer-restart failures have their own messages; these are proven by unit tests, or by a provoked restart, not by a natural failure.
- The working list holds at most 300 MB and warns at 150 MB (section 5.14).
- The largest sizes have not been run by anyone: a 300 MB list, a 300 MB merge, a 500-page PDF to images at 300 dpi, 200 images in one Images to PDF, and a 150 MB upload to the vault. Several steps copy the bytes more than once, so the figures are design limits, not measured ones.

### Screen and records

- When the engagement changes while the PDF Tools screen is closed, the working list is cleared by a watcher within about one second, not instantly. Until then the files stay in memory but are not shown. (Phase 2's change to `src/contexts/EngagementContext.tsx` adds the question of section 4; it does not make the clearing immediate.)
- A message naming a file (for example "result made", "not added") can stay on screen for a few seconds after an engagement switch. It is the same user and nothing can be done with it.
- The "In Evidence Vault" mark can be out of date until the next destination dialog is opened, if the record is deleted in the Evidence Vault screen. Attaching rechecks the record (section 6).
- There is no warning when the application is closed while results are "Not saved". A warning needs a change to the main process (`electron/main.cjs`) that interacts with the automatic update's restart; it was not made in phase 2 either.
- Opening PDF Tools loads up to 500 activity-log rows of the shared hook (the hook's own limit), although only its write function is used. The shared upload hook shows its own "File uploaded successfully" message for each file, without a file name, even after an engagement switch.
- The Evidence Vault picker list is not virtualised, so a vault with hundreds of files may be slow to scroll.
- Both PDF libraries are packed whole into the installer, although the screen's code already holds what it uses, and `public/pdf-tools` is packed twice. This adds to the installer size only.

### Not checked by the automated test

- Native save and folder dialogs cannot be driven by the automated application test, which replaces them with stand-ins, and are checked by hand, as are dragging files in from the desktop and a screen-reader pass. This is not theoretical: on 14 November 2026 the owner's hand check found that saving to a folder failed with "A save window is already open", after automated verification had passed (cause and fix: section 6). Choosing Desktop, Documents and Downloads themselves is accepted and was verified with the real folder window in a throw-away probe; the same through the application screen, and the single-file Save As window into those folders, are not yet verified.
- Installed package: a Windows package was built in a scratch copy in phase 1 and the application test run against its unpacked build passed 31 checks with 1 skipped (the download fallback, which needs the test's entry script). The package was not installed and not published, and the installer was not run.
- Network use by the main process, and by a packaged application at start-up (it checks its update feed), is not observed by the application test.
- Rendering of JPEG 2000, JBIG2, CMYK and CJK-font PDFs from `file://` with a real file; dark theme; Hindi or other non-Latin file names end to end; a file whose full path is longer than 260 characters (in a review such a file was not added and nothing was said; whether the browser's file input or PDF Tools drops it needs a check with the real Windows file window).

## 11a. Known limits (phase 2)

### Redact

Redact is switched on at revision 8. These limits describe the tool as built after fix waves 5 and 6.

- No separate independent test ran after fix waves 5 and 6: on the owner's instruction of 16 November 2026 Redact was switched on and checked with the whole module in one final pass (5.9). Tests 1 to 5 found, in order, 8 survivors, 8 survivors, 8 blocking findings, 14 blocking findings and 4 blocking items; all were fixed.
- Test 5's planned new cases on unusual PDF structures against the newest protections (text positions proved against the page's pixels; the rule for when a page may be copied unchanged) were not built. Those protections rest on the 342 earlier cases run again (324 held, 7 refused safely, 11 left the value, all 11 stated limits), on the build lanes' own tests and on the ordinary-document tests.
- The limits the dialog itself states stand (5.9, "What it cannot see"): text inside pictures, barcodes, QR codes and scanned pages; letters drawn as shapes; a stamp set to print but not to show on screen; a value split between two table cells or two columns; a word broken at a line end without a hyphen; text in a font that shows one letter and gives another when copied. The 11 cases that left a value in test 5 were all of these kinds.
- Not findable at all: text drawn as pictures or outlines (scans, outlined text, a Type 3 glyph that draws the value as outlines only); text whose font has no usable character map, or one whose map gives other plausible letters with no glyph names to compare; characters written out of reading order; a value split by other text in reading order (two columns). Encoded copies of a value (base64, reversed text) in links and comments are not looked for. Only boxes the user draws remove such text.
- A value is found the first time only in a form the patterns know; some forms are found only when the same value is also written plainly elsewhere (a code wrapped over two lines, a dotted code, an email with a space before `@`, a percent-encoded value). A typed name glued to the word before it is not found (a name broken by a hyphen at a line end is found). Look-alike letters from another alphabet and Devanagari digits are not read as the value. Old-format voter IDs and driving licences, a demat client ID written apart from its DP ID, and a BSR code and challan serial with words between them are not found. The UPI handle list is a fixed list: a new bank handle is "possible", not missed.
- Boxes may cover more than the value: up to a word or two on letter-spaced lines and in fonts far from Arial; a box that cannot be closed is widened to the whole line (in one all-kinds test file, 216 of 707 boxes); a watermark pattern tiled over a page is covered tile by tile; text or a drawing that cannot be read on a rebuilt page is covered whole (bullets in a symbol font with no character map are boxed). Two drawings compared at the run's resolution join a mark smaller than one 6 px cell next to a box to it. There is no shrink control: untick and draw instead.
- In the searchable form, every word next to a redacted value leaves the text layer (the box padding and the word padding together exceed an ordinary space): the text stays visible in the picture but cannot be searched there. Hindi, the rupee sign and other characters outside Windows Latin-1 are left out of the text layer of redacted pages; the note names Hindi only where the redacted pages have Hindi, and otherwise speaks of "some scripts and symbols".
- A redacted page becomes a picture at 150, 200 or 300 dpi: softer than the original when zoomed past that, and larger. Comments, links and form fields on a redacted page become part of its picture: links there stop working and fields there can no longer be filled in. Pages copied unchanged lose attachments in comments, media, scripts, page actions, private application data, XMP metadata and thumbnails; the document loses its properties, XMP, attachments, scripts, named destinations, page labels, tags and article threads. Information inside images on unchanged pages (for example a photo's camera data) is not removed.
- A signed report whose signature dictionary or certificate holds a typed name asks "Flatten this page?" on every run; answering yes is safe.
- A 10-digit mobile number written with no prefix, label or grouping, an unlabelled IP address, and a name or address without a label are not proposed; type them in. A name or address after a label is only ever proposed unticked: the tool cannot tell a person from a firm.
- Time: the proof draws each rebuilt page twice more and reads every page once more; a 10-page statement with a PAN on every page took 7.0–7.7 s in Node and 9–11 s in the application at revision 6 (the application reopens each copy of the file for every page; a second renderer document would save about 0.5 s on 20 pages, section 3). At revision 5, before the fix waves, a 200-page statement took 89.6 s to search and 314 s to redact on a loaded machine, giving 41.1 MB, with the renderer at 873 MB. A test file of 28 pages with 707 areas took 209 s as Searchable and 41 s as Picture only in Node.
- A value split by a page turn is not joined: when more than four lines of other text stand between its halves; when a half is longer than about 64 characters or three lines and lines must be left out to join it; when the value's own line is taken for a row that the next page repeats; or when the lines to leave out are not next to the turn in drawing order (columns are the only reading by position). A PAN or an account number that stands nowhere whole, only across a page turn, is not found: the rule for a value with no whole mention is for typed words only.
- A typed value where letters cannot be read: one unreadable glyph stands for at most three letters inside one word; a space the text reader puts inside a word is not bridged; a typed value with under a third of its letters readable, or in a script outside the 16 the code lists, gets no place of its own. The unreadable text of its script is then covered whole on the pages being redacted, which blacks out more than was asked (on the test letter 10.4% of the page against 2.7% asked). Devanagari digits are not read as a number.
- Account and card numbers: where the end of a number is uncertain the list shows the shorter number (9 to 16 digits) and the digits after it are covered, so two account numbers that share those first digits appear as one value and are covered together. The covered area also takes a short figure written right after the number (a count before "EMIs", a footnote mark, the figure that starts the next line: 2 places, one digit each, on 140 test documents). A date joined to an account number by a hyphen right after its label is not read as a labelled account.
- Labels not read: the second line of a name wrapped in its table cell under a column heading (on one test document 286 px of two surnames stayed visible under the DPDP profile); wrapped column headings other than "Date of / Birth"; an account column with no IFSC beside it under a two-row heading; an addressee after a designation line when the name has no title; a name after a label in an Indian script; a "Name" heading wrapped in its cell; tables whose empty cells shift the cell position; the letter-spaced label "A A D H A A R". Text of a link address can be offered as a possible name.
- Slanted text: boxes are upright rectangles, so a slanted watermark over a page is covered whole when a chosen value stands at a slant in it (on the one-page test statement about 61% of the page went black against 40% asked). Three ordinary test documents get more black than the values need (11.1% against 2.7%, 9.7% against 2.7%, 9.9% against 5.6% of a page); the cause was not traced.
- Window holds on a 500-page statement, measured in test 5 and not paced since: four later steps hold the window 0.4–0.8 s each ("Checking" up to 0.79 s, "Putting together" up to 0.65 s, "Drawing" up to 0.59 s, saving up to 0.37 s). Two review actions still search every page at once and can hold the window on long files: "Redact this text everywhere" and ticking a held group. Placing every value on a dense register page takes several seconds, in turns (slow, not a hold). The final pass's own figures are in 5.9 ("Switch-on").
- Found in the final pass of 16 November 2026 and left as they are: on the two-page salary register under Balanced, drawing the review list holds the window about 0.6 s; on a 500-page statement "Checking…" still holds up to 0.71 s after its fix (one 256 KB reading is itself a 0.35–0.5 s stretch; a smaller reading is a next task); typing a full name does not cover the surname standing alone elsewhere ("Dear Shri …,"), so the surname has to be typed too; on a page labelled only in other Indian scripts the mobile and account number are not ticked by the find and have to be typed or boxed; on one test document a name that was not chosen, beside a widened box, stayed visible in the picture but lost its searchable text (cause not traced).
- The size promised before the run is about four times too small on bank statements (4.2 times in test 5).
- A busy machine: the find and the run try a read or a drawing again twice and then stop with a sentence that names the busy computer; nothing is added. On the build of test 5, under heavy load, long files of 150 to 500 pages were refused in 15 of 15 tries; the retries and a time limit that counts silence were added after that and were not measured under the same load.
- Small points from test 5, not changed: a blank page in a file invites boxes and the run then refuses; "about 0 B" is shown under More options on such a file; "was also made a picture, to be safe" can appear on a file whose font hides its letters, where the value may still stay (a stated limit).
- A drawn box can be placed with the keyboard before it is added, but an added box cannot yet be moved with the arrows. The Redact dialog keeps its own frame; it uses the shared key guard, the Stop focus rule and the lost-Escape handling, but after Escape during its Stop question it stays open where the shared frame closes.
- One file per run; no batch (an owner decision, section 3). The results were seen in pdf.js and in the testers' own readers only until the owner's second-reader check.

### Bookmarks

- A bookmark whose page is left out by Split, Delete pages or Organise pages is dropped even when bookmarks under it are kept (they move up a level), so a chapter heading can disappear while its sections remain (lead ruling, 5.10).
- Bookmarks that open other files, launch something or run a script are not kept by any tool that rewrites bookmarks; nor are links from a bookmark to a structure element.
- A file with more than 10,000 bookmarks or deeper than 32 levels cannot be edited with the Bookmarks tool, and the other tools carry none of its bookmarks. The 10,000 and 32 limits were chosen, not measured against real documents.
- Saving 500 pages with 2,000 bookmarks took 13.6–17.1 s on the window thread on a loaded machine, with Stop between the save and the check only. The reading worker carries its own copy of pdf-lib (a 526 KB part of the build). Titles are cleaned when read, so a rewrite also tidies the file's own titles (spaces collapsed).
- A merge whose sources together hold more than 10,000 bookmarks keeps the per-file bookmarks and leaves out whole sources that do not fit, counted in the note.

### Page viewer and Find

- No text selection or copy, and no side-by-side comparison; very large zoom on a large page is drawn at reduced sharpness (16 megapixel cap).
- The first sharp drawing of a 300 dpi scan page takes 2.3–3.0 s (the enlarged thumbnail is shown meanwhile). After the page renderer stops answering, pages come back after about 31–35 s; closing and reopening does not speed this up. All drawing (viewer, thumbnails, tool runs) shares one worker.
- A page that pdf.js draws only in part gets no note. "Blank page" also appears on pages that are blank on purpose.
- Opened from Organise pages, the viewer shows the page as it is in the file, not with the planned rotation, and numbers pages in file order. The viewer's own buttons still say "Turn the view left/right" (it turns the view only).
- After a control turns itself off, its tooltip shows the reason again only once the pointer has left it (the reason is always available to screen readers).
- Find: Hindi that a file stores in the order it is drawn (some PDF writers put the vowel sign ि before its consonant, or store some vowel signs with no character at all) is only partly found; files that store Hindi in reading order match fully. Text is searched in the order the file stores it, so a phrase drawn in pieces out of reading order, or across columns, may not be found. Inside a piece of text, character positions are estimated from a similar system font, so in a very different font a highlight can be a little off inside a long line (the line's extent is exact); vertical writing highlights the whole piece. Scans have no text until OCR (phase 3). Reading a 320-page ledger took 10–11.4 s at 100% CPU, to be re-timed on an office laptop. Up to about 24 MB of page text is kept while the box is open.

### Organise pages

- Moving a page in a 300-page file took about 85–110 ms per key press on a loaded machine; dragging works within the 200 tiles shown.
- "· was N" appears on every tile whose number changed, so removing page 1 marks every later tile.
- The activity log cuts the summary at 120 characters, which can cut the library's sentence about what was not kept; the result's record and the `notCarried` metadata keep it whole.
- The page check covers page content and the forms it draws, not patterns, Type 3 glyphs or comment appearances. A name holding a real `#` followed by a lower-case hex pair in one of those places can lose its image when the file also has a genuine lower-case escape.
- A run that both moves and rotates saves twice (about 1.6 s more on 300 pages).

### Watermark, stamp, logo, and Page numbers with header and footer

- Text uses the standard PDF fonts: Latin characters only (Windows Latin-1); Hindi and ₹ are refused with a sentence (section 3, candidates). A watermark behind a scanned page cannot be seen. A mark in front is painted under form fields and comments, which may cover part of it.
- "May be hidden behind" is an estimate from the page's own drawing (clip paths, shadings, fills and masked pictures are read; overlapping covers are added up).
- A stamp, logo, header or footer that fits the previewed page but not a later chosen page is refused only at Run, naming the page.
- The preview takes about 0.4–0.7 s after a change on a loaded machine.
- "Letter height" in mm is Helvetica's capital height: Times and Courier come out smaller than the mm typed.
- On `/UserUnit` pages, sizes and margins are real points; phase 1 page numbers on such pages move (deliberate). Pages whose drawing is damaged or uses filters the library cannot read with certainty may be misplaced; they are flagged. Two filters (`/A85`, `/LZW`) are handled by reasoning only, without a test file.
- CMYK JPEG logos are redrawn by the browser's decoder; this was reasoned, not run with a CMYK file in the application.
- Typing ₹, or any character outside the loaded font subset, into a field can make the application request a font from Google Fonts (section 12).

### Protect with password

- It runs on the screen's main thread in slices: a 52 MB scan took 35.7 s in all on a loaded machine, 26 MB took 18–25 s, and a 320-page text ledger 21–26 s. Files over 150 MB are refused. A forgotten password cannot be recovered. A protected result cannot be used as input to another tool.
- The check reads the source and the result with the same library's parser, so a misreading of the source by that parser would be carried into the copy unnoticed (an independent parser found none on 8 files).
- Content that only earlier saved revisions hold is kept, encrypted, and comes back after unlocking.
- The file's structure is visible without the password (5.18). A signed file's signature value is encrypted, which departs from the standard. A PDF/A file is no longer archival once protected.
- While the dialog is open the password is in the screen's state and in the field's value attribute; "Show" turns the fields into plain text for the operating system's input services. A copy of the last full value typed may stay in the browser engine's own memory, where JavaScript cannot reach or erase it (seen once in a heap snapshot, as for any typed text). Stop during the unlocking part of the check is not pinned by a test.
- Non-English passwords: readers differ (in the trial, one reader refused passwords over 127 bytes and another refused an accented letter); the dialog warns; Adobe Acrobat Reader, Microsoft Edge and Google Chrome are still to be tried.

### Resize or crop pages

- Trimming hides, it does not remove (lead ruling, said in the dialog and the result message): VERA's own viewer no longer shows or extracts the trimmed text; other programs may.
- Structure (accessibility) layout boxes are not moved. A page whose drawing closes more than it opens, or cannot be read, is resized safely (nothing escapes the new layout) but flagged "Check page N", because readers differ on such pages. Pages with viewports or measuring scales are flagged.
- One crop for all chosen pages (with the even-page swap). There is no large view of the crop frame (the preview is about 300 px high so that the dialog fits 1280 × 720); the boxes give exact values.
- A field's text size is scaled on the widget, which strict readers may ignore for fields with several widgets. The page sizes for the counts before Run are read by pdf.js; if that read fails, the dialog counts nothing and the run still checks every page. The whole file is saved again by pdf-lib (as every tool does); pages already the size keep their dictionary and content byte for byte, not the file.

### Split into several files

- At bookmarks uses the main bookmarks only: a file with one main bookmark and its chapters under it gives one file (choosing the level is a candidate, section 3).
- The files of a split into ranges or at bookmarks are grouped in the list as "(first name) and N more" (parts of Equal parts keep "(name) — N parts").
- Each file holds the fonts its pages use; a ledger with an embedded font split into ten files takes about twice its own size in all.

### Compress, Fit to a size

- A search is four passes over the images (plus one quick re-save), each about as long as one level: about 40–60 s for a 20-page 300 dpi colour scan on a loaded machine, and about 12 minutes for a 300-page, 158 MB scan (one Balanced pass alone: 4.6 minutes). Every pass can be stopped.
- The ladder's steps are about 10–30% apart (wider at the top), so a result is usually within about a quarter under the size asked; a generous size on a 300–400 dpi scan can come out well under it.
- The search holds the parsed document, its first re-save and at most two results: about three to four times the file.
- A grey image with its own profile is written as plain RGB when the browser's encoder writes three components (it always does), so the grey profile is not applied. CMYK, Lab, palette, Separation, DeviceN and masked images are not reduced by any level. Not run on a real grey-profiled or CalRGB scan from a scanner.
- There is no size estimate before the run. Keeping or removing a result that does not fit is the result row and its message, not a question in the dialog.
- The level's or size's part of a long default name is kept by cutting the name's stem at its end, not in the middle as other default names are cut (`compressFit.ts`).

### TIFF

- A TIFF whose strips together claim more bytes than the file holds (corrupt byte counts) is refused as damaged; a file holds at most 4,000,000 strips or tiles and 500,000 directory entries, and directories that overlap or loop end the file as damaged.
- Formats and sizes outside 5.7 and 5.14 are refused with a sentence; old-style JPEG, common in some old fax archives, is refused. A TIFF whose strips overstate their byte counts past the end of the file is read when the data itself is whole.
- Fax pages are stored as decoded 1-bit pictures: on the synthetic text page that is 0.55 times the size of the Group 4 data, on a noisy page 0.77 times; on real scans Group 4 may be smaller (size only).
- pdf.js 5.4.296 draws the last row of a 1-bit picture only in part (up to 7 pixels short), so each converted 1-bit page is stored with one more white row just below the page's bottom edge, which the page box cuts off. Scanned PDFs from elsewhere still show this pdf.js limit in the application, invisible in practice.
- Not tried with a real scanner or portal TIFF, nor in other readers.

### All phase 2 tools

- A deliberately built PDF can hold the window for some seconds while it is read, because pdf-lib's own reading is not split finer than its waits (on a loaded machine an 8 MB file with a stream that never ends took 9 s, and 75,000 tiny objects 39 s); nothing leaks and nothing is lost. Reading added files in a background worker would remove this (section 3).
- Merge and Organise pages (moving pages) hold the window about 0.6 s inside pdf-lib's writer on a 320-page file; it cannot be sliced without replacing the writer.
- Images to PDF holds the window about 0.3 s per picture while pdf-lib turns it into the PDF's picture (section 3).
- A vault file over the size limit is read before it is refused; refusing it earlier needs a size check in the main process.
- Stop lands at the end of the step, not during the read, in Compress, intake, Images to PDF, TIFF, Protect and unlock, and in Organise pages' final check.
- Bookmarks, Watermark, Page numbers with header and footer, Protect with password and Resize or crop pages re-save the source as it is, so, like Page numbers and Compress in phase 1, they keep content of earlier saved revisions; Redact, Organise pages, Split, Delete pages and Merge do not.
- Names written with lower-case `#xx` escapes: Split, Delete pages, Merge, Page numbers, Watermark, Compress, Bookmarks, Resize or crop pages and Redact's copied pages can lose an image or font named that way (as in phase 1, 11). Organise pages checks every page and refuses instead. A general fix is a candidate (section 3).
- The carried-content sentence counts comments with text, attachments and filled properties; it does not look for hidden or white text, form values, or XMP fields other than title, creator, description and keywords.
- Results were compared in pdf.js, and by reviewers in other open-source readers; not in Adobe Acrobat Reader, Microsoft Edge or Google Chrome.
- Every screen was driven in the real application by its build lane on scratch production builds; the repository's own application test is in section 9. Timings were taken on a machine at full load from parallel work (and it slept several times during the lanes), so they are upper bounds, not performance measures.
- The dark theme's low contrast in dialog text (section 12); the loading outline is very faint in the dark theme.

## 11b. Known limits (phase 3)

Collected from the build lanes', the integration lane's and the final lane's reports and checked against the code. Each is stated once.

### Reading a page (PDF to Excel and PDF to Word)

- Merged cells: a table drawn with lines keeps its merged cells, and a table read only from how its text lines up gets merged headings over its columns; other merges are not made.
- In a table without drawn lines, the second line of a narration is a row of its own, with its other cells empty; it is never joined to the row above, because the page does not say which row it belongs to. No amount moves. In a table with lines, the lines of one cell are joined.
- Three or more columns of running text, and spreads (two pages drawn as one), are read as one table whose rows are the lines across all the columns. Every word is there, roughly in its column, but the rows come out uneven and the page can grow; four of the 24 annual-report pages never seen while building and three public files gained a page for this reason. This is the largest class that still differs. Two columns are read as columns.
- Word boxes can be up to 4.5 pt too wide for the inner words of a line that is the only text in its font on the page (the reader cannot tell that font's widths); every other word was within 1 pt. Amounts are single words and their boxes were exact.
- Bold is read from the font's name, its descriptor and the tables of its file. A report that names every face alike (every face of one report was named light, weight 300, in its descriptors and in its font files, and only the outlines differed) loses its bold on its totals. Headings still go by size.
- Lines drawn inside a form inside a form are not read; dashed, white or zero-width lines count like any other line. When a page's lines cannot be read, its tables are found from the text alone and nothing is said. A page with content over 3,000,000 characters or more than 20,000 line pieces is read without its lines.
- Right-to-left text is joined right to left when most of a cell's or line's words are right to left; Hindi is left to right and passes through unchanged. Hindi stored in drawing order rather than reading order was not tested.
- A page with fewer than 20 characters is counted as having little or no text, and its words are still written: a scan's stamped page number lands in the result, and a page holding only a short label ("Annexure A") is counted too. The message's advice, "Make the file searchable to convert them", is followed by Make searchable for every such page with anything but text drawn on it; a page that holds only those few characters of text has nothing more to read, and Make searchable says so ("… or is blank; nothing to recognise.").
- The first build was tested on invented fixtures only. The quality rework was tested on three sets (the owner's own 3 files, 30 invented documents, 61 public PDFs; section 9); no client's document was converted, and none of the sets is kept in the repository.

### PDF to Excel

- Dr and Cr never change the sign: the value is positive and the format shows a space and "Dr" or "Cr" after it (always in that case and spacing). Whether a Dr balance is plus or minus is left to the user.
- Kept as text, so they cannot be added up: dates; leading zeros; more than 15 digits; `%`; a lone `-`; `1.234,56`; digits apart by a plain space (`12 345`); an en dash; `+500`; a currency mark after the number; two marks; `-500 Dr`; `.50` and `500.`; and a bare whole number of more than 8 digits (an account, mobile or customer number, but also a whole amount of ten crore or more written with no commas and no decimals).
- The currency mark is not kept in the cell's format. The Indian grouping is written for the cell's own number of digits, so a cell edited to a number of another size shows its commas oddly.
- A table that is not consistent with any earlier table on the sheet starts as a new table from its own columns, after a blank row; at most 8 earlier tables are remembered.
- A row holds at most 80 cells (the rest is joined, as text, into the 80th); a cell at most 32,767 characters (the rest is cut); one sheet at most 1,048,576 rows (refused, with "One sheet per page" offered); 1,000 pages a file.
- The workbook is written in a background worker; where no worker can start, it is written on the window's thread and holds the window for seconds on a long file. Stop while the worker writes was not tried in the application (the unit tests cover it).
- A 500-page statement takes about 33 s (PDF to Word about 26 s) on this two-core machine after the quality rework (the lane's figures of 20 November 2026; the first build took 29 s and 20 s). Most of that time is waiting for the page renderer's text. Excel also has no shared column grid for a sheet: a header block above a statement shares the statement's columns, so a page with both is wide. A superscript in a cell is plain text ("31st"). The barcode of one sample stood a little left of its place and over the start of the code printed under it. Loose slanted strokes are not drawn on a sheet. A faint picture behind text is not put in the sheet. A sheet row holds at most three lines of the page.
- The preview and a run started while it reads both read the file, which costs time.
- A workbook made from a signed PDF is not marked "Signature no longer valid" (5.1).
- **Pictures and text (T-068, 21 November 2026).** A picture never lies over the text of a cell. Where it would (the page shows it left of a text or partly over it, and the sheet writes that text at the left edge of the same column), the picture stands in empty rows of its own just above that text, in its size. The cost: a logo the page shows beside a name stands above the name in the workbook, not beside it; a picture among a table's rows stands above the whole table, because a table's rows stay together; and the sheet has those extra rows. A picture with free room beside its text stays beside its rows as before. Whether a picture would cover a text is reckoned from the page's own measures, not from the fonts Excel will use, so a text much wider in Excel than on the page can still reach a picture standing right of it. A picture more than four times taller than the rows it stands beside is not made smaller than a quarter of its size and can reach over the rows under it. A picture the page shows behind its text (three or more words over it, or drawn faint) is left out of a workbook, as before; PDF to Word keeps it behind the text.

### PDF to Word

- The look of the page is kept as far as the engine reads it (5.11); what it does not read is listed in the next group. Fonts Word does not have are replaced by similar ones; each run is scaled to its width on the page so that lines still end where they end, but the letters are not the page's own.
- A hyphen at a line end before a small letter is dropped, so a hyphenated compound broken there loses its hyphen ("co-" / "operative" gives "cooperative").
- A list of labels and amounts in two columns stays text with a tab between them; an aligned table becomes a Word table only with 3 column starts or more, each used by 3 rows or more.
- 1,000 pages a file.

### Conversion quality (PDF to Word and PDF to Excel; the rework of 19 and 20 November 2026)

Taken from the round 3 lane's "what still differs", in plain words. The scores that go with them are in section 9; they are a measure, not a promise.

- **Designed pages** (slides, text on pictures and coloured boxes, tables drawn with filled boxes instead of lines, flow charts) still come out weak: two public files scored 0.08 and 0.10 and still gain pages. Rounded and filled boxes behind text are missing, and a table drawn only with fills has no grid.
- **Page count.** Six of the 50 public files do not keep their page count; every invented document and all 43 annual-report pages used while building do. Of the 24 annual-report pages never seen while building, 20 do.
- **A page drawn turned** comes out as a wide page with upright text and a real table (a wide note that was 6 pages became 1, with all its words found). The words-in-place measure stays 0 for such a page by construction. A picture on it is turned in Word and left out of a workbook. **Decided by the owner on 20 November 2026.**
- **The rupee sign** is written where the one letter a page sets in a rupee-named font stands, because the files met hold neither glyph names nor a table that says "rupee"; the font's name is all they say. A glyph named for the rupee sign is followed as it is. The rule goes by a name, which sits uneasily with the principle of one engine driven only by what is on the page. **The owner was asked twice on 20 November 2026 and gave no ruling; the lead kept the rule as the lead's call, open to the owner's change.** The owner's other principle stands: where a page shows a black square, the result shows a black square and does not guess that a rupee sign was meant.
- **Bold lost** where a file names every face light (above, in "Reading a page").
- **Text at its own spot** (labels beside placed boxes, the foot of a page, turned labels) is a box of text anchored to the page, and rules and filled boxes are shapes at their exact places. They do not move with the text round them when the document is edited.
- **Ticks and crosses.** A drawn tick or cross is the sign only when it is two straight strokes of the usual shape. Any other slanted stroke is a line of the page in Word and absent from a workbook; marks drawn with curves are not read. Lines drawn as curves or as dashes of many pieces are not read.
- **Limits per page:** at most 300 shapes and 64 pictures; pictures of the kinds listed in 5.11 are left out.
- **One form** of the held-back set still has its row of PIN boxes one row low; on an invented form of the same kind the row is right, and the reason was not looked into.
- **Not done:** legacy-font Hindi, small capitals, rules as paragraph borders, reading serif or sans from the font file before its name, numbered paragraphs inside table cells (no failing page found), rich text in a cell.
- **Scans.** A scan has no text to read, so it must go through Make searchable first; the tool says so. A scan made searchable and then converted has its words typed from the recognition and its picture left out (5.11). The chain is run by the application test (P3-28 to P3-32 for Excel, P3-36 for Word and Excel) and by 8 invented scans (section 9, "Scans through the real application"); a real scan was not run through the application. The limits, in plain words:
  - On a scan, whatever recognition did not read is absent from the Word page: white-on-dark header bands, logos, stamps, ruling lines and shading. The scan picture is left out, so nothing else shows them. This is the lead's ruling of 20 November 2026, open to the owner's change. Keeping the non-text parts of a scan picture is a follow-up.
  - Only invisible text counts as a scan layer. A layer hidden another way still prints double.
  - A tilted scan of a table gets no table in Excel: its amounts come as text in one column, and the application says so ("… had no table and was written as text"). Straightening scans before the tables are read is a follow-up.
  - Wrong recognised numbers can still reach number cells. PDF to Excel cannot tell a wrong number from a right one; the message asks the user to check numbers against the page (on the invented bank statement 6 number cells held numbers that are not in the document).
  - A mixed file (some pages with text, some scans) can show both the "little or no text" note and the scan sentence.
  - On a very faint scan the Word page is nearly empty (the faint ledger: 13 of 353 real words).
  - A searchable scan converted to Word loses bold print (headings, totals): the text is typed again from recognition, which does not say which words are bold.
- **Opened in Word and Excel by the lanes only.** Results were printed through Word and Excel on this computer and compared with their source pages. They were not opened in Adobe Acrobat Reader, and the owner's hand check of the reworked output has not been done.

### Document check

- It never says a signature is valid and reads no certificate; the signer, date, reason and place are what the file states. Whether more was written after a signature is not judged for a file opened with its password.
- "Pages that look scanned" follows Make searchable's rule: a page with fewer than 20 characters and anything but text drawn on it. So a page with little text and a small logo, a stamp or a ruled line "looks scanned" too, although it is not a scan; Make searchable would recognise that page as well.
- "Attachments: Yes" also counts file-attachment comments.
- The report uses the standard fonts: a value with Hindi, ₹ or another character outside them prints as `[word in another script]` (it is shown as it is on screen). Shown values are cut at 300 characters.
- On each page with fewer than 20 characters the page's content is read once to see whether anything is drawn; on a page with a very large drawing that step could hold the window longer than 0.3 s. Not measured, and no timing was taken on a large or heavy file. The fact list is long on a small screen (it scrolls; the main button stays in view).

### Compare

- Text only (owner decision, 17 November 2026): a change in layout, fonts, pictures, signatures, stamps or colours is not seen.
- A moved page is shown as removed in one place and added in another.
- Pages without text are not compared ("3 pages have no text to compare; they may be scanned."); a file with no text on any page is refused.
- In the report ₹ is printed as "Rs.", and Hindi and other scripts as `[word in another script]` (counted in the message).
- The counts of words added, removed and changed cover pages found in both versions only. The report details the first 200 differing pages and counts the rest, lists at most 200 changes a page, and shortens a passage of more than 40 words to its first 25 and last 10.
- A page with more than 5,000 different words a side, or one that uses up the exact diff's budget, is compared in larger pieces, and a differing passage of more than 2,000 words is shown as removed and added whole; the message says so.
- A hyphen at a line end is no difference in either form; a hyphen inside a line, or before a capital at a line end, is compared as written.
- 1,000 pages a file; 1,000 pages against 1,000 different pages used about 102 MB more memory in the lane's measurement.

### Make searchable

- English only (owner decision, 17 November 2026): Hindi and other scripts are not recognised, and characters outside the standard font (₹, ligatures, Devanagari) are left out of the searchable text and counted.
- Recognised text can be wrong, and the engine's own confidence does not reliably say where: on a faint, noisy photocopy of a ruled ledger the trial read 22 of 70 amounts; in all, 9 of 55 wrong amounts had a confidence of 80 or more. "Numbers to check" caught 46 of the 55 and also marked 20 of 383 right ones. The faint photocopy pages were not run again in the application.
- A page with under 10 words on A4, such as a cover with a title only, is called faint. A blank back of a sheet is recognised and reported as "No words were found on page N; it may be blank or too faint."
- A page with 20 characters of text or more is not recognised, so a scan that already carries a printed header, footer or page number of that length is copied as it is. "Recognise every page" is not offered.
- Words made only of `|` or `_` are left out (ruled column lines read as `|`); a single em dash is kept.
- Each page has 60 s and is then left as it was; a page the engine fails on, or that cannot be drawn, is left as it was too, and named. After three failures in a row the remaining pages are not tried and are left as they were. In the record a failed page is counted with the skipped pages (`pagesSkipped`), not on its own.
- The dialog promises about 5 to 20 seconds a page; on this two-core machine a page took from under a second (a blank page) to 19.1 s (a scanned-looking ledger), and a 30-page scan 5.7 minutes (mean 10.2 s a page).
- Memory peaked at 688 MB (working set of the window's process) on the 30-page scan; the longest file run in the application had 30 pages.
- After Stop the engine's thread goes on for about 2 s before it ends; nothing is added.
- Straightening was tested on one direction of skew (2° clockwise) only. Not measured: real scanner or camera output, pages turned 90°, two-column pages, handwriting, stamps over text, a processor without the faster instruction sets, and the system's temporary folder before and after a run.
- Not driven by the repository's application test: the viewer's Find on a searchable result and a failed engine start (both were driven in the engine lane's copy). The dialog has no unit test.
- The engine's files are in the installer twice (8 files, 14.8 MB each copy), as the packaging setting stands; the installer is 181.8 MB against phase 2's 161.1 MB (section 3, candidates).
- Print in another script (Hindi, for example) is left out, not read: the engine reads English only (owner decision, 17 November 2026), and half of a real two-language scan stays unsearchable. Reading it would need a second language data file and a font that can write Devanagari in the text layer (the standard font cannot): a package and asset decision, not made.
- English words printed close under a line of another script can be lost or misread (on the two checked pages of the real scan 15 of 318 and 7 of 98 real words were still missing).
- Rotated stamps, signatures and handwriting are not read.
- The "little could be read" measure (5.12) also names a sparse page with a stamp and a page half in another language, because both are partly read with few words kept; it cannot tell them from a faint page.
- The rule leaves out some right words with the wrong ones, and a few short nonsense marks with digits can stay as "numbers to check" (figures in section 9, "The small losses").
- Faint photocopies are still read badly (about a third of the words). The cause is found and measured: on 12 faint pictures the automatic layout finds 33% of the real words and 96 of 682 numbers, the same pages read as one block 80.5% and 415 numbers; one block is worse on clean ruled tables, so it cannot replace the automatic layout. A second try as one block, keeping the reading with clearly more words, was measured but not built, because it can double the time on pages that come out partly read (every page of a two-language file, with no gain there): an open follow-up.
- The owner's file was proven through a harness with the same engine and layer code, not through the application's window; the application itself was proven on invented scans only. Not opened in Adobe Acrobat Reader. The message's new sentence about parts of pages is the lane's wording.

### All phase 3 tools

- Reports and conversions use the standard PDF fonts or Calibri; no font is embedded, so Hindi and ₹ cannot be printed in the Document check and Compare reports.
- An Excel or Word result cannot be previewed in VERA and cannot be used by another tool.
- The workbooks and Word documents of the first build were read back with exceljs and by unzipping; the quality rework then opened results in Word and Excel on this computer (see "Conversion quality" above). The reports and searchable results were read with pdf.js, not in Adobe Acrobat Reader, Microsoft Edge or Google Chrome.
- The dialogs have no component tests; their logic is tested in the form modules, and they were checked by eye in the real application.
- Built and tested on Windows only (Windows 11 on a two-core machine); a Mac has not been tried. The installer was built, not installed.
- Timings were taken on a two-core machine, sometimes shared with other lanes' work, so they are upper bounds.

## 12. Outside this feature, for the owner

These are problems in the application that were found while building PDF Tools. They are not caused by it and **none has been changed**. They are listed for the owner to decide.

- `src/index.css` imports the Inter font from Google Fonts. Displaying a character outside the font subset already loaded, for example the rupee sign, can cause a font request to `fonts.gstatic.com`. No file content is sent, but this conflicts with the "no network" aim of section 7. A fix is to host the font inside the application, which edits a shared file.
- The main process does not enforce section and box locks on working-paper attachments, so Audit Execution lets users attach to locked items. PDF Tools is stricter: it does not offer locked sections or boxes.
- Deleting an Evidence Vault record leaves Audit Execution attachments pointing at it.
- `uploadFile` in the Evidence Vault hook inserts the database row before it writes the file, so quitting in between leaves a record with no file, and it shows the raw error text of the main process to the user.
- A stray "0" appears next to each file on the Evidence Vault screen and in each section header of Audit Execution (a flag stored as 0 is printed).
- The Audit Trail Details column cuts long entries at the right edge (about 45 characters at 1280 px width), so the sources, destination and SHA-256 of a PDF Tools entry need horizontal scrolling. The full text is in the record.
- The application has no `will-download` handler, so a download opens Electron's own Save As window.
- Deep links of the form `?tab=` on VERA Tools bounced to another tab in some cases (on `a15b4c3` VERA Tools follows `?tab=` only when it changes).
- The header update button overlaps the engagement switcher at 1280 px width.
- The packaged application checks its update feed at start-up.
- `AuthContext.tsx` writes the signed-in user's id to the console at sign-in.
- An automated security check flagged a possible stored cross-site-scripting problem in `public/caat/caat-tools.html` (CAAT Tools, T-024): values from a saved workspace or shared mapping profile are put into the page without escaping. This was not verified by us and is not part of PDF Tools.

Found while building phase 2 (T-052); none has been changed:

- **Audit Trail times are shown 5½ hours behind IST** ("14 Nov 2026 19:01" for an action at 00:31 IST on 15 November), and "Today's activity" read 0. The owner decided on 15 November 2026 that nothing changes there; it is listed here only.
- The Google Fonts request is wider than said above: the application fetches its fonts (Inter, JetBrains Mono) from `fonts.googleapis.com` and `fonts.gstatic.com` at every start-up, before PDF Tools opens, and any character of another subset shown anywhere (other currency signs, accented, Cyrillic or Greek letters) triggers a further request. Hindi and emoji did not.
- PostHog analytics (`posthog-js`) is in the bundle and is initialised when the Feedback page is opened, with automatic capture off but session recording not explicitly disabled. If session replay were ever enabled for the project, the rest of the session (PDF Tools file names and thumbnails included) could be recorded. Setting `disable_session_recording: true` would rule it out.
- Electron's own menu keys act behind every dialog: Ctrl+R reloads the window and Ctrl+W closes it, even with a PDF Tools dialog or the viewer open (unsaved results are then lost).
- Closing VERA with results not saved gives no warning. A question needs about 9 lines in `electron/main.cjs` plus a listener in PDF Tools, and must be tested with the automatic update's restart, which a cancelled close would hold up.
- Ctrl+Y ("My Tasks") goes to `/my-dashboard`, which has no route: the 404 page logs an error. Ctrl+B is both "Financial Statement Review" and the sidebar's fold.
- The application's dark theme leaves titles, labels, file names and Close low in contrast in the tool dialogs (phase 1 dialogs too); `--destructive` and `--warning` have no dark values, and the newer `--*-soft` badge tokens have no dark values either, so PDF Tools did not adopt them.
- The GST validator `isValidGstin` refuses state code 38 (Ladakh) and codes 96 and 99 (5.9); whether that is right is legal content for the owner to check.
- From the UX audit, application-wide: the header's build label, search box, update button and engagement switcher overlap at 1280 px and even at 1920 px; the sidebar brand reads "AuditPro / Audit Management" and its title is hard to see in the light theme; the header search box looks usable but is not wired up; two toast systems are mounted; the shared status badge's amber text measures 2.4:1; working-paper wording differs between screens ("Workpaper Ref", "WP Ref", "working paper"); the VERA Tools page header and tabs take about 175 px of height.
- With DevTools attached and its Page domain on, Chromium makes `window.confirm` return true at once, so any test driven that way can never answer Cancel to a question; it must stand the question in (section 9).

## 13. Inputs from the owner's earlier tools (14 November 2026)

The owner offered two desktop tools of his own as input for the whole of PDF Tools, every phase ("a suggestion, not a compulsion"): "PDF Redact Pro" and "PDF Manager Pro", both written in Python with PyMuPDF and PyQt5. Their code was read; their sample documents were not opened. The owner deletes both folders after this study, so this section is the record. Only behaviour, rules and numbers transfer, not code: PyMuPDF and Ghostscript are AGPL-licensed (and PyMuPDF is Python), so neither can be bundled into this application.

### How the earlier tools map to PDF Tools

| Earlier tool feature | PDF Tools |
| --- | --- |
| Merge, split by ranges, delete pages, page numbers, compress (3 levels), images to and from PDF, open protected files | Built in phase 1 |
| Compression target chosen from the scan's own resolution | Phase 1 already decides per image from its drawn size; compare the level tables below when tuning |
| Extra page-number styles (roman, alphabet, circles, "Pg-1", "1.") | Phase 2, 5.17 |
| Redaction categories, profiles, redact everywhere, approve or reject list, find and redact, replacement token | Phase 2, 5.9 (built and switched on, see 5.9) |
| Watermark; rotate and reorder pages; header, footer, stamp, logo; password-protect output | Phase 2 (owner decision, 14 November 2026): 5.15–5.18 |
| Page box: crop, or add margins | Built in phase 2: Resize or crop pages (5.19) |
| Several steps in one run (his single pipeline); batch over a folder | Not scheduled; candidates, with the review rule for Redact to be settled (section 3) |
| PDF to Excel (ruling-line tables, else row and column clustering), PDF to Word (layout, text, image fallbacks) | Built in phase 3 (5.11): tables from drawn lines, else from how the text lines up, else the page's text in column A; PDF to Word as text, headings and tables, without pictures and without a layout-preserving converter |
| OCR (automatic when a page has fewer than 20 characters of text, or forced; language; resolution) feeding search, Redact and Convert | Built in phase 3 (5.12): automatic only (fewer than 20 characters and anything but text drawn on the page), English only, 300 dpi |
| Sidecar audit file, log file, file paths | Replaced by the Audit Trail entry; nothing outside the application is written, and no matched value is kept |
| Delete sources after conversion | Not adopted: a source is never altered or removed |

What phase 2 took from them, and where it chose differently, is said in each section: Redact's categories, profiles, every-occurrence search, joining of boxes, the 95% safety rule, the review list, typed terms with a reason, the `[REDACTED]` token, metadata and attachment removal (always on in PDF Tools, optional in his tool), and counts by reason in the record (5.9; padding 1.5 pt instead of 1 pt; mobile, IP address, name and address rules narrowed after measuring false matches; the text under a box is turned into a picture instead of being removed from the page, because his method needs PyMuPDF); the watermark, stamp and logo numbers (5.16); the header and footer tokens and styles (5.17; margin kept at phase 1's 28 pt; circles drawn lighter); AES-256 with one password (5.18; all permissions granted explicitly).

### His pipeline

PDF Manager Pro applies every tool the user switches on to every output page in one run, in a fixed order: watermark behind, page content, watermark in front, page number, header, footer, stamp and logo, page rotation; then compression; then the password; then conversion. Merge, split and batch are only different ways of choosing which pages go through. PDF Tools runs one tool at a time and keeps the chain of steps in its record; a saved preset of several steps is a candidate (section 3). Watermark (5.16) keeps his drawing order.

### Numbers kept for later work

- Compression levels Low, Medium and High: target resolution from the input scan's own resolution (input 300 dpi or more: 200, 150, 100 dpi; 200–299: 150, 100, 72; 150–199: 150, 100, 72; below 150: 100, 100, 72); JPEG quality 85, 60–65 and 30–45; images under 80 px skipped; fonts subset; High also removes XML metadata. Ghostscript was used when installed, PyMuPDF otherwise.
- Page ranges `1-3,5,7-9`; empty means all; out-of-range values clamped and bad parts ignored silently (PDF Tools reports them instead).
- Page numbers: 9 positions, 20 pt padding (10 pt at the bottom).
- Convert to images: PNG at 144 dpi, `page_0001.png`.

### Phase 3 design inputs

**PDF to Excel** (one worksheet per page, named `Page_n`):

1. Tables found from the page's ruling lines (his settings: lines strategy, intersection tolerance 8, snap tolerance 4, join tolerance 4), each table written as rows.
2. If a page has no ruled table: words grouped into rows when their tops are within 4 pt; column starts found by clustering the words' start positions at 18 pt, the cluster width widened by 6 pt at a time until there are at most 80 columns; each word goes to the nearest column start; words that land in the same cell are joined with a space; empty cells at the end of a row are trimmed.
3. If that gives nothing: the page's text in cell A1.
4. Columns sized to their content.
Phase 3 (5.11) adds what his tool did not: numbers written as numbers, Indian digit grouping understood, and a preview before saving. As built, it chose differently in three places: "All pages on one sheet" is the default, with `Page_n` sheets as the other choice (a 30-page statement as 30 sheets is hard to use); columns are found from the clear strips between cells rather than by clustering where words start (a column of right-aligned amounts starts in a different place on every row), with his 4 pt row tolerance kept, and his 18 pt cluster widened 6 pt at a time used only to bring more than 80 columns down to 80; and a page whose table matches an earlier one carries on in its columns (5.11).

**PDF to Word**: a layout-preserving converter first; if the page needs recognition, paragraphs of recognised text with a page break per page ("[No text detected]" for an empty page); as a last resort, each page as an image. No layout-preserving converter is available in this application's stack; phase 3 must choose one or design without it. As built, phase 3 first designed without one: an editable text version. The owner rejected that on 19 November 2026 after comparing a real page with its output, and the converter was rebuilt to keep the look of the pages with its own engine (5.11); there is still no picture fallback, so a page with little or no text keeps its few words and is counted, and the message points to Make searchable.

**OCR**: a page counts as scanned when it has fewer than 20 characters of text ("auto"), or every page is recognised ("force"); a language setting (default English) and a resolution from 72 to 300 dpi; recognised words grouped by block, paragraph and line into rows for Excel (his engine's page segmentation mode 6). In his redaction tool, pages were rendered at 3×, words above a confidence threshold matched whole against the same patterns, and boxed with 3 pt horizontal and 2 pt vertical padding. Phase 2's Redact takes words with their positions, so recognised words can feed it the same way. As built in phase 3 (5.12): automatic only, with one rule shared with Document check (fewer than 20 characters of text and anything but text drawn on the page); English only; pages drawn at 300 dpi; the engine's automatic page layout rather than his mode 6 (in the trial the single-block default read 0 of 70 amounts on a ruled ledger), and words placed in the invisible text layer by line, not grouped into rows.

## 14. History

- **Revision 1 and 2** (13 November 2026): the first design and the lead's review of it (the request source above).
- **Revision 3** (14 November 2026): phase 1 as built, committed and pushed on `feat/pdf-tools` (first from `main` at `26d3b9f37a9f3aadd8b3d5bf456538e195a60a67`, then on `8ee2107`). At revision 3 no pull request was open; pull request #111 was opened and merged to `main` the same day, merge commit `fb607b5`. It changed only what the build made untrue or left out: the compression values, the page-number preview, how the screen is mounted, how dependencies were added, where the destinations are offered, what each tool carries over, how cancelling works, how results are recorded, and the limits found while building. Phase 1 was checked by 43 unit test files with 892 tests, by the application test (32 of 32 checks) and by the same test against an unpacked Windows package built in a scratch copy (31 checks with 1 skipped); the owner's hand check then found the folder-save defect fixed by the listener in `electron/main.cjs` (section 6). The records `CURRENT_STATUS.md` and `SESSION_HANDOFF.md` hold the full phase 1 results.
- **Revision 4** (14 November 2026): phase 2 designed to build-ready detail from throw-away trials in Node, before any of it was built: Redact, Bookmarks, the page viewer, and the four tools the owner added that day.
- **Revision 5** (14 November 2026): phase 2 as first built, on `main` at `36130e6`, with Redact switched off after independent test 1.

- **Revision 8** (16 November 2026): Redact switched on (`BUILT = true`) after test 5 and fix waves 5 and 6, on the owner's instruction to close Redact first and then test everything together; 5.9 as built after those waves, with the on-screen sentences they added; new known limits (11a); the application test at 143 checks; `main` at `be297bd`.
- **Revision 9** (18 November 2026): phase 2 merged to `main` in pull request #118 (`8dc2165`, 17 November 2026); phase 3 (T-064) as built on `feat/pdf-tools-phase-3`: PDF to Excel and PDF to Word (5.11), Make searchable (5.12), Document check and Compare (5.13), the Excel and Word rows (section 4), their destinations and records (section 6), how they are built (section 8), how they were tested, with the final pass's figures (section 9), and their known limits (11b).
- **Revision 10** (20 November 2026): the conversion-quality rework of PDF to Word and PDF to Excel (T-064, D-013). The owner rejected the first Word output on 19 November 2026; the converter now keeps the look of the pages with one shared engine (5.11, "How the look of the page is kept"), after three rounds tested on three sets (section 9, "Conversion quality"). The owner's two converter principles are recorded in D-013; a page drawn turned was decided by him on 20 November 2026 (a wide upright page with a real table), and the rupee sign read by a font's name is the lead's call after two questions to him without a ruling. The list of built tools is known at once, so opening PDF Tools no longer loads every tool's code (section 8). Known limits: 11b, "Conversion quality". Figures: the lane's of 20 November 2026 at the time; the final figures are in the second addendum below.
- **Revision 10, addendum** (20 November 2026, later the same day; T-064, D-013, GAP-23): the owner decided that a page drawn turned comes out as a wide upright page with a real table; he was asked twice about the rupee sign read by a font's name and gave no ruling, so it is the lead's call, open to his change. Make searchable was reworked after the owner's trial of a real two-language scan (5.12, section 9, 11b): words the engine itself doubts are left out, the text size is measured from the row, a space follows each word of a line, the message names parts of pages that could not be read, and the record holds eleven counts. Figures at that time: 3,915 unit tests passed (190 test files in the source inventory), type check 49 with none under PDF Tools, application test 180 of 180, the Make searchable lane's; they were replaced the same day (second addendum).
- **Revision 10, second addendum** (20 November 2026, later the same day; T-064, D-013, GAP-23): the independent final pass on the reworked tree (190 test files, 3,915 tests, application test 180 of 180) ran 8 invented scans through the real application and found that PDF to Word printed a searchable scan twice, the picture behind and the recognised text on top. Fixed the same day: where a page's words are invisible text over a picture, the words are typed and that picture is left out; both converters say "N pages are scans: …" and the record holds `pagesFromScan`; Make searchable says "Little could be read on page …" for a page of which little was read; the engine's notes no longer go to the console; attribute values in the Word file are escaped and colours must be six hex digits (5.11, 5.12, section 6, section 8, section 9, 11b). That the scan picture is left out is the lead's ruling, open to the owner's change. Final figures: unit suite 191 test files, 3,938 tests, all passed, none skipped; type check 49, none under PDF Tools; ESLint clean on 106 changed files; application test 181 of 181 (the second run: the first stopped at the retry step of scenario INT-4 on a busy machine, and the re-run passed); 500-page statement: PDF to Excel 33.5 s, PDF to Word 26.1 s; the lead re-ran the unit suite himself afterwards. Replaces the "to be confirmed" wording elsewhere in these records.
- **Revision 10, third addendum** (21 November 2026; T-068, GAP-23): phase 3 is on `main` (pull request #121, merge commit `05e35db`, 21 November 2026; committed as `67d11eb` on 20 November 2026 on the owner's word), and the lines of these records that still said "uncommitted" are made true; no UAT is recorded. A defect found after the merge is fixed: PDF to Excel could put a picture over the text of a cell (an invented trial balance with a logo left of the company's name: the logo covered the start of the name in cell A1). The cause: the sheet writes a text at the left edge of its column wherever it starts on the page, while a picture keeps its distance from that edge. The rule now (5.11, 11b): where a picture would lie over a text on the sheet, it stands in empty rows of its own just above that text, in its size; every other picture stays beside its rows. Tests: `excelPictures.test.ts` (10 tests; 6 failed on the old code); no existing expectation changed. Checks of 21 November 2026: unit suite 192 files, 3,948 tests, 3,948 passed, 0 failed (the lead's own run of 21 November 2026, after the line-ending fix; the lane's earlier runs showed three failures that are history: two `ocrAssets.test.ts` tests over the recognition files' line endings, now fixed, and one 5-second time-out of a `compareReport.test.ts` test on a busy machine); type check 49, none under PDF Tools; application test 181 of 181 on the first run. The line-ending fault of the recognition files is fixed: with `core.autocrlf=true` Git had written them with CRLF, so they no longer matched the package originals (two `ocrAssets.test.ts` tests failed, on every Windows clone since the merge of phase 3); a new `.gitattributes` at the repository root (a shared file) holds one rule, `public/pdf-tools/ocr/** -text`, which keeps their bytes as committed. 11b also says now that a searchable scan converted to Word loses bold print. The team's user guide was updated for phase 3 on 20 November 2026; it is kept outside the repository.
- **Phase 3, how it was built.** 17 November 2026: the owner's decisions (all four tools in one phase, Make searchable last; Review is Document check and Compare; Compare looks at text only; Make searchable reads English only, and the package files may change for its engine). The lead then wrote a build contract against this document and the code: the open decisions settled, each lane's files and the interfaces between them frozen (the shared layout reader's types first, so that the Excel and Word lanes could build against it), the number rules, the limits, the risks and the tests. A short first step put the new tool ids, stubs and toolbar entries in place, so that every dialog compiled. Five lanes then built in parallel on disjoint files: the shared page-layout reader, Document check, Compare, PDF to Excel and PDF to Word, each with unit tests, none running the application (with several lanes in one tree, each judged the type check and ESLint by its own files). The lead ruled on each hand-back, and three lanes ran a second round on those rulings: little-text pages keep their words, the Compare count and the line-end hyphen, and the table references and the number rules for PDF to Excel (whose crowded-column fault its own test had found). An integration lane then wired the tools in, added the Excel and Word rows, wrote scenarios P3-1 to P3-26, and first ran the tools in the real application; it found the defects listed in section 9, among them the application build failing on the Excel write worker (the engine trial, building the tree for its own proofs, had found the same failure) and the toolbar's fourth row. Beside it, a trial of the recognition engine in the real application recommended "go, with conditions"; on 18 November 2026 the owner said go ahead on the terms in 5.12. At about 00:50 on 18 November 2026 the account's usage limit stopped both the integration lane and the engine trial before either had written its report; both were resumed with their work kept and told to write their reports early. Make searchable was then built in two lanes: part A (the page plan, text layer, quality rule and words, with no engine and no package change) and part B (the engine, its packages added by hand, the drawing, run and dialog). At about 04:30 the usage limit stopped the integration lane and part B again; both were resumed at 08:45, and from then on no more than two heavy lanes ran at once. About 10:15 `main` moved to `1df122c` (pull requests #119 and #120); the branch was fast-forwarded with the working tree kept (three records merged keeping both sides, ours first; no PDF Tools, package or label file touched by `main`). One lane then wired Make searchable in, applied the lead's last rulings (a failing page does not end the run; "about 5 to 20 seconds a page"; Document check counts scanned pages by Make searchable's rule), traced UX-4 to the test's own click timing, and ran one combined final pass on a clean copy of the tree at `1df122c`, which passed. Last, the task was renumbered from T-053 to T-064 in our lines of code and records only. The owner told the lead on 18 November 2026 to "do what needs to be done" while he was away; the commit still needed his word (given on 20 November 2026: commit `67d11eb`; merged in pull request #121, merge commit `05e35db`, 21 November 2026). On 19 November 2026 his hand check rejected the first PDF to Word output; three rounds of conversion-quality work followed (one lane each, on a general engine, tested on three sets of documents), and on 20 November 2026 the lead changed how the tab loads its tools so that it opens at once.
- **Task number.** Phase 3 was built under the number T-053 (pull request #117 took T-048 to T-051, and phase 2 became T-052) and renumbered T-064 on 18 November 2026, because pull requests #119 (T-053 to T-063) and #120 (T-053) use T-053; the workspace notes keep T-053. Phase 2 was planned as T-032; `main` used T-032 and T-033 while it ran, then T-034 to T-044 for Financial Statement Review, Ledger Analysis and other work, and on 16 November 2026 T-045 to T-047 (pull request #116), so phase 2, which was T-045 until then, is T-052. Older workspace notes call it T-032, T-033, T-034 or T-045.
- **`main` moved under the branch** (each time the branch was fast-forwarded with the working tree kept): `fb607b5` (PR #111, PDF Tools phase 1), `6e5da5e` (PR #112, GSTIN lookup and bulk validation), `b835def` (start-up label), `36130e6` (PR #113, Permanent File), `a15b4c3` (PR #114, Ledger Analysis, which replaces the Tally Tools tab; PR #115, Financial Statement Review), `be297bd` (PR #116, Financial Statement Review menu names, layout and Excel theme; no code overlap). Phase 2 then reached `main` through `f79f212` (PR #117, Financial Statement Review T-048 to T-051, merged into the phase 2 branch first) and `8dc2165` (PR #118, phase 2); phase 3 started from `8dc2165`, and on 18 November 2026 `main` moved to `1df122c` (PR #119, Financial Statement Review T-053 to T-063; PR #120, CAAT Tools). Only the project records, `inventory.json` and the start-up label files overlapped; `main`'s text was taken and the phase 2 entries added to it.
- **Redact.** Test 1 (14 November) found B1 to B8; the owner then directed that Redact ships in phase 2, switched on, with a wider identifier set, and that phase 2 is committed only after Redact passes. Fix wave 1 fixed B1 to B8 and added 37 kinds; test 2 (15 November) found R1 to R8; fix wave 2 changed the design to the guarantee at the level of each drawing (5.9); test 3 (15 November) kept Redact off with eight blocking findings, fixed in fix wave 3; test 4 (15–16 November) kept it off with 14 blocking findings; fix wave 4 (default deny) followed; test 5 (16 November, the fourth re-test) kept it off with 4 blocking items, fixed in fix waves 5 and 6. On 16 November the owner first kept the rule that phase 2 is committed only after Redact passes, and later that day directed that Redact is closed first and everything is then tested together; Redact was switched on that day and checked with the whole module in one final pass, with no separate round after the last fixes.
